The parent-to-constituent transport $\Omega_{iI} = \exp(\phi_i^{(\ell)})\exp(-\phi_I^{(\ell+1)})$, which carries the parent's laws into agent $i$'s frame for the top-down priors, and its inverse $\Omega_{Ii}$, which carries constituent beliefs into the parent frame for the bottom-up aggregation that builds $\bar{\mu}_I$ and $\bar{\Sigma}_I$, are special cases of the general law $\Omega_{ab} = \exp(\phi_a)\exp(-\phi_b)$ with $a$ and $b$ at different levels. Any parent frame $U_I \in G$ makes $\Omega_{iI}$ a well-defined group element, so intra-level and cross-level re-expressions act through the same group. By Proposition~\ref{prop:regime1_removability} the parent frame cancels from every constituent prior in Regime~I, so its choice is a gauge convention; the construction of a parent frame from constituent frames, and its noncompact $\mathrm{GL}^+(K_q)$ caveats, are in Sections~\ref{sec:meta_agent_formation} and~\ref{sec:meta_agent_variational}.

\subsection{Curvature Structure: Four Notions}

Four distinct notions of curvature appear, and only the first is active in Part~I. \emph{Fiber curvature}: the fiber $\mathcal{B}$ is a Riemannian manifold, and the manifold of Gaussian distributions under the Fisher-Rao metric has sectional curvature of mixed sign for $K_q \geq 2$~\cite{Skovgaard1984}. The SPD covariance sector $\mathbb{S}^+_{K_q}$ is a Riemannian symmetric space of nonpositive curvature, and the univariate case and the location-scale family with fixed correlation reduce to hyperbolic geometry of constant negative curvature, but the full joint mean-covariance manifold admits 2-planes of strictly positive curvature: for orthonormal pure-mean directions the sectional curvature equals $+1/4$, a value that is not the supremum, since mixed mean-covariance planes reach slightly larger values. This fiber curvature governs natural-gradient flow within each fiber. \emph{Group curvature}: the Riemannian curvature of the structure group depends on the metric chosen on it. $\mathrm{SO}(3) \cong \mathbb{RP}^3$ with its bi-invariant metric has constant positive curvature, whereas $\mathrm{GL}^+(K_q)$ for $K_q \geq 2$ carries no bi-invariant Riemannian metric, so any curvature assigned to it depends on a non-canonical choice of left- or right-invariant metric~\cite{Milnor1976}. Noncommutativity is a separate, metric-free property: the order of unrelated transports matters ($\Omega_{ab}\Omega_{cd} \neq \Omega_{cd}\Omega_{ab}$ for disjoint pairs), while for chained transports through a common intermediate agent the cocycle $\Omega_{ij}\Omega_{jk} = \Omega_{ik}$ holds identically whatever the commutators. \emph{Gauge-field curvature}: the field strength $F^{(i)}_{\mu\nu}$ of Eq.~\eqref{eq:field_strength} vanishes identically in Regime~I (Section~\ref{sec:connection_forms}); a nonzero field strength requires an independent base connection, which is deferred, and the graph holonomy of independent links is a different object. \emph{Base curvature}: $\mathcal{C}$ carries no physical metric, the fiducial chart metric of the working framework is flat, and metrics that represent agent-relative geometry are induced by beliefs through pullback (Section~\ref{sec:pullback}). Reading induced curvature as gravity is a structural placeholder of Part~II (Section~\ref{sec:speculative_physics}).

\subsection{Working Framework: Simplifications and Scope}
\label{sec:working_framework}

One structural fact and four simplifications keep every quantity computable in closed form. The structural fact: beliefs and priors share the latent-state manifold $\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{state}}$, and generative models and hyper-priors share the model-parameter manifold $\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{model}}$, both \emph{by construction} from the cross-level prior relation~\eqref{eq:cross_scale_shadow} and not as separate assumptions (Section~\ref{sec:cross_scale_shadows}). The four simplifications follow. Both fibers are Gaussian. The structure group is $G = \mathrm{GL}^+(K_q, \mathbb{R})$ acting on the belief fiber, with the compact subgroups $\mathrm{SO}(N) \subset \mathrm{GL}(N)$, including $\mathrm{SO}(3)$, as special cases for analytic clarity; a complexified group appears only in the worked signature example of Section~\ref{sec:signature_resolution}. The base manifold is flat, $\mathcal{C} = \mathbb{R}^2$ with its Euclidean chart. Gauge frames are smooth, and the field strength vanishes identically in Regime~I however fast the frames vary (Section~\ref{sec:connection_forms}).

These choices give closed-form relative entropies, known curvature of the Gaussian fibers, and transparent group actions. They keep gauge covariance with nontrivial transport operators, information-geometric dynamics through Fisher metrics, multilevel hierarchical structure, curved statistical manifolds, and non-commutative group structure; they give up graph and base holonomy, non-Gaussian belief structures, heterogeneous agent types, curved base geometry, and gauge-field curvature $F_{\mu\nu}$ (Regime~I flatness).

\paragraph{Matched bundles on the two-manifold structure.}
Beliefs and priors share $\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{state}} =: \mathcal{B}$, the representation $\rho_{\mathrm{state}} =: \rho$, and one associated bundle $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{state}} =: \mathcal{E}$, and models and hyper-priors share $\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{model}}$ and $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{model}}$. Setting the slow subsystem aside, each agent $i$ is represented by a single section $\sigma^i: \mathcal{U}_i \to \mathcal{E}$ carrying $\sigma^i(c) = (q_i(c), p_i(c))$. This is reasonable because beliefs and priors are both probability distributions over the same underlying space, distinguished by separate components and not by separate bundle structure; what is given up is the ability to model qualitatively different belief and prior types, for example Gaussian beliefs with categorical priors. Equivalently, the canonical functional~\eqref{eq:free_energy_functional_final} contains no cross-bundle morphism coupling the model and belief channels; the two channels couple only through the frame sector and the within-channel cross-level priors of Eq.~\eqref{eq:cross_scale_shadow}. In the shared-frame configuration $\Omega_{ij}$ and $\tilde{\Omega}_{ij}$ act as the same element of $G$ in two representations; in the independent-frame configuration the model fiber carries its own $\mathrm{GL}(K_m)$ bundle and frame field $\tilde{\phi}_i$ (the option following Eq.~\eqref{eq:envelope_gradient_model}), $\tilde{\Omega}_{ij}$ becomes an independent transport, and even the structure-group identification is absent, leaving the cross-level priors as the only inter-channel coupling.

\paragraph{Gaussian probability manifold.}
The fiber is $\mathcal{B} = \{\mathcal{N}(\mu, \Sigma) : \mu \in \mathbb{R}^{K_q}, \Sigma \in \mathbb{S}^+_{K_q}\}$, with relative entropy in the closed form of Eq.~\eqref{eq:gaussian_kl}. Gaussians are the maximum-entropy distributions for given mean and covariance. They are a simplifying choice (Section~\ref{sec:statistical_manifolds}), and a non-Gaussian exponential-family fiber requires a common bijective sample-space action that preserves the family and leaves the chosen divergence computable. The Gaussian theorem below supplies this closure for linear pushforwards of Gaussian states; mixture models, heavy-tailed distributions, and density-operator fibers require separate closure and computation proofs. A complex or quantum fiber with complex-valued amplitudes is a separate program (Section~\ref{sec:open_problems}).

\paragraph{Gauge group: $\mathrm{GL}(K_q)$ and its compact subgroups.}
\label{sec:gauge_group_choice}
The full Gaussian family on $\mathbb{R}^{K_q}$ is preserved under the affine group $x \mapsto Ax + b$ with $A \in \mathrm{GL}(K_q)$, $b \in \mathbb{R}^{K_q}$, and the relative entropy (indeed every $f$-divergence) is invariant under any common bijective change of variables, since the Jacobian factors cancel in the density ratio. Restricting to the origin-preserving linear subgroup $G = \mathrm{GL}(K_q) \subset \mathrm{Aff}(K_q)$, equivalently treating mean vectors as frame coordinates and not as elements of an affine space with translation freedom, gives the working gauge group. This is a modeling choice: $\mathrm{GL}(K_q)$ is the linear sector of the Gaussian symmetry, and the translation sector $b$, which would correspond to frame-dependent shifts of agent means, is available without being used. The invariance theorem below is the standard linear-pushforward statement~\cite{Amari2016}.

\begin{theorem}[$\mathrm{GL}(K_q)$ Gauge Invariance]
\label{thm:glk_invariance}
For any two Gaussian distributions $P = \mathcal{N}(\mu_P, \Sigma_P)$ and $Q = \mathcal{N}(\mu_Q, \Sigma_Q)$ on $\mathbb{R}^{K_q}$, and any invertible linear transformation $\Omega \in \mathrm{GL}(K_q)$, the relative entropy is invariant under simultaneous pushforward:
\begin{equation}
D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\Omega_* P \| \Omega_* Q) = D_{\mathrm{KL}}(P \| Q),
\label{eq:glk_invariance}
\end{equation}
where $\Omega_* P = \mathcal{N}(\Omega\mu_P, \Omega\Sigma_P\Omega^\top)$ denotes the pushforward of $P$ under $\Omega$. We assume $\Sigma_P, \Sigma_Q \succ 0$ throughout; the pushforward $\Omega\Sigma\Omega^\top$ remains positive-definite by Sylvester's law of inertia for $\Omega$ invertible. Singular $\Omega$ ($\det\Omega = 0$) is excluded by the assumption $\Omega \in \mathrm{GL}(K_q)$, which would otherwise make the pushforward degenerate.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
We use the Gaussian closed form of Eq.~\eqref{eq:gaussian_kl} (recorded again in Appendix~\ref{app:gaussian_kl}). Under the pushforwards $\Omega_* P = \mathcal{N}(\Omega\mu_P, \Omega\Sigma_P\Omega^\top)$ and $\Omega_* Q = \mathcal{N}(\Omega\mu_Q, \Omega\Sigma_Q\Omega^\top)$, the trace term satisfies
\begin{equation}
\operatorname{tr}\big[(\Omega\Sigma_Q\Omega^\top)^{-1}(\Omega\Sigma_P\Omega^\top)\big] = \operatorname{tr}\big[\Omega^{-\top}\Sigma_Q^{-1}\Omega^{-1}\Omega\Sigma_P\Omega^\top\big] = \operatorname{tr}(\Sigma_Q^{-1}\Sigma_P),
\end{equation}
the quadratic term satisfies
\begin{align}
&(\Omega\mu_P - \Omega\mu_Q)^\top (\Omega\Sigma_Q\Omega^\top)^{-1} (\Omega\mu_P - \Omega\mu_Q) \nonumber\\
&= (\mu_P - \mu_Q)^\top \Omega^\top \Omega^{-\top}\Sigma_Q^{-1}\Omega^{-1}\Omega (\mu_P - \mu_Q) = (\mu_P - \mu_Q)^\top \Sigma_Q^{-1}(\mu_P - \mu_Q),
\end{align}
and the log-determinant term satisfies
\begin{equation}
\log\frac{\det(\Omega\Sigma_Q\Omega^\top)}{\det(\Omega\Sigma_P\Omega^\top)} = \log\frac{(\det\Omega)^2 \det\Sigma_Q}{(\det\Omega)^2 \det\Sigma_P} = \log\frac{\det\Sigma_Q}{\det\Sigma_P},
\end{equation}
the $(\det\Omega)^2$ factors canceling identically. Since all three components are invariant,
\begin{equation}
D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\Omega_* P \| \Omega_* Q)
= D_{\mathrm{KL}}(P \| Q).
\end{equation}
\end{proof}

The proof extends to all $f$-divergences: for any convex $f$ with $f(1) = 0$,
\begin{equation}
D_f(\Omega_* P \| \Omega_* Q) = D_f(P \| Q), \quad \forall \Omega \in \mathrm{GL}(K_q),
\end{equation}
since the factors $|\det\Omega|$ from the density transformation cancel in the ratio $p(x)/q(x)$. Transport operators therefore need only satisfy $\det(\Omega_{ij}) \neq 0$, and the full $\mathrm{GL}(K_q)$ acts by passive re-expression without changing any divergence. The consequence for Regime~I is Proposition~\ref{prop:regime1_removability}: applied with $\Omega = U_i^{-1}$, the theorem removes the frames from every pairwise term. The exponential parameterization $e^{\phi}$ used throughout restricts to $\det > 0$, since $\det(\exp\phi) = \exp(\operatorname{tr}\phi) > 0$, and the canonical statement of its coverage limitation is this: for $K_q \ge 2$ the real matrix exponential $\exp: \mathfrak{gl}(K_q, \mathbb{R}) \to \mathrm{GL}(K_q, \mathbb{R})$ is \emph{not} surjective onto $\mathrm{GL}^+(K_q, \mathbb{R})$. Its image is the set of matrices admitting a real logarithm, a proper subset of the identity component (matrices with negative real eigenvalues of odd algebraic multiplicity, for instance, admit no real logarithm). The parameterization includes a neighborhood of the identity but is not a global parameterization of $\mathrm{GL}^+(K_q, \mathbb{R})$; products of two exponentials are required, and by the polar factorization following Eq.~\eqref{eq:transport_def} sufficient, for full coverage of $\mathrm{GL}^+$, while the component $\det < 0$ is not reached and is reserved for future study.

The gauge action on Gaussian states is $\rho(\Omega)\cdot(\mu,\Sigma)=(\Omega\mu,\Omega\Sigma\Omega^\top)$, the fundamental representation of $\mathrm{GL}(K_q)$. For belief vectors of dimension $d>3$ with $\mathrm{SO}(3)$ structure, $\rho$ decomposes into irreducibles with $d=\sum_k n_k(2j_k+1)$, where $j_k$ is the spin of the $k$-th irreducible block.

\paragraph{Flat base manifold.}
\label{sec:flat_base}
For base-space constructions we use a flat Euclidean background chart, $\mathcal{C} = \mathbb{R}^2$ with $g_{\mathcal{C}} = \delta_{\mu\nu}$. Every integral over $\mathcal{C}$ in the functional is taken against the Lebesgue density $dc$ of this chart, and the sector thresholds of Section~\ref{sec:collective_geometry} use its metric; these are fixed background structures, so the base is not free of structure that the agents did not produce. The chart metric is a device for integration, differentiation, and support placement, not a property of the index manifold, which carries no physical metric; the metrics that represent agent-relative geometry are the pullbacks induced by beliefs (Section~\ref{sec:pullback}). Those pullbacks enter no term of the functional, so the arc of Wheeler's loop that runs from the induced geometry back to the agents is not constructed here (W5); closing it would require the induced geometry to enter the dynamics, for example as the integration measure, which is base-transport work deferred to Supplement~B~\cite{DennisGaugeVFESuppB2026}. An induced pullback has rank at most $\dim\mathcal{C}$, so on the working base $\mathbb{R}^2$ it has at most two nonzero eigenvalues. Agents occupy compact support regions $\mathcal{U}_i \subset \mathbb{R}^2$. The degenerate case $\dim\mathcal{C}=0$, with all agents at a single base point $c_*$, is the setting in which the companion Theory develops the construction~\cite[Ch.~2]{DennisGaugeVFE2026}; there coarse-graining is purely informational. These choices give up intrinsic curvature of the base, global topological effects, and compact base manifolds without boundary; curved base manifolds are deferred with the rest of the base-transport theory.

\paragraph{Gauge-covariant relative entropy and the zero-dimensional limit.}
With these simplifications the transported relative entropy $D_{\mathrm{KL}}[q_i(c) \| \Omega_{ij}(c)q_j(c)]$ is invariant under simultaneous re-expression of both arguments (Theorem~\ref{thm:glk_invariance}). When the base manifold collapses to a single point ($\dim\mathcal{C} = 0$), all frames are constants $\phi_i(c) = \phi_i$, the frame gradients and curvature vanish, $A_\mu = F_{\mu\nu} = 0$, and the integral over $\mathcal{C}$ reduces to evaluation at $c_*$. The active gauge group is then one global copy of $G$ (Section~\ref{sec:gauge_covariance_principle}), and in Regime~I the frames are removable (Proposition~\ref{prop:regime1_removability}), so the construction at a point is a system of interacting probability laws related by passive re-expressions, not a gauge theory in the physicist's sense. Its gauge content at a point consists of the independent links of Regime~II, with their per-edge twist classes and graph holonomies, and of the frames' relation to fields held in a fixed frame.

With these simplifications the rung carries gauge-covariant transport between agents, information-geometric dynamics on curved statistical manifolds, and the cross-level structure on which multilevel meta-agent formation and top-down priors are built in Sections~\ref{sec:meta_agent_formation}--\ref{sec:ouroboros}. The results of this section have status P or D (Table~\ref{tab:claim_status}); the physical readings of the induced metrics (Section~\ref{sec:pullback}) and of the signature construction carry status S. With the rung's geometry fixed, the next section equips it with the consensus free-energy functional, whose descent drives the dynamics of the rest of the paper.
\section{The Single-Scale Consensus Free-Energy Functional}\label{sec:free_energy}

A single agent that minimizes the variational free energy $F[q] = \mathbb{E}_{q}[\log q(x) - \log p(o, x)] \geq -\log p(o)$ performs approximate Bayesian inference: the bound is tight exactly when $q$ equals the posterior $p(x \mid o)$, and the functional decomposes into a complexity term $D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q \| p)$ that anchors beliefs to the prior and an accuracy term $-\mathbb{E}_{q}[\log p(o \mid x)]$ that rewards explaining observations~\cite{Friston2010, parr2022active} (preliminaries in Appendix~\ref{app:variational_prelim}). This section extends the single-agent functional to a population of agents, obtains the inter-agent coupling and its Gibbs (softmax) attention weights from a source-selection construction, and assembles the complete functional whose descent defines the dynamics of the rest of the paper. We call that object the consensus free-energy functional. It is a composite potential: it is assembled from relative entropies and has the energy-minus-entropy form of a free energy, but unlike $F[q]$ it is not an upper bound on the surprisal of any single normalized model (Section~\ref{sec:complete_free_energy}), and the name ``variational free energy'' is reserved for the single-agent bound. Its stationary points are the consensus states that serve as the paper's formal model of Wheeler's question of how one world arises out of many observer-participants (W3), a structural correspondence (status S), not a derivation.

\subsection{Multi-Agent Extension: From Single Inference to Collective Dynamics}

The inter-agent belief-coupling terms $\sum_{ij}\beta_{ij}D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i\|\Omega_{ij}q_j)$, the attention-entropy term $\tau\beta_{ij}\log(\beta_{ij}/\pi_{ij})$, and the model-channel analog with $\gamma$ do not appear in closed variational form with an inter-frame transport law in the standard free-energy-principle literature~\cite{Friston2010, parr2022active}. The closest precedent is federated inference and belief sharing~\cite{friston2024federated}, in which an agent treats a neighbor's broadcast posterior as an observation and integrates it by variational updating, with precision deciding whom to attend to. In the flat-transport case $\Omega_{ij} = I$ the alignment block satisfies $\sum_j \beta_{ij}D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i\|q_j) = -H(q_i) - \sum_j \beta_{ij}\mathbb{E}_{q_i}[\log q_j]$, so it equals federated inference's treatment of broadcast posteriors as observation likelihoods up to the source-independent belief entropy $-H(q_i)$, with the attention weights in the normalized-precision role; we do not match the two precision dynamics exactly. Relative to federated inference the new ingredient is the $\mathrm{GL}(K_q)$ transport, not the coupling; relative to consensus with edge maps on graphs and to gauge-equivariant networks (Section~\ref{sec:related_work}), it is the coupling of probability laws through a relative-entropy functional with state-dependent attention weights. The multi-agent functional is therefore an engineered consensus energy, not a derivation from the free-energy principle alone; we retain the principle's citations for its conceptual backbone (variational inference, the evidence lower bound, and the complexity and accuracy decomposition).

The cross-scale prior has a different status. Section~\ref{sec:cross_scale_shadows} shows that the prior $p_i^{(\ell)}=\Omega_{iI}[q_I^{(\ell+1)}]$ is exact as the one-episode-lagged predictive prior of an episode-indexed joint~\cite[Ch.~12]{DennisGaugeVFE2026}; its same-time use in the energy is an energy-level simplification (Table~\ref{tab:claim_status}).

The extension introduces several ingredients. Each agent $i$ carries a belief $q_i(c)$ and a prior $p_i(c)$, local sections over the base manifold $\mathcal{C}$ that this paper analyzes at a fixed point; agents carry gauge frames $\phi_i(c) \in \mathfrak{gl}(K_q)$ that encode their internal coordinate systems (Section~\ref{sec:gauge_group_choice}); transport operators $\Omega_{ij} \in \mathrm{GL}^+(K_q)$, defined as in~\eqref{eq:transport_def}, translate between frames; and presence functions $\chi_i(c)$ indicate where agents maintain beliefs. The functional combines self terms, which align each belief with its prior through $D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| p_i)$ and each generative model with its hyper-prior through $D_{\mathrm{KL}}(s_i \| r_i)$; belief coupling $\beta_{ij}D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i\|\Omega_{ij}q_j)$, which penalizes disagreement after transport; model coupling $\gamma_{ij}D_{\mathrm{KL}}(s_i\|\tilde{\Omega}_{ij}s_j)$, which aligns generative models; and observation terms, through which beliefs explain sensory data.

Pairwise potentials $\psi_{ij}(q_i, \Omega_{ij}[q_j])$ could be chosen freely, but an arbitrary choice fixes neither the pairwise energies nor the weights with which each agent attends to them, and it has no connection to variational inference or information geometry. The construction below fixes both. The weights follow exactly from entropy-regularized minimization of a source-selection relative entropy. The pairwise energy is the relative entropy $D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i\|\Omega_{ij}q_j)$, with the receiver's law in the first slot (the variational, or I-projection, direction); the scope of its selection by the conditional representation theorem is stated in the status paragraph following the Gibbs solution.

\subsection{Attention from a Mixture-of-Sources Consensus Energy}
\label{sec:mixture_derivation}

We obtain the inter-agent coupling and its attention weights from a source-selection construction over transported neighbors. The construction is stated at a single base point $c \in \mathcal{C}$, and all distributions and group elements below are evaluated there; integrating the pointwise functional against agent supports gives the functional over $\mathcal{C}$ of Section~\ref{sec:complete_free_energy}.

\paragraph{Mixture-of-sources construction.}
Agent $i$ (the receiver) updates its belief $q_i(k)$ by attending to a set of source agents $j$. For the purpose of the alignment energy, the construction treats agent $i$'s latent state $k$ as if drawn from one of the neighboring agents, selected by a categorical latent variable $z \in \{1, \ldots, N\}$:
\begin{equation}
P(k, z) = P(k \mid z) P(z),
\label{eq:mixture_joint}
\end{equation}
where $P(z = j) = \pi_j$ is the prior probability of attending to agent $j$ (the source prior), and $P(k \mid z = j) = \mathcal{N}(k; \Omega_{ij}\mu_j, \Omega_{ij}\Sigma_j\Omega_{ij}^\top)$ is agent $j$'s belief transported into agent $i$'s frame by $\Omega_{ij}$. Agent $j$'s belief $q_j(k_j)$ lives in $j$'s local frame, and $\Omega_{ij}q_j$ is agent $i$'s representation of what agent $j$ believes. The model contains no observed variable: the alignment energy below is a relative entropy to the prior joint $P(k,z)$. The attention row obtained from it is the recognition-optimal source row, constant in $k$, and not the posterior source row $P(z = j \mid k) \propto \pi_j (\Omega_{ij}q_j)(k)$, which varies with $k$; the two differ by a nonnegative Jensen gap~\cite[Ch.~6]{DennisGaugeVFE2026} (the proposition on the cost of the constant-row restriction).

\paragraph{Variational family.}
We restrict the variational family to the product
\begin{equation}
Q(k, z) = q_i(k) \beta(z)
\label{eq:mixture_posterior}
\end{equation}
and minimize its relative entropy to $P(k,z)$, where $\beta(z = j) \equiv \beta_{ij} \geq 0$ are attention weights with $\sum_j \beta_{ij} = 1$. The factorization $Q(k \mid z) = q_i(k)$ for all $z$ is a substantive structural restriction and not a tractability simplification: it imposes source independence on the consensus belief, requiring that the one belief $q_i$ serve under every candidate source $j$. Without it the minimizer of $D_{\mathrm{KL}}(Q \| P)$ over unconstrained $Q(k,z)$ is $Q = P$, with $\beta = \pi$, $Q(k \mid z{=}j) = \Omega_{ij}q_j$, and zero alignment energy, so no divergence-dependent weights and no consensus pressure on a single belief arise. The Gibbs form $\beta_{ij} \propto \pi_j \exp[-D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| \Omega_{ij}q_j)/\tau]$ derived below is therefore the exact consequence of the source-independence restriction together with the entropy-regularized relative-entropy energy, and not of the variational principle alone.

\paragraph{Alignment energy.}
For a fixed row of source templates $P(k\mid z{=}j)$, the alignment component is the relative entropy of the restricted variational law to the source-selection model,
\begin{equation}
\mathcal{F}_{\text{align}} = D_{\mathrm{KL}}\big(Q(k,z) \| P(k,z)\big) = \sum_j \beta_{ij} \int q_i(k) \log \frac{q_i(k) \beta_{ij}}{P(k \mid z{=}j) \pi_j} \mathrm{d}k.
\end{equation}
Decomposing the logarithm yields
\begin{equation}
\mathcal{F}_{\text{align}} = \sum_j \beta_{ij} \left[ \underbrace{\int q_i(k) \log \frac{q_i(k)}{P(k \mid z{=}j)} \mathrm{d}k}_{= D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| \Omega_{ij} q_j)} + \log \beta_{ij} - \log \pi_j \right].
\label{eq:mixture_free_energy}
\end{equation}
The identification of the integral with $D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| \Omega_{ij} q_j)$ follows from the definition of the relative entropy after setting $P(k \mid z{=}j) = (\Omega_{ij} q_j)(k)$. It is an exact row identity for fixed templates; the dependence of the templates on the other agents' beliefs is addressed in the status paragraph below. Defining the alignment energy $E_{ij} \equiv D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| \Omega_{ij} q_j)$ gives
\begin{equation}
\mathcal{F}_{\text{align}} = \sum_j \beta_{ij} \big( E_{ij} + \log \beta_{ij} - \log \pi_j \big).
\label{eq:mixture_energy_entropy}
\end{equation}
For a uniform source prior $\pi_j = 1/N$ this has the energy-minus-entropy form $\mathcal{F}_{\text{align}} = \langle E \rangle_\beta - H(\beta) + \text{const}$, with $H(\beta) = -\sum_j \beta_{ij} \log \beta_{ij}$ the entropy of the attention distribution. For a non-uniform prior the prior term depends on $\beta$, and $\mathcal{F}_{\text{align}} = \langle E \rangle_\beta - H(\beta) - \sum_j \beta_{ij} \log \pi_j = \langle E \rangle_\beta + D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\beta_i \| \pi)$.

\paragraph{Minimization and the Gibbs solution.}
Minimizing $\mathcal{F}_{\text{align}}$ with respect to $\beta_{ij}$ subject to $\sum_j \beta_{ij} = 1$ with a Lagrange multiplier,
\begin{equation}
\mathcal{L} = \sum_j \beta_{ij}(E_{ij} + \log \beta_{ij} - \log \pi_j) - \lambda\left(\sum_j \beta_{ij} - 1\right),
\end{equation}
the stationarity condition $\partial \mathcal{L} / \partial \beta_{ik} = 0$ gives
\begin{equation}
E_{ik} + \log \beta_{ik} + 1 - \log \pi_k - \lambda = 0.
\end{equation}
Solving and normalizing gives Gibbs weights over the sources, at unit temperature with energies $E_{ik}$ and prior weights $\pi_k$,
\begin{equation}
\beta_{ik} = \frac{\pi_k \exp(-E_{ik})}{\sum_m \pi_m \exp(-E_{im})}.
\label{eq:softmax_attention_general}
\end{equation}
With $\operatorname{softmax}_k(x_k) = e^{x_k}/\sum_m e^{x_m}$, a uniform source prior $\pi_k = 1/N$ gives
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\beta_{ik} = \operatorname{softmax}_k(-E_{ik}) = \operatorname{softmax}_k\big(-D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| \Omega_{ik} q_k)\big).}
\label{eq:mixture_softmax}
\end{equation}
A temperature $\tau>0$ is introduced by writing the entropy-regularized objective as $\sum_j\beta_{ij}E_{ij}+\tau\sum_j\beta_{ij}\log(\beta_{ij}/\pi_j)$, whose minimizer is the Gibbs row at temperature $\tau$,
\begin{equation}
\beta_{ik}
= \frac{\pi_k\exp(-E_{ik}/\tau)}{\sum_m \pi_m\exp(-E_{im}/\tau)}.
\end{equation}
The energy carries the factor $1/\tau$, while the prior $\pi_k$ enters at unit log-weight. The energies, the temperature, and the weights are dimensionless, with relative entropies measured in nats. For $\tau \neq 1$ the tempered objective is not the relative entropy of the source-selection model above; that identification holds only at $\tau = 1$.

In the special case of a single base point with isotropic Gaussian beliefs of a common covariance and trivial frames, the energies reduce to scaled squared distances between means, and with a separately introduced bilinear compatibility between the compared vectors the Gibbs row reduces to the scaled dot-product attention of machine-learning sequence models~\cite{vaswani2017attention}. That limit, its variants, and its empirical study are developed in a companion paper~\cite{DennisGLKAttention2026}; none of it is used in the present paper.

\paragraph{Status of the construction.} The mixture-of-sources construction is a consensus-energy ansatz, not a derivation from a fixed state-level generative model: the component laws $P(k \mid z{=}j) = \Omega_{ij}q_j$ depend on the variational beliefs $q_j$ of other agents, so the row model moves when the population state moves. Retaining the entropy and prior term makes the resulting population functional a scalar potential whose row optimum has the log-partition value derived in Section~\ref{sec:complete_free_energy}. The choice of divergence is characterized by the conditional representation theorem (Appendix~\ref{app:conditional_representation}, Theorem~\ref{thm:representation_app}) within the class fixed by that appendix's assumptions (an $f$-divergence coupling, linear coupling in the weights, and exponential-family closure of the minimizer) and the real-analyticity hypothesis on the generator $f$. Within that class, the relative entropy $D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i\|\Omega_{ij}q_j)$ is the unique $f$-divergence for which agent $i$'s best response, with neighbor beliefs and attention held fixed, is the geometric-mean Boltzmann form. In the joint scalar functional, where each belief is also a transported template in its neighbors' rows, no nonzero $f$-divergence yields free-form exponential-family closure, and a Gaussian family imposes closure by restriction. The theorem therefore does not select the divergence of the joint functional; that divergence is declared here, as it is in the companion theory~\cite[Chs.~6 and~12]{DennisGaugeVFE2026}. Theorem~\ref{thm:state_level_elbo_nogo} proves that the within-level terms cannot, on an open mean-field family, equal the negative evidence lower bound of one fixed joint on the original agent-state variables. The obstruction does not exclude the exact zero-coupling hierarchical joint, the equilibrium-frozen auxiliary construction of Theorem~\ref{thm:self_consistent_source_equilibrium}, or the belief-configuration Gibbs lift of Theorem~\ref{thm:belief_configuration_gibbs_lift}.

\paragraph{Non-uniform source priors.} The source prior $\pi_j$ in~\eqref{eq:softmax_attention_general} carries structural prior knowledge about which sources are available and how likely each is to be relevant, independently of the current beliefs. Zero prior mass removes a source from the row altogether, so an availability or ordering restriction is a prior with restricted support; a prior that decays with a declared distance between agents encodes locality; and any such prior enters the logits additively as $\log\pi_j$, outside the factor $1/\tau$. If the self pair is admitted, $\Omega_{ii} = I$ gives $E_{ii} = 0$, the smallest energy in the row, so under a prior uniform on the support the self pair carries the largest weight; a prior that removes or down-weights $j = i$ is a separate modeling choice. The source prior is an input of the model; only the Gibbs form of the row is derived.

\subsection{Transport and Frame Redundancy}
\label{sec:gauge_transport_arbitrary}

The transport operators are pointwise frame transformations defined as in~\eqref{eq:transport_def}, with $\Omega_{ij}(c) \in \mathrm{GL}^+(K_q)$ and $\phi_i: \mathcal{U}_i \to \mathfrak{gl}(K_q)$ agent $i$'s frame field. They act on latent vectors through a representation $\rho$, $\Omega_{ij}[k_j] := \rho(\Omega_{ij}) k_j \in \mathbb{R}^{K_q}$ and $\tilde{\Omega}_{ij}[m_j] := \rho(\tilde{\Omega}_{ij}) m_j \in \mathbb{R}^{K_m}$; in the fundamental representation this is multiplication by an invertible matrix.

Under a common right translation $U_i(c) \mapsto U_i(c) g(c)$ of all frames every transport operator is unchanged, $\Omega_{ij}(c) \mapsto U_i(c) g(c) g(c)^{-1} U_j(c)^{-1} = \Omega_{ij}(c)$, so this change of reference (Section~\ref{sec:gauge_covariance_principle}) changes no term of the functional; the additive form $\phi_i \mapsto \phi_i + \xi$ represents it only when $\xi$ commutes with every $\phi_i$. At a single base point the active symmetry is one global copy of $G$ acting on frames and beliefs together, and the Regime~I frames can be removed from every alignment term by transporting each belief into a common frame (Proposition~\ref{prop:regime1_removability}), since $D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| U_iU_j^{-1}q_j) = D_{\mathrm{KL}}(U_i^{-1}q_i \| U_j^{-1}q_j)$ by Theorem~\ref{thm:glk_invariance}. On a base of dimension at least one the frame fields also induce the connection form of Eq.~\eqref{eq:connection_form} and the field strength of Eq.~\eqref{eq:field_strength}, flat in Regime~I (Section~\ref{sec:connection_forms}), with path-ordered transport $\Omega_\gamma = \mathcal{P}\exp(-\int_\gamma A_\mu \mathrm{d}x^\mu)$ along a path $\gamma$ and trivial holonomy for the vertex-local transport (Lemma~\ref{thm:vanishing_holonomy}). Transport along the base lies outside the single-point analysis of this paper and is deferred to the companion treatment of covers and connection descent~\cite[Ch.~B4]{DennisGaugeVFESuppB2026}.

\subsection{Base Priors and the Population Construction}

At intermediate levels each agent's prior $p_i$ and hyper-prior $r_i$ are set by the parent meta-agent through Eq.~\eqref{eq:cross_scale_shadow}, exact in the one-episode-lagged sense and used at the same time in the energy: $p_i^{(\ell)}(k_i; c) = \mathcal{N}(k_i; \mu_{0,i}^{(q)}(c), \Sigma_{0,i}^{(q)}(c))$ regularizes the latent state $k_i$ with parameters determined by $\Omega_{iI}[q_I^{(\ell+1)}]$, and $r_i^{(\ell)}(m_i; c) = \mathcal{N}(m_i; \mu_{0,i}^{(s)}(c), \Sigma_{0,i}^{(s)}(c))$ regularizes the generative-model parameters $m_i$ with parameters determined by $\tilde\Omega_{iI}[s_I^{(\ell+1)}]$. Different agents receive different prior parameters through different transport operators $\Omega_{iI}$ and the spatial profile of the parent's belief field, not through independent inductive biases. Because $p_i$ moves with the parent belief, treating it as fixed on the fast timescale requires the parent laws to enter only between episodes (Section~\ref{sec:timescales}), a schedule that is assumed and not derived. The population construction at each point $c$ combines the single-agent terms~\cite{Friston2010, parr2022active} with the two row-wise source-selection alignments derived above: the belief channel uses $\beta_{ij}(c)$ and $\Omega_{ij}(c)$, and the model channel uses $\gamma_{ij}(c)$ and $\tilde{\Omega}_{ij}(c)$. At the top level, where no parent exists, $r_i^{(\ell_{\max})}$ is a declared root (Section~\ref{sec:ouroboros}).

\subsection{The Pointwise Consensus Functional}

At a single point $c \in \mathcal{C}$ the consensus free-energy functional is
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
\mathcal{F}(c) &= \sum_i D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i(c) \| p_i(c)) + \lambda_h \sum_i D_{\mathrm{KL}}(s_i(c) \| r_i(c)) \\
&\quad + \sum_{i,j} \beta_{ij}(c) D_{\mathrm{KL}}\big(q_i(c) \big\| \Omega_{ij}(c)q_j(c)\big) + \tau \sum_{i,j} \beta_{ij}(c) \log\frac{\beta_{ij}(c)}{\pi_{ij}(c)} \\
&\quad + \sum_{i,j} \gamma_{ij}(c) D_{\mathrm{KL}}\big(s_i(c) \big\| \tilde{\Omega}_{ij}(c)s_j(c)\big) + \tau_\gamma \sum_{i,j} \gamma_{ij}(c) \log\frac{\gamma_{ij}(c)}{\pi^{(s)}_{ij}(c)} \\
&\quad - \sum_i \mathbb{E}_{q_i(c) \otimes s_i(c)}\big[\log p(o_i(c) \mid k_i, m_i)\big],
\end{aligned}
\label{eq:pointwise_free_energy}
\end{equation}
where $\beta_{ij}(c)$ and $\gamma_{ij}(c)$ are free variational parameters (one normalized row per agent and channel), $\pi_{ij}(c)$ and $\pi^{(s)}_{ij}(c)$ are the source priors of the two channels (Section~\ref{sec:mixture_derivation}), and $\tau > 0$ and $\tau_\gamma > 0$ are the belief-channel and model-channel temperatures. In the observation term $k_i \sim q_i(c)$ is agent $i$'s latent state, $m_i \sim s_i(c)$ its generative-model parameter, and $o_i(c)$ its observation at $c$; a record shared by all agents is the special case $o_i = o$.

\paragraph{Typing of the observation term.} The observation term is typed jointly in $(k_i, m_i)$ because the evidence bound forces this typing. For agent $i$'s private block, with prior $p_i(\mathrm{d}k) r_i(\mathrm{d}m)$, likelihood $p(o_i \mid k, m)$, and any recognition law $\zeta_i$ on $(k_i, m_i)$ with marginals $q_i$ and $s_i$, the negative evidence lower bound is $D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\zeta_i \| p_i \otimes r_i) - \mathbb{E}_{\zeta_i}[\log p(o_i \mid k_i, m_i)]$, the energy form of the collective free energy~\cite[Ch.~5]{DennisGaugeVFE2026}, and
\begin{equation}
D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\zeta_i \| p_i \otimes r_i) = D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| p_i) + D_{\mathrm{KL}}(s_i \| r_i) + I_{\zeta_i}(K; M),
\label{eq:observation_typing_ledger}
\end{equation}
which follows by writing $\log[\zeta_i/(p_i r_i)] = \log[\zeta_i/(q_i s_i)] + \log(q_i/p_i) + \log(s_i/r_i)$ and taking the $\zeta_i$-expectation. Under the product restriction $\zeta_i = q_i \otimes s_i$ the mutual information vanishes, and the block contributes the two self terms, with $\lambda_h = 1$, and the observation term of~\eqref{eq:pointwise_free_energy}. The predictive form $-\mathbb{E}_{q_i}[\log \int p(o_i \mid k_i, m) s_i(\mathrm{d}m)]$ lies below the joint term by the Jensen gap $\mathbb{E}_{q_i}[D_{\mathrm{KL}}(s_i \| s_i^{(o,k)})] \geq 0$, where $s_i^{(o,k)}(\mathrm{d}m) \propto p(o_i \mid k, m) s_i(\mathrm{d}m)$; paired with $D_{\mathrm{KL}}(s_i \| r_i)$ it is not the negative evidence lower bound of the block and can fall below the block's surprisal.

\paragraph{Temperature.} The temperature may be parameterized as $\tau = \kappa\sqrt{K_q}$ with a dimensionless constant $\kappa$. For independent coordinates the spread of the energies $E_{ij}$ across sources grows as $\sqrt{K_q}$, so this choice keeps logit differences of order one as $K_q$ varies; it does not normalize the absolute logits. On the model fiber the same rule gives $\tau_\gamma = \kappa\sqrt{K_m}$, which equals $\tau$ only when $K_m = K_q$ or when one temperature is declared for both channels. The temperature is not a variational parameter of $\mathcal{F}$. With $Z_i = \sum_j \pi_{ij} e^{-E_{ij}/\tau}$, the row value at the Gibbs weights is $-\tau \log Z_i$ (Section~\ref{sec:complete_free_energy}), and, using $\log(\beta^*_{ij}/\pi_{ij}) = -E_{ij}/\tau - \log Z_i$,
\begin{equation}
\frac{\partial}{\partial \tau}\big(-\tau \log Z_i\big) = -\log Z_i - \frac{\langle E_i \rangle_{\beta_i^*}}{\tau} = D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\beta_i^* \| \pi_i) \geq 0,
\end{equation}
the analog of $\partial F/\partial T = -S$ with $S = -D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\beta_i^* \| \pi_i) \leq 0$. Minimizing $\mathcal{F}$ over $\tau$ therefore drives $\tau \to 0^+$, where $-\tau \log Z_i \to \min_j E_{ij}$ on the support of $\pi_i$ and each row collapses onto its lowest-energy source. The constant $\kappa$ is accordingly declared as part of the model, and an objective that adapts it must be external to $\mathcal{F}$.

\subsection{The Complete Consensus Functional}
\label{sec:complete_free_energy}

\paragraph{Status of the functional.} The functional displayed below is an ansatz, not a theorem. Its form was fixed by requiring every term to transform covariantly under the frame changes of Section~\ref{sec:one_rung}, together with the source-selection reading of Section~\ref{sec:mixture_derivation}; it is not obtained from a single normalized generative model over the joint population state (Theorem~\ref{thm:state_level_elbo_nogo}). In the terminology of the companion theory it is a composite potential, a descent potential assembled from normalized pieces that carries no evidence interpretation of its own~\cite[Ch.~12]{DennisGaugeVFE2026}. Where normalized laws are required the companion supersedes it: its population objective is one collective evidence bound~\cite[Ch.~5]{DennisGaugeVFE2026}, and pairwise divergences enter an evidence bound there only as terms of a normalized Gibbs prior inside the tower free energy~\cite[Ch.~12]{DennisGaugeVFE2026}. Given the functional, the Gibbs attention row~\eqref{eq:beta_optimal} and the reduced row value $-\tau \log Z_i$ follow exactly, as shown below and in Appendix~\ref{app:gradients}. The geometric-mean Boltzmann form of the belief update holds only for the frozen-neighbor best response of Section~\ref{sec:mixture_derivation}; at a stationary point of the complete functional each belief also responds to the rows in which it is a sender, and the geometric pool is not stationary there. Because the belief entropy enters through the self term and through each of agent $i$'s alignment rows, the functional weights $H(q_i)$ by $1 + B_i$ with $B_i := \sum_j \beta_{ij} = 1$, so the frozen-row best response is a Gibbs state at temperature two in units in which Bayesian updating has temperature one (Appendix~\ref{app:covariance_dynamics}).

The exact probabilistic status of the attention row is established in the companion theory, and it bounds what may be claimed here. On a sample space enlarged by a source label with prior $\pi_i$ and a record likelihood proportional to $\exp(-D_{ij}/\tau)$, the optimal recognition row held constant in the latent state is the Gibbs row of the expected energies, and the row contributes $D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\beta_i \| \pi_i) + \tau^{-1}\sum_j \beta_{ij} \mathbb{E} D_{ij}$ to the negative evidence lower bound~\cite[Ch.~6]{DennisGaugeVFE2026} (the proposition on exact posterior and recognition attention). The row block of Eq.~\eqref{eq:free_energy_functional_final} is $\tau$ times this quantity, so for $\tau \neq 1$ it is not a sector of one evidence bound alongside the unit-weight self and observation terms~\cite[Ch.~12]{DennisGaugeVFE2026} (the proposition on Gibbs rows). A second condition arises when the source records are Gaussian links. Let $y_i \mid y_j, J_i = j \sim \mathcal{N}(\Omega_{ij} y_j, L_{ij})$, with the sources topologically ordered so that the links define a normalized directed joint, and let the recognition be mean-field Gaussian with $q_j = \mathcal{N}(\mu_j, \Sigma_j)$. With $A_{ij} = \Omega_{ij}\Sigma_j\Omega_{ij}^\top$, the coordinate update of the label is
\begin{equation}
\log \beta_{ij} = \log \pi_{ij} - \tfrac{1}{2}\log\det L_{ij} - \tfrac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}\big[L_{ij}^{-1}(\Sigma_i + A_{ij})\big] - \tfrac{1}{2}(\mu_i - \Omega_{ij}\mu_j)^\top L_{ij}^{-1}(\mu_i - \Omega_{ij}\mu_j) + \text{const},
\end{equation}
while $-E_{ij}/\tau = -\frac{1}{2\tau}[\operatorname{tr}(A_{ij}^{-1}\Sigma_i) + (\mu_i - \Omega_{ij}\mu_j)^\top A_{ij}^{-1}(\mu_i - \Omega_{ij}\mu_j) - K_q + \log\det A_{ij} - \log\det\Sigma_i]$. Matching the two as an identity in the unconstrained sender mean forces $L_{ij} = \tau A_{ij}$; the trace terms then agree up to a source-independent constant, and the log-determinants leave the extra source-dependent logit $-\tfrac{1}{2}(1 - 1/\tau)\log\det(\Omega_{ij}\Sigma_j\Omega_{ij}^\top)$, which vanishes for every configuration only at $\tau = 1$. The row at the operating temperature is therefore the exact coordinate update of no model in this family, and the tie $L_{ij} = \tau A_{ij}$ sets a generative parameter equal to a recognition covariance, so the joint is fixed only if $\Sigma_j$ is frozen at its current value during the update; the tie is a declared postulate and not a stationary point of the same objective. None of this alters the status of the functional as an ansatz, and none of it is needed for the consequences derived below, which follow from the functional as posited.

Extending to fields over the base manifold $\mathcal{C}$ with presence functions $\chi_i(c)$, we obtain the canonical functional of this paper:
\begin{equation}
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\mathcal{F}[\{q_i\}, \{p_i\}, \{s_i\}, \{r_i\}, \{\phi_i\}, \{\beta_{ij}\}, \{\gamma_{ij}\}]
&= \sum_i \int_{\mathcal{C}} \chi_i(c) D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i(c) \| p_i(c)) \mathrm{d}c \\
&\quad + \lambda_h \sum_i \int_{\mathcal{C}} \chi_i(c) D_{\mathrm{KL}}(s_i(c) \| r_i(c)) \mathrm{d}c \\
&\quad + \sum_{i} \int_{\mathcal{C}} \chi_i(c) \sum_j \Big[\beta_{ij}(c) D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| \Omega_{ij} q_j) + \tau \beta_{ij}(c) \log\tfrac{\beta_{ij}(c)}{\tilde{\pi}_{ij}(c)}\Big] \mathrm{d}c \\
&\quad + \sum_{i} \int_{\mathcal{C}} \chi_i(c) \sum_j \Big[\gamma_{ij}(c) D_{\mathrm{KL}}(s_i \| \tilde{\Omega}_{ij} s_j) + \tau_\gamma \gamma_{ij}(c) \log\tfrac{\gamma_{ij}(c)}{\tilde{\pi}^{(s)}_{ij}(c)}\Big] \mathrm{d}c \\
&\quad - \sum_i \int_{\mathcal{C}} \chi_i(c) \mathbb{E}_{q_i(c) \otimes s_i(c)}\big[\log p(o_i(c) \mid k_i, m_i)\big] \mathrm{d}c,
\end{aligned}
}
\label{eq:free_energy_functional_final}
\end{equation}
where the sender-presence-weighted prior is $\tilde{\pi}_{ij}(c) := \chi_j(c)\pi_{ij}(c) / \sum_k \chi_k(c)\pi_{ik}(c)$, defined where $\chi_i(c) > 0$ and the denominator is positive (and analogously $\tilde{\pi}^{(s)}_{ij}$), and the observation term carries the joint typing of the pointwise functional. The weight $\lambda_h > 0$ on the model-channel self term equals $1$ when the self and observation terms form the evidence bound of each agent's private block (the typing paragraph above); setting $\lambda_h = 0$ freezes the slow subsystem, a special case that a prospective test must declare explicitly. The same $\lambda_h$ weights the depth-one model coupling of Eq.~\eqref{eq:ouroboros_F} and is held fixed across levels by assumption in the hierarchical coarse-graining of Section~\ref{sec:rg_status}; the exact coarse-graining is not shown to preserve it, and at one loop it shifts a coefficient of the same kind, the parent's entropy weight, in proportion to cluster size (Eq.~\eqref{eq:rg_entropy_shift}). The belief self term $D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| p_i)$ is shown with unit weight, the $\alpha = 1$ specialization of the per-agent self-coupling weight $\alpha_i(c)$; promoting this weight to a state-dependent variational parameter is the generalization developed in Section~\ref{sec:precision_envelope}. Differentiating the row Lagrangian $L_i = \int \chi_i \sum_j[\beta_{ij}D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| \Omega_{ij} q_j) + \tau\beta_{ij}\log(\beta_{ij}/\tilde{\pi}_{ij})] - \lambda_i(c)(\sum_j \beta_{ij} - 1)$ and imposing the row-normalization constraint $\sum_j \beta_{ij}(c) = 1$ yields the closed-form Gibbs row
\begin{equation}
\beta_{ij}^*(c) =
\frac{
  \chi_{j}(c)\pi_{ij}(c)\exp\left[
    -\tfrac{1}{\tau}D_{\mathrm{KL}}\big(q_i(c) \big\| \Omega_{ij}q_j(c)\big)
  \right]
}{
  \displaystyle\sum_{k}
  \chi_{k}(c)\pi_{ik}(c)\exp\left[
    -\tfrac{1}{\tau}D_{\mathrm{KL}}\big(q_i(c) \big\| \Omega_{ik}q_k(c)\big)
  \right]
}.
\label{eq:beta_optimal}
\end{equation}
The receiver presence $\chi_i(c)$ multiplies the whole row and does not affect its minimizer. The sender presence $\chi_j(c)$ enters multiplicatively in numerator and denominator, so $\beta^*_{ij}(c)$ vanishes where $\chi_j(c) = 0$ and reduces to the prior-weighted Gibbs row where all senders are fully present; for hard masks $\chi_j \in \{0, 1\}$ it is the Gibbs row restricted to the active set $\mathcal{J}_i(c) = \{j : \chi_j(c) = 1\}$. The result is the general solution~\eqref{eq:softmax_attention_general} in pair-indexed form, and a uniform prior on the active set recovers~\eqref{eq:mixture_softmax}. The model-channel weights $\gamma_{ij}^*(c)$ have the analogous form, with $\tau_\gamma$, $\tilde{\Omega}_{ij}$, $s_j$, and $\pi^{(s)}_{ij}$ in place of $\tau$, $\Omega_{ij}$, $q_j$, and $\pi_{ij}$.

Here $\chi_i(c) \in [0, 1]$ is agent $i$'s presence function. Each alignment row is weighted by its receiver's presence, as the self and observation terms are, and the sender presences are carried inside $\tilde{\pi}_{ij}$, so that the row-simplex constraint $\sum_j \beta_{ij}(c) = 1$ is uniform across pairs. Absorbing the full pairwise presence $\chi_i\chi_j$ into the prior instead would cancel $\chi_i$ between numerator and denominator and leave the row with unit weight where agent $i$ is absent, which is why the receiver presence multiplies the row explicitly. This bookkeeping is the convention for every later restatement of the alignment terms. We adopt the smooth-section convention $\chi_i: \mathcal{C} \to [0, 1]$ in place of a hard $\{0, 1\}$ indicator, because the bundle language of Section~\ref{sec:agents_as_sections} requires agents to be smooth sections over open domains, and a discontinuous $\chi_i$ would make the integrands distributional at support boundaries; the informal $\chi_i \in \{0, 1\}$ is the sharply localized limit.

Setting $\gamma_{ij} = 0$ freezes model alignment, a case that a prospective test must declare separately from an active model channel. The model-alignment term is retained because consensus on generative models, the content of $\gamma_{ij}D_{\mathrm{KL}}(s_i\|\tilde{\Omega}_{ij}s_j)$, is required by the meta-agent and declared-root constructions of Sections~\ref{sec:meta_agent_formation}--\ref{sec:ouroboros}. Stiffness terms (Section~\ref{sec:mass}) are optional additions at a point. A Yang-Mills penalty~\cite{Yang1954} on the curvature of Eq.~\eqref{eq:field_strength} vanishes identically for Regime-I frames by the Maurer-Cartan identity (Section~\ref{sec:connection_forms}); a nonzero lattice action belongs to the independent-link sector of Section~\ref{sec:discrete_regime_ii}; and base-direction regularizers such as frame-field smoothness belong to base transport, deferred to~\cite[Ch.~B4]{DennisGaugeVFESuppB2026}.

\paragraph{Complete functional versus the entropy-suppressed surrogate.}
The boxed functional~\eqref{eq:free_energy_functional_final} includes the attention entropy and prior terms explicitly. For one row, write
\begin{equation}
\mathcal{F}_{i,\mathrm{full}}(\beta,x)=\sum_j\beta_{ij}E_{ij}(x)+\tau\sum_j\beta_{ij}\log\frac{\beta_{ij}}{\tilde\pi_{ij}},
\qquad
Z_i(x)=\sum_j\tilde\pi_{ij}\exp\left[-\frac{E_{ij}(x)}{\tau}\right].
\label{eq:attention_full_reduced_identity}
\end{equation}
Minimization on the row simplex gives $\beta_{ij}^*=\tilde\pi_{ij}\exp(-E_{ij}/\tau)/Z_i$ and the exact identity
\begin{equation}
\mathcal{F}_{i,\mathrm{full}}(\beta^*(x),x)=\mathcal{F}_{i,\mathrm{red}}(x)=-\tau\log Z_i(x).
\label{eq:attention_full_equals_reduced}
\end{equation}
The row stationarity condition makes the explicit $\mathrm{d}\beta^*/\mathrm{d}x$ terms cancel, so
\begin{equation}
\mathrm{d}\mathcal{F}_{i,\mathrm{red}}=\sum_j\beta_{ij}^*\mathrm{d}E_{ij}.
\label{eq:attention_envelope_differential}
\end{equation}
The entropy-suppressed quantity $S_i(x):=\sum_j\beta_{ij}^*(x)E_{ij}(x)$ is a different scalar potential. Differentiating every occurrence of $x$ gives $\mathrm{d}S_i=\sum_j\beta_{ij}^*\mathrm{d}E_{ij}-\tau^{-1}\operatorname{Cov}_{\beta_i^*}(E_{ij},\mathrm{d}E_{ij})$. Its Hessian remains symmetric wherever the energies are twice continuously differentiable; the response term shows only that $S_i\neq\mathcal{F}_{i,\mathrm{red}}$, not that $S_i$ is nonintegrable. A nonintegrable field arises only after an additional asymmetric approximation that keeps the receiver-row derivatives and drops the sender-row derivatives of the same scalar. The dynamics of this paper descend the complete functional: the reduced rows~\eqref{eq:attention_full_reduced_identity} of both alignment channels are added to one scalar, and that scalar is differentiated. Neither the entropy-suppressed surrogate nor the receiver-only field is used.

\section{State-Dependent Precision, the Envelope Theorem, Environmental Agents, and Symmetry Breaking}\label{sec:precision_envelope}

The canonical functional~\eqref{eq:free_energy_functional_final} fixes the belief self-coupling weight at unity. This section first promotes that weight to a state-dependent variational parameter, then performs the envelope reduction that substitutes the optimal attention weights back into the functional, then recasts the external observation terms as couplings to environmental agents, and finally identifies the global symmetry that observations break explicitly.

\subsection{State-Dependent Prior Precision}
\label{sec:state_dependent_precision}

The self-coupling term $D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i(c)\|p_i(c))$ in Eq.~\eqref{eq:free_energy_functional_final} carries an implicit unit weight, equivalent to assuming that each agent's prior precision is fixed and uniform in context. In a generative hierarchy this is restrictive: the trust an agent should place in its prior depends on how far its current belief has moved from it, and this varies across the manifold and with the agent's beliefs. Promoting the self-coupling weight to a per-agent variational parameter $\alpha_i(c) > 0$ reproduces the precision-weighted prediction-error form of the self-coupling gradient. Here $\alpha_i(c)$ acts as a state-dependent scalar gain on the prior-precision metric, not as the optimized precision matrix itself, so the correspondence with predictive coding is structural and does not recover its precision update exactly.

\paragraph{Precision as a variational parameter.}
With $\alpha_i$ a per-agent variational parameter and $R(\alpha_i)$ a precision regularizer preventing degenerate $\alpha_i \to 0$ or $\alpha_i \to \infty$ solutions, the self-coupling sector of Eq.~\eqref{eq:free_energy_functional_final} generalizes to
\begin{equation}
\mathcal{F}_i^{\mathrm{adaptive}}
= \int_\mathcal{C} \chi_i(c)\Big[\alpha_i(c) D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i(c)\|p_i(c)) + R(\alpha_i(c))\Big] dc
+ \mathcal{F}_i^{\mathrm{align}}
- \int_\mathcal{C} \chi_i(c) \mathbb{E}_{q_i(c)\otimes s_i(c)}[\log p(o_i(c)\mid k_i,m_i)] dc,
\label{eq:free_energy_adaptive}
\end{equation}
where $\mathcal{F}_i^{\mathrm{align}}$ collects the alignment terms unchanged from Eq.~\eqref{eq:free_energy_functional_final}. We take the log-barrier regularizer
\begin{equation}
R(\alpha_i) = b_0 \alpha_i - c_0 \log \alpha_i, \qquad b_0 > 0, \quad c_0 > 0,
\label{eq:precision_regularizer}
\end{equation}
in which the linear term penalizes arbitrarily large precision and the logarithmic term enforces $\alpha_i > 0$ and penalizes vanishing precision. For the rate-parameterized density $p(\alpha_i\mid b_0,c_0)=b_0^{c_0+1}\alpha_i^{c_0}\exp(-b_0\alpha_i)/\Gamma(c_0+1)$, the full negative log-density is
\begin{equation}
-\log p(\alpha_i\mid b_0,c_0)
=b_0\alpha_i-c_0\log\alpha_i+\log\Gamma(c_0+1)-(c_0+1)\log b_0.
\label{eq:precision_gamma_normalized}
\end{equation}
The last two terms may be dropped only when $b_0$ and $c_0$ are fixed. Holding $\alpha_i$ fixed, their hyperparameter derivatives are
\begin{equation}
\partial_{b_0}[-\log p]=\alpha_i-\frac{c_0+1}{b_0},
\qquad
\partial_{c_0}[-\log p]=-\log\alpha_i+\psi(c_0+1)-\log b_0,
\label{eq:precision_gamma_hyper_derivatives}
\end{equation}
where $\psi$ is the digamma function. Learning $b_0$ and $c_0$ would require these terms together with a hyperprior on them, and it is not pursued here: the hyperparameters are fixed throughout. The stationary point~\eqref{eq:state_dependent_alpha} derived next is the minimizer of the declared penalty $\alpha_i D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i\|p_i)+R(\alpha_i)$ at fixed hyperparameters. It is not the MAP precision of a normalized hierarchical model, because the term $\alpha_i D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i\|p_i)$ carries no $\alpha_i$-dependent normalizer. For comparison, the normalized hierarchical Gaussian model $k_i\sim\mathcal{N}(\mu_{p,i},\Sigma_{p,i}/\alpha_i)$ with the same Gamma prior has, at stationarity of $\mathbb{E}_{q_i}[-\log p(k_i\mid\alpha_i)]-\log p(\alpha_i\mid b_0,c_0)$, the precision $\alpha_i=(c_0+K_q/2)/(b_0+Q_i)$ with $Q_i=\tfrac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{q_i}[(k_i-\mu_{p,i})^\top\Sigma_{p,i}^{-1}(k_i-\mu_{p,i})]$, which differs from Eq.~\eqref{eq:state_dependent_alpha} in both numerator and denominator.

\paragraph{Optimal precision.}
Stationarity of $\mathcal{F}_i^{\mathrm{adaptive}}$ with respect to $\alpha_i$ at fixed beliefs gives $D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i\|p_i) + b_0 - c_0/\alpha_i = 0$, with closed-form solution
\begin{equation}
\boxed{
\alpha_i^*(c) = \frac{c_0}{b_0 + D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i(c)\|p_i(c))}.
}
\label{eq:state_dependent_alpha}
\end{equation}
The per-agent objective $g(\alpha_i) = (b_0 + D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i\|p_i))\alpha_i - c_0\log\alpha_i$ has $g''(\alpha_i) = c_0/\alpha_i^2 > 0$ for all $\alpha_i > 0$, so it is strictly convex on the positive ray and $\alpha_i^*$ is its unique global minimizer. When an agent's belief is close to its prior, $D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i\|p_i) \approx 0$ and $\alpha_i^* \approx c_0/b_0$ takes its maximum value, so the agent trusts its prior strongly and resists displacement by the alignment and observation terms; when the belief has drifted far, $D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i\|p_i)$ is large and $\alpha_i^* \to 0$, so the prior's influence is reduced and the alignment and observation terms dominate. The hyperparameter $b_0$ controls sensitivity, and the ratio $c_0/b_0$ sets the maximum precision attained when beliefs match the prior exactly. Substituting the optimum gives the reduced self term in closed form,
\begin{equation}
\min_{\alpha_i>0}\big[\alpha_i D + R(\alpha_i)\big] = c_0 + c_0\log\frac{b_0 + D}{c_0},
\qquad D = D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i\|p_i),
\end{equation}
a logarithmic penalty that is concave in $D$. Its slope $c_0/(b_0+D)=\alpha_i^*$ decreases as the belief drifts, which is the weakening of the anchor described above stated in one formula.

\paragraph{Adaptive anchoring and the uniform limit.}
With isotropic prior covariance $\bar\Sigma_{p,i} = \sigma_p^2 I$ and narrow Gaussian beliefs $q_i = \mathcal{N}(\mu_i, \sigma_q^2 I)$ with $\sigma_q^2 \ll \sigma_p^2$, the adaptive prior term contributes the $\mu_i$-dependent quadratic anchoring $\frac{\alpha_i}{2\sigma_p^2}\|\mu_i - \mu_{\mathrm{prior}}\|^2$ with per-agent stiffness $\lambda_{p,i} \equiv \alpha_i/\sigma_p^2$. Substituting the optimum~\eqref{eq:state_dependent_alpha} makes this stiffness state dependent in the way just described. The constant-precision specialization $\alpha_i \to \alpha = 1$ collapses Eq.~\eqref{eq:free_energy_adaptive} to the canonical functional~\eqref{eq:free_energy_functional_final} and gives a uniform quadratic penalty with stiffness $\lambda_p = \alpha/\sigma_p^2$, the product of two quantities the framework treats independently, a precision gain and a prior width. This specialization connects to the precision-as-stiffness interpretation of Section~\ref{sec:mass}.

\paragraph{Per-coordinate precision for diagonal Gaussians.}
\label{par:per_coord_alpha}
For agents whose belief and prior are diagonal Gaussians in a fixed frame, $q_i = \mathcal{N}(\mu_i, \operatorname{diag}(\sigma_{q,i}))$ and $p_i = \mathcal{N}(\mu_{p,i}, \operatorname{diag}(\sigma_{p,i}))$, the joint distributions factorize and the relative entropy inherits the additive decomposition
\begin{equation}
D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| p_i) = \sum_{k=1}^{K_q} D_{\mathrm{KL}}^{(k)}(q_i^{(k)} \| p_i^{(k)}),
\quad
D_{\mathrm{KL}}^{(k)} = \tfrac{1}{2}\left[ \frac{\sigma_{q,i}^{(k)}}{\sigma_{p,i}^{(k)}} + \frac{(\mu_{q,i}^{(k)} - \mu_{p,i}^{(k)})^2}{\sigma_{p,i}^{(k)}} - 1 + \log \frac{\sigma_{p,i}^{(k)}}{\sigma_{q,i}^{(k)}} \right].
\label{eq:kl_decomposition_per_coord}
\end{equation}
The standard mean-field treatment of a product of univariate Gaussians under Gamma-Normal conjugacy~\cite[\S 10.1.3, \S 2.3.6]{Bishop2006} places an independent Gamma prior on each coordinate precision with hyperparameters $(c_0^{(k)}, b_0^{(k)})$. Here $\alpha_i^{(k)}$ weights an entire divergence and does not parameterize a Gaussian precision, so the correspondence is structural and is not an exact conjugacy. Promoting the precision parameter from a scalar $\alpha_i$ to a per-coordinate vector $\alpha_i^{(k)} > 0$ replaces the self-coupling term by
\begin{equation}
\alpha_i  D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| p_i) + R(\alpha_i)
\longmapsto
\sum_{k=1}^{K_q} \Big[\alpha_i^{(k)} D_{\mathrm{KL}}^{(k)} + R_k(\alpha_i^{(k)})\Big],
\qquad
R_k(\alpha_i^{(k)}) = b_0^{(k)} \alpha_i^{(k)} - c_0^{(k)} \log \alpha_i^{(k)},
\label{eq:adaptive_self_coupling_per_coord}
\end{equation}
with stationary solution
\begin{equation}
\boxed{
\alpha_i^{(k)*}(c) = \frac{c_0^{(k)}}{b_0^{(k)} + D_{\mathrm{KL}}^{(k)}(q_i^{(k)}(c) \| p_i^{(k)}(c))}.
}
\label{eq:state_dependent_alpha_per_coord}
\end{equation}
This per-coordinate analog of Eq.~\eqref{eq:state_dependent_alpha} behaves in the same way coordinate by coordinate, with maximum precision $c_0^{(k)}/b_0^{(k)}$. The scalar form is recovered by tying $(c_0^{(k)}, b_0^{(k)}) = (c_0, b_0)$ across $k$ and using one shared scalar gain on the summed divergence, yielding $\alpha_i^* = c_0 / (b_0 + \sum_k D_{\mathrm{KL}}^{(k)})$. The per-coordinate form is an optional refinement; the rest of the paper uses the scalar form with fixed $b_0$ and $c_0$.

\paragraph{Product rule and envelope cancellation.}
The closed form~\eqref{eq:state_dependent_alpha} makes $\alpha_i^*$ a function of the belief through $D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| p_i)$. Differentiating with respect to any belief parameter $\theta \in \{\mu_i, \Sigma_i\}$,
\begin{equation}
\frac{\partial \alpha_i^*}{\partial \theta} = -\frac{(\alpha_i^*)^2}{c_0} \frac{\partial D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| p_i)}{\partial \theta},
\label{eq:alpha_chain_rule_itfb}
\end{equation}
and applying the product rule to the self-coupling term $\alpha_i^* D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| p_i)$ in isolation gives
\begin{equation}
\frac{\partial}{\partial \theta}\bigl(\alpha_i^* D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| p_i)\bigr) = \alpha_i^* \frac{\partial D_{\mathrm{KL}}}{\partial \theta} - \frac{(\alpha_i^*)^2}{c_0} D_{\mathrm{KL}} \frac{\partial D_{\mathrm{KL}}}{\partial \theta} = (\alpha_i^*)^2 \frac{b_0}{c_0} \frac{\partial D_{\mathrm{KL}}}{\partial \theta}.
\label{eq:alpha_product_rule_itfb}
\end{equation}
Equation~\eqref{eq:alpha_product_rule_itfb} is the derivative of $\alpha_i^* D_{\mathrm{KL}}$ alone, not of the complete adaptive sector, which also carries the regularizer $R(\alpha_i^*)$. Differentiating $R(\alpha_i^*)$ with the same chain rule and using the stationarity identity $b_0 - c_0/\alpha_i^* = -D_{\mathrm{KL}}$ gives $\partial R(\alpha_i^*)/\partial\theta = +(\alpha_i^*)^2 c_0^{-1} D_{\mathrm{KL}}  \partial D_{\mathrm{KL}}/\partial\theta$, which combines with Eq.~\eqref{eq:alpha_product_rule_itfb} to
\begin{equation}
\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta}\bigl(\alpha_i^* D_{\mathrm{KL}} + R(\alpha_i^*)\bigr) = \frac{(\alpha_i^*)^2}{c_0}\bigl(b_0 + D_{\mathrm{KL}}\bigr)\frac{\partial D_{\mathrm{KL}}}{\partial\theta} = \alpha_i^*\frac{\partial D_{\mathrm{KL}}}{\partial\theta},
\label{eq:alpha_envelope_cancel_itfb}
\end{equation}
the envelope form, since $b_0 + D_{\mathrm{KL}} = c_0/\alpha_i^*$. The product-rule correction is therefore canceled exactly by the regularizer's $\theta$-dependence: differentiating the complete adaptive functional~\eqref{eq:free_energy_adaptive}, with $R$ included, gives $\alpha_i^* \partial D_{\mathrm{KL}}/\partial\theta$, and no correction term survives in the precision sector. The canonical attention block has the same cancellation structure: differentiating its complete energy-plus-entropy scalar yields Eq.~\eqref{eq:attention_envelope_differential}, whereas differentiating the entropy-suppressed scalar $S_i$ yields the separate covariance response term.

\subsection{The Envelope Theorem and the Reduced Free Energy}
\label{sec:envelope_theorem}

The boxed functional~\eqref{eq:free_energy_functional_final} is the complete (un-minimized) consensus functional, in which $\beta_{ij}$ and $\gamma_{ij}$ are optimized jointly with the belief and model fields. Substituting the optimal weights $\beta_{ij}^* = \operatorname{softmax}_j(-E_{ij}/\tau + \log\pi_{ij})$ from~\eqref{eq:beta_optimal} into the tempered row~\eqref{eq:attention_full_reduced_identity} yields the reduced alignment energy $-\tau\log Z_i$ of Eq.~\eqref{eq:attention_full_equals_reduced}, where $Z_i = \sum_j \pi_{ij}\exp(-E_{ij}/\tau)$. For a physicist the row is the Helmholtz free energy of one discrete source variable at temperature $\tau$ with reference measure $\pi_i$,
\begin{equation}
-\tau\log Z_i = \langle E_i\rangle_{\beta_i^*} - \tau S_i,
\qquad
S_i = -D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\beta_i^*\|\pi_i),
\end{equation}
and the softmax is its Gibbs state by the Gibbs variational principle. The unit-temperature row~\eqref{eq:mixture_energy_entropy} is a different function: evaluated at the same tempered weights it gives $(1-1/\tau)\langle E_i\rangle_{\beta_i^*}-\log Z_i$, so the two rows agree only at $\tau=1$. Holding the model sections fixed and setting aside the model-channel alignment terms (which carry an analogous structure in $s_i$ and $\gamma_{ij}$), the reduced free energy at point $c$ is
\begin{equation}
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{red}}[\{q_i\}]
&=\sum_i D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i\|p_i)
-\tau\sum_i \log Z_i
-\sum_i \mathbb{E}_{q_i\otimes s_i}[\log p(o_i\mid k_i,m_i)],
\end{aligned}
}
\label{eq:free_energy_reduced}
\end{equation}
with $E_{ij} = D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| \Omega_{ij} q_j)$ and $Z_i = \sum_j \pi_{ij} \exp(-E_{ij}/\tau)$; the presence weights $\chi_i$ are suppressed for readability in the display and restored in the gradient below.

The envelope theorem~\cite{MilgromSegal2002} guarantees that the gradient of $\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{red}}$ contains only explicit derivatives of the complete functional evaluated at the optimal attention weights, with the sector receiving the derivative matching the variable being varied. With each row weighted by its receiver presence as in Eq.~\eqref{eq:free_energy_functional_final}, for belief variables $x_i \in \{\mu_i,\Sigma_i\}$ the model-channel terms do not contribute unless the fibers are explicitly tied, and
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
\nabla_{x_i}\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{red}}
&= \chi_i(c)\nabla_{x_i}D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i\|p_i)
+ \chi_i(c)\sum_j \beta^*_{ij}(c)\nabla_{x_i}E_{ij}
+ \sum_\ell \chi_\ell(c)\beta^*_{\ell i}(c)\nabla_{x_i}E_{\ell i} \\
&\quad - \chi_i(c)\nabla_{x_i}\mathbb{E}_{q_i\otimes s_i}[\log p(o_i\mid k_i,m_i)],
\end{aligned}
\label{eq:envelope_gradient_belief}
\end{equation}
and for model-fiber variables $y_i \in \{\mu^s_i,\Sigma^s_i\}$,
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
\nabla_{y_i}\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{red}}
&= \lambda_h\chi_i(c)\nabla_{y_i}D_{\mathrm{KL}}(s_i\|r_i)
+ \chi_i(c)\sum_j \gamma^*_{ij}(c)\nabla_{y_i}\tilde E_{ij}
+ \sum_\ell \chi_\ell(c)\gamma^*_{\ell i}(c)\nabla_{y_i}\tilde E_{\ell i} \\
&\quad - \chi_i(c)\nabla_{y_i}\mathbb{E}_{q_i\otimes s_i}[\log p(o_i\mid k_i,m_i)].
\end{aligned}
\label{eq:envelope_gradient_model}
\end{equation}
For shared frame variables $\phi_i$, both channels contribute only when the same frame field induces both $\Omega_{ij}$ and $\tilde{\Omega}_{ij}$; with independent frame fields the belief-channel derivative is taken with respect to $\phi_i$ and the model-channel derivative with respect to $\tilde{\phi}_i$. The envelope reduction applies to the adaptive functional~\eqref{eq:free_energy_adaptive} in the same way it applies to~\eqref{eq:free_energy_functional_final}, with the inner optimum over $\alpha_i$ absorbed by the closed form~\eqref{eq:state_dependent_alpha}, so $\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{red}}$ is available for gradient computation without an explicit Lagrange multiplier on $\alpha_i$.

The stationary formula predicts that $\beta_{ij}(c)$ concentrates where the transported divergence $D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i(c)\|\Omega_{ij}(c)[q_j(c)])$ is small relative to competing senders under the same prior and temperature. This is a prediction of the formula, not an observed spatial attention field.

\subsection{Interpretation of the Free Energy Terms}

Two readings of Eq.~\eqref{eq:free_energy_functional_final} are load-bearing. First, the prior $p_i$ that the self-consistency term anchors against is the transported parent law of Eq.~\eqref{eq:cross_scale_shadow}, $p_i^{(\ell)} = \Omega_{iI}[q_I^{(\ell+1)}]$, not an independent learned object, and it is exact only in the one-episode-lagged reading, as the predictive prior of an episode-indexed joint (Section~\ref{sec:cross_scale_shadows};~\cite[Ch.~12]{DennisGaugeVFE2026}); where the energy evaluates it at the current parent law, that is the energy-level simplification of Table~\ref{tab:claim_status} (status S) and not a prior of one joint. The agent's own model section $s_i$ is regulated separately by its hyper-prior $r_i$ on the model fiber through $D_{\mathrm{KL}}(s_i\|r_i)$, so $s_i$ does not act through $p_i$ at the same level. Second, the per-agent priors $p_i$ are not coupled across agents in this formulation; inter-agent agreement at the slow level is carried by the model coupling on $s_i$. The observation term anchors the system to its records and breaks the global belief-rotation symmetry explicitly (Section~\ref{sec:symmetry_breaking}): without it, the population could rotate rigidly into a configuration that is self-consistent but agrees with no record.

\subsection{Recasting External Observations as Environmental-Agent Couplings}

The functional as presented includes observation terms $-\mathbb{E}_{q_i\otimes s_i}[\log p(o_i(c)\mid k_i,m_i)]$, where $o_i$ is a record of external data. This form connects to standard active inference~\cite{Friston2010, parr2022active} but introduces an asymmetry: agents are internal to the framework while observations come from outside. At the level of kernels the asymmetry can be removed at no cost. Every normalized observation kernel is the message of an operational environment node, exactly for the joint law of latent state and record, and conversely every environment state with a message policy yields a normalized kernel; this presentation keeps the conditioning on the record and is not exact for a variational problem over the enlarged state~\cite[Ch.~5]{DennisGaugeVFE2026} (the observation-interaction kernel equivalence theorem). This section gives the Gaussian counterpart inside the consensus functional: observation terms with Gaussian linear sensors are represented by couplings to environmental agents, exactly at the level of the mean gradient and exactly for the full belief flow after one substitution.

\subsubsection{Environmental Agents}

We replace each observation by an environmental agent $e_k \in \mathcal{E}$ whose belief $q_{e_k}(c)$ is concentrated around the state encoded by observation $o_k$, coupled to agent $i$ through the standard information-theoretic term $\sum_{k \in \mathcal{E}} \beta_{i,e_k}  D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i(c) \| \Omega_{i,e_k}[q_{e_k}(c)])$. When environmental agents have sharp beliefs, the coupling drives agent $i$'s belief mean toward the encoded values exactly as observation terms would; the covariance channel differs by the entropy term derived below. Environmental agents enter as a boundary class of constituent agents whose dynamics are fixed by construction ($q_{e_k} = p_{e_k}$, with $\beta_{i,e_k} = 1$ a fixed coupling outside the row-normalized simplex $\sum_j \beta_{ij} = 1$); they carry sensory information and do not minimize $\mathcal{F}$ themselves. The asymmetry between agent and environment is therefore relocated to the choice of which agents are dynamic and which are boundary; it is not removed.

\subsubsection{Formal Equivalence}

\begin{proposition}[Gaussian-sensor equivalence]\label{prop:gaussian_sensor_equivalence}
Consider observation terms whose likelihood does not depend on the model parameter $m_i$, so that the $s_i$-expectation is trivial, with Gaussian linear likelihood $o_k = H k + w_k$ with $w_k \sim \mathcal{N}(0, R)$, $R \succ 0$, and $\Lambda_o := H^\top R^{-1} H \succ 0$, entering
\begin{equation}
\mathcal{F}_{\text{obs}} = \mathcal{F}_{\text{internal}}[\{q_i\}] - \sum_{i,k} \int \chi_{ik}(c)  \mathbb{E}_{q_i(c)}[\log p(o_k\mid k_i)]  dc .
\end{equation}
Set $m_k := \Lambda_o^{-1} H^\top R^{-1} o_k$ and define environmental agents $q_{e_k} = p_{e_k} = \mathcal{N}(m_k, \Sigma_o)$ with $\Sigma_o := \Lambda_o^{-1}$, couplings $\beta_{i,e_k} = 1$, and links $\Omega_{i,e_k} = I$, entering
\begin{equation}
\mathcal{F}_{\text{agent}} = \mathcal{F}_{\text{internal}}[\{q_i\}] + \sum_{i,k} \int \chi_{ik}(c)  \beta_{i,e_k}(c)  D_{\mathrm{KL}}\big(q_i(c) \| \Omega_{i,e_k}[q_{e_k}(c)]\big)  dc .
\end{equation}
Then for Gaussian beliefs $q_i = \mathcal{N}(\mu_i, \Sigma_i)$: (i) $\partial_{\mu_i}\mathcal{F}_{\text{obs}} = \partial_{\mu_i}\mathcal{F}_{\text{agent}}$; (ii) at each point and for each coupled environmental agent, the covariance gradients differ by $-\tfrac{1}{2}\Sigma_i^{-1}$; (iii) replacing each environmental relative entropy by the cross-entropy $-\mathbb{E}_{q_i}[\log q_{e_k}]$ makes $\mathcal{F}_{\text{agent}} - \mathcal{F}_{\text{obs}}$ independent of $\{q_i\}$, so the two belief flows coincide. The sensor covariance $\Sigma_o$ is fixed by $(H, R)$ and is not a free parameter.
\end{proposition}

\noindent\emph{Proof.} Completing the square, $-\log p(o_k\mid k) = \tfrac{1}{2}(k - m_k)^\top \Lambda_o (k - m_k) + c_k$ with $c_k$ independent of $k$, so
\begin{equation}
-\mathbb{E}_{q_i}[\log p(o_k\mid k_i)] = \tfrac{1}{2}\big[(\mu_i - m_k)^\top \Lambda_o (\mu_i - m_k) + \mathrm{tr}(\Lambda_o \Sigma_i)\big] + c_k,
\end{equation}
while for Gaussian $q_i$ in the sensor's frame
\begin{equation}
D_{\mathrm{KL}}\big(q_i \| q_{e_k}\big) = \tfrac{1}{2}\big[(\mu_i - m_k)^\top \Lambda_o (\mu_i - m_k) + \mathrm{tr}(\Lambda_o \Sigma_i) + \log\tfrac{|\Sigma_o|}{|\Sigma_i|} - K_q\big].
\end{equation}
Both have mean gradient $\Lambda_o(\mu_i - m_k)$, which is (i). Differentiating with respect to $\Sigma_i$ gives
\begin{equation}
\partial_{\Sigma_i}D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| q_{e_k}) = \tfrac{1}{2}\big(\Lambda_o - \Sigma_i^{-1}\big),
\end{equation}
against $\partial_{\Sigma_i}[-\mathbb{E}_{q_i}\log p(o_k \mid k_i)] = \tfrac{1}{2}\Lambda_o$, which is (ii); the discrepancy comes from the entropy term $-\tfrac{1}{2}\log|\Sigma_i|$ inside the relative entropy. The cross-entropy $-\mathbb{E}_{q_i}[\log q_{e_k}] = \tfrac{1}{2}[(\mu_i - m_k)^\top \Lambda_o (\mu_i - m_k) + \mathrm{tr}(\Lambda_o \Sigma_i)] + \tfrac{1}{2}\log|2\pi\Sigma_o|$ differs from the expected negative log-likelihood by a constant independent of $q_i$, which is (iii). $\square$

The scope of the proposition is sharp. If $\operatorname{rank} H < K_q$, the induced precision $H^\top R^{-1}H$ is singular, $q_{e_k}$ is not a normalized density, and the relative entropy is undefined. For a nonlinear or non-Gaussian likelihood, such as a readout $o = \tanh(2k) + w$, the expected mean gradient of the observation term is in general not affine in $\mu_i$, whereas the mean gradient $\Lambda_o(\mu_i - m)$ of every Gaussian environmental coupling is affine, so no Gaussian environmental agent reproduces it. Outside the Gaussian linear case the exact statement is the kernel-level equivalence cited above.

\textbf{Construction.} Since $q_i(c)$ is a density over the latent fiber $\mathbb{R}^{K_q}$, the environmental agent is specified in fiber variables, and $m_k \in \mathbb{R}^{K_q}$ is the fiber encoding of $o_k$ under the sensor model (for identity readout, $m_k = o_k$). The active-inference special case $\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{state}} \cong \mathcal{C}$ with $K_q = \dim\mathcal{C}$ and $m_k = c_k$ recovers the reading in which beliefs range over noumenal locations directly, whereas any configuration with $K_q \ne \dim\mathcal{C}$ lies outside that case. A Dirac choice $q_{e_k}(c) = \delta(k - m_k)$ gives an infinite relative entropy, since a belief with a density is not absolutely continuous with respect to a point mass, so environmental agents carry finite sensory precision and the standard active-inference identification is recovered in the high-precision limit $\Sigma_o \to 0$. For each observation $o_k$ at noumenal location $c_k$ the support $\chi_{e_k}$ is concentrated near $c_k$. The equality $p_{e_k} = q_{e_k}$ is stipulated as a boundary condition and is not derived through the cross-scale relation of Eq.~\eqref{eq:cross_scale_shadow}; environmental agents do not inherit a prior from a meta-agent, and we do not specify primitive model or hyper-prior sections for them, so the environmental sub-class is a degenerate special case of the agent definition (Section~\ref{sec:agent_definition}). The choice $\Omega_{i,e_k} = I$ declares that the sensor's frame coincides with the receiving agent's frame, that is, that the observation kernel is written in agent $i$'s own coordinates. It is a choice of coordinates for a datum that is then held fixed, and it changes no physical quantity; Section~\ref{sec:symmetry_breaking} shows that data held fixed in this way act as external fields.

The cross-entropy form of part (iii),
\begin{equation}
\mathcal{F}_{\text{agent}}^{\mathrm{xent}}(q_i, q_{e_k}) := -\mathbb{E}_{q_i}[\log q_{e_k}] = D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| q_{e_k}) + H(q_i),
\label{eq:xent_resolution}
\end{equation}
recovers the negative log-likelihood up to a constant when $q_{e_k}$ is the Gaussian sensor density, and equals the relative entropy plus the agent's entropy $H(q_i) = \tfrac{1}{2}\log|2\pi e \Sigma_i|$. It is structurally identical to the observation operator $-\mathbb{E}_{q_i\otimes s_i}[\log p(o_i(c)\mid k_i, m_i)]$ of the canonical functional~\eqref{eq:free_energy_functional_final}~\cite{Friston2010, parr2022active}, so the substitution identifies the environmental coupling with the existing observation slot. Under this substitution every term in the free energy is written as an agent-to-agent or agent-to-environmental-agent coupling, with no explicit observation symbol $o$. The dependence on external information is not eliminated, since $q_{e_k}$ carries the location $c_k$ in exactly the role $o_k$ played, and Section~\ref{sec:symmetry_breaking} identifies environmental agents as external fields that break the global belief-rotation symmetry. The Markov-blanket-of-sensors picture, in which sensory blankets are themselves composed of sensory blankets down to single bits, is a heuristic composition with the cross-scale machinery of Eq.~\eqref{eq:cross_scale_shadow}; an equation-level cross-scale construction with $e_k$ carrying a level label $\ell' < \ell$ is not given here.

Like physical action principles, the consensus functional is a scalar integrated over a manifold, invariant under the redundancies of Section~\ref{sec:gauge_covariance_principle}, and rendered stationary by the dynamics. The analogy holds at the level of variational stationarity only. The dynamics here is a first-order natural-gradient flow, not the second-order Euler-Lagrange system of classical mechanics, general relativity, or Yang-Mills theory~\cite{Yang1954}, and $\mathcal{F}$ is dimensionless in nats while a physical action carries units of action.

\subsection{Explicit Breaking of the Global Belief-Rotation Symmetry}
\label{sec:symmetry_breaking}

The functional has two global invariances at fixed structural data, and observations break only one of them.
