In the trichotomy of Section~\ref{sec:gauge_covariance_principle}~\cite[Ch.~2]{DennisGaugeVFE2026}, per-agent frame changes $U_i \mapsto g_i U_i$ with every datum of agent $i$ re-expressed are passive re-expressions, a common right translation $U_i \mapsto U_i g$ is a change of reference, and the active group at one base point is a single global copy of $G$.

The right translation $U_i\mapsto U_i g$ leaves every transport $\Omega_{ij}=U_iU_j^{-1}$, every belief, every model section, and every transported prior unchanged. The observation term contains no frame, so it is invariant as well, and no term of the functional breaks this action: configurations related by it are the same physical state, and the action is a labeling redundancy. It can be broken only by exempting some frame from the transformation, for instance the frame $U_{e_k}$ of an environmental agent, which then becomes a fixed background field; the links $\Omega_{i,e_k}=U_iU_{e_k}^{-1}$ change under $U_i\mapsto U_ig$ when $U_{e_k}$ is held fixed.

The symmetry that observations do break is the diagonal left action
\begin{equation}
U_i\mapsto hU_i,\qquad
q_i\mapsto h_\# q_i,\qquad
p_i\mapsto h_\# p_i,\qquad
s_i\mapsto h_\# s_i,\qquad
r_i\mapsto h_\# r_i,
\qquad h\in G,
\end{equation}
with one $h$ for all agents and the model-channel pushforwards taken through $\rho_{\mathrm{model}}$. It maps $\Omega_{ij}\mapsto h\Omega_{ij}h^{-1}$, so by the invariance of relative entropy under a common pushforward (Theorem~\ref{thm:glk_invariance}) every self, alignment, and attention term is unchanged, and the observation-free functional is invariant. The action rotates the collective belief configuration rigidly and moves the consensus belief, so configurations related by it are physically distinct states that the observation-free functional cannot tell apart. A fixed observation kernel $p(o\mid k)$, written in agent $i$'s coordinates and not transformed with the agents, is not invariant, since $\mathbb{E}_{h_\# q_i}[\log p(o\mid k)]\neq\mathbb{E}_{q_i}[\log p(o\mid k)]$ in general. It therefore breaks this global symmetry explicitly, in the sense of an external field in statistical mechanics. The same holds for every other datum held fixed while the agents are rotated: the frame and belief of an environmental agent, and a declared root prior at the top of the hierarchy that is not transformed. If the observation kernel were instead re-expressed along with the agents, the transformation would reduce to a passive change of coordinates and nothing would be broken; the physical content of the breaking is the relation of the population to data held fixed. This also separates explicit breaking from gauge fixing: setting $\Omega_{i,e_k}=I$ in the environmental-agent construction chooses coordinates in which the sensor's frame coincides with agent $i$'s frame and changes no physical quantity.

These statements concern the functional. They do not establish that numerical trajectories converge to a common orbit of the belief-rotation symmetry. Orbit membership must be tested by an orbit-sensitive residual, such as the minimum pairwise Procrustes residual over a declared compact subgroup, since equality of belief norms does not suffice. No spontaneous-symmetry-breaking or Goldstone claim follows without an internal selection mechanism and an analysis of the fluctuation spectrum, and whether source-induced specialization yields stable multi-child clusters is left to the prospective validation of Section~\ref{sec:participatory}.

\section{Two-Timescale Dynamics and the Adiabatic Hypothesis}\label{sec:timescales}

\subsection{An Imposed Time-Scale Separation}

Physical systems often exhibit widely separated timescales, and such separation licenses hierarchical descriptions in which fast variables equilibrate on the frozen background of slow ones. We adopt this structure for the consensus functional as a declaration: the functional does not choose the fast-slow split, the rate ratio, or the block mobilities~\cite[Ch.~8]{DennisGaugeVFE2026}. Following the typing of that chapter, the fast block consists of the beliefs $\{q_i(c)\}$, which respond to observations, together with the attention rows held at their Gibbs optimum; the slow block consists of the model sections $\{s_i(c)\}$ and the prior parameters. The priors $\{p_i(c)\}$ and hyper-priors $\{r_i(c)\}$ are prior parameters in this sense: in the lagged reading of Section~\ref{sec:cross_scale_shadows} they are fixed within an episode and updated between episodes from the parent laws of the previous episode, and they have no dynamics of their own beyond that schedule. At the top level the hyper-prior is a fixed boundary condition, the declared root of the hierarchy (Section~\ref{sec:ouroboros}). At one base point the frames are not a separate block (Section~\ref{sec:frame_evolution}).

\subsection{Fast Subsystem: Belief Dynamics}

On the fast timescale, agents update beliefs $q_i$ to explain new observations while the priors $p_i$, the model sections, and the frames are held fixed. The relevant free energy is
\begin{align}
\mathcal{F}_{\text{fast}}[\{q_i\}] = {}& \sum_i \int \chi_i(c)  D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i(c) \| p_i(c))  dc \nonumber\\
&+ \sum_{ij} \int \chi_i(c)\beta_{ij}(c)  D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i(c) \| \Omega_{ij}[q_j(c)])  dc \nonumber\\
&- \sum_i \int \chi_i(c)  \mathbb{E}_{q_i\otimes s_i}[\log p(o_i(c) \mid k_i, m_i)]  dc,
\end{align}
where the attention weights $\beta_{ij}$ are held at the softmax optimum $\beta^*_{ij}$ of Eq.~\eqref{eq:beta_optimal} during the belief updates, each row is weighted by its receiver presence $\chi_i$, and sender presence is carried inside the attention prior $\tilde{\pi}_{ij}$ as in Section~\ref{sec:complete_free_energy}. Under this block-coordinate convention the attention-entropy block $\tau\beta_{ij}\log(\beta_{ij}/\tilde{\pi}_{ij})$ of the canonical functional~\eqref{eq:free_energy_functional_final} is independent of $q_i$ and is omitted from the displayed scalar. The omission leaves the belief gradient unchanged, so the fast flow is block-coordinate descent of Eq.~\eqref{eq:free_energy_functional_final} in $q$ at fixed $\beta = \beta^*$, which by the envelope theorem (Section~\ref{sec:envelope_theorem}) coincides with the gradient of the reduced free energy $\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{red}}$. The displayed $\mathcal{F}_{\text{fast}}$ is therefore gradient-equivalent bookkeeping at fixed $\beta^*$, not the entropy-suppressed surrogate of Section~\ref{sec:complete_free_energy}, whose $\beta^*(q)$ dependence is differentiated through. We omit explicit base-space integrals in the equations below for clarity, but they are understood to be present.

Natural-gradient descent on beliefs follows $\partial q_i/\partial t = -\eta_q  \tilde{\nabla}_{q_i} \mathcal{F}_{\text{fast}}$ (we write $t$ for the flow time throughout this section, reserving $\tau$ for the attention temperature), where $\tilde{\nabla}_{q_i} = g_{\mathcal{B}}^{-1} \nabla_{q_i}$ is the natural gradient with respect to the Fisher-Rao metric $g_{\mathcal{B}}$ on $\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{state}}$. For Gaussian beliefs $q_i = \mathcal{N}(\mu_i, \Sigma_i)$ the Fisher metric in the moment parameterization $(\mu_i, \Sigma_i)$ is block-diagonal with $g_{\mu\mu} = \Sigma_i^{-1}$ on the mean block and $g_{\Sigma\Sigma}[V, W] = \tfrac{1}{2}\mathrm{tr}(\Sigma_i^{-1} V \Sigma_i^{-1} W)$ on the covariance block~\cite{Amari2016}. The block rate $\eta_q$ is a mobility: it fixes the unit of flow time, and only ratios of block rates enter the timescale argument below. This subsystem performs variational inference, with agents adjusting beliefs to explain observations while respecting their current priors and coordinating with nearby agents.

\subsection{Slow Subsystem: Model Learning}

On longer timescales, agents update their model sections $s_i$ to reflect long-term statistical structure, regularized by the model hyper-priors $r_i$, coupled to neighbors through the model-alignment term, and tied to the records through the jointly typed observation term:
\begin{equation}
\mathcal{F}_{\text{slow}}[\{s_i\}] = \lambda_h \sum_i \int \chi_i(c)  D_{\mathrm{KL}}(s_i(c) \| r_i(c))  dc
+ \sum_{ij} \int \chi_i(c)\gamma_{ij}(c)  D_{\mathrm{KL}}(s_i(c) \| \tilde{\Omega}_{ij}[s_j(c)])  dc
- \sum_i \int \chi_i(c)  \mathbb{E}_{q_i\otimes s_i}[\log p(o_i(c) \mid k_i, m_i)]  dc,
\end{equation}
where $r_i$ are the model hyper-priors, supplied by the parent's model section at the previous episode (Section~\ref{sec:cross_scale_shadows}) and by the fixed root at the top level. The same block-coordinate and absorbed-prior conventions apply: sender presence is carried inside $\tilde{\pi}^{(s)}_{ij}$ (Section~\ref{sec:complete_free_energy}), and $\gamma_{ij}$ is held at its softmax optimum $\gamma^*_{ij}$ during the $s$-updates, so the omitted entropy block $\tau_\gamma\gamma_{ij}\log(\gamma_{ij}/\tilde{\pi}^{(s)}_{ij})$ is independent of $s_i$ and the displayed scalar is gradient-equivalent to Eq.~\eqref{eq:free_energy_functional_final} in $s$ at fixed $\gamma = \gamma^*$. Natural-gradient descent on models follows $\partial s_i/\partial t = -\eta_s  \tilde{\nabla}_{s_i} \mathcal{F}_{\text{slow}}$ with block rate $\eta_s = \epsilon\eta_q$, $\epsilon \ll 1$, on which agents adjust their expectations about how the world works and move toward shared models.

Within an episode the fast subsystem sees $p_i$ and $r_i$ as constants. Whether an episode is long enough for the fast block to relax is a hypothesis on the schedule and is not derived. A same-time assignment, in which $p_i$ follows the current parent belief during the fast updates, is not adopted for the dynamics, because for coherent clusters the parent belief moves at a rate comparable to that of its constituents (Proposition~\ref{prop:meta_clock_slowdown}), so that assignment would close the loop $q_i \to q_I \to p_i \to q_i$ on the fast timescale and remove the separation exactly when meta-agents exist.

\subsection{Gauge Frames at One Base Point}
\label{sec:frame_evolution}

Frames $\phi_i(c)$ may also be evolved to lower the functional, but at one base point they are not an independent slow variable. In Regime I every alignment, attention, and transported-prior term depends on the pair $(q_i, U_i)$ only through the frame-free law point $(U_i^{-1})_\# q_i$, by the invariance of relative entropy under a common pushforward (Theorem~\ref{thm:glk_invariance}). Evolving a frame at fixed coordinate belief therefore moves the law point along its $G$-orbit, and the frame flow acts as an additional anisotropic mobility on beliefs. Frame content at a point is relative to data held fixed. The observation kernel reads $q_i$ in agent $i$'s own coordinates, so $U_i$ is the link between the agent's observation leg and its interaction legs, and evolving frames amounts to learning that link; the record kernels of~\cite[Ch.~5]{DennisGaugeVFE2026} give this its typing, since a record may carry its own frame and a link on each of its legs. For independent links on the interaction graph (Regime II), the links are genuine interaction variables, and whether they are learned, and on which schedule, is part of the declaration. In either case the frame rate $\eta_\phi$ is a block mobility, and a rate ratio between blocks is an anisotropic mobility that can change the selected orbit, not only the speed along it~\cite[Chs.~7 and~8]{DennisGaugeVFE2026}. Its value is a modeling choice with consequences.

When frames are evolved, no frame regularizer is included: they move only through the terms of $\mathcal{F}$ in which they appear, under the metric-preconditioned group flow $\dot U_i = -\eta_\phi U_i \xi_i$, where $\xi_i$ is the left-trivialized Lie-algebra velocity of Eq.~\eqref{eq:gauge_natural_gradient_def}. A gradient penalty $\lambda_\phi \sum_i \int \|\nabla \phi_i(c)\|^2  dc$ would couple frames at different base points; it belongs to base-transport theory and is deferred~\cite{DennisGaugeVFESuppB2026}. The chart form $\partial\phi_i/\partial t = -\eta_\phi v_{\phi_i}$ in the parameterization $U_i = \exp(\phi_i)$ agrees with the group flow to first order in the step, as shown below.

\subsection{Timescale Separation and the Adiabatic Hypothesis}
\label{sec:adiabatic_hierarchy}

We declare the rate ratio $\eta_s/\eta_q = \epsilon$ with $\epsilon \ll 1$ between the slow and fast blocks. Write $f$ for the fast coordinates, $s$ for the slow ones, and $\mathsf G_{\mathrm f}$, $\mathsf G_{\mathrm s}$ for their block metrics. For every $\epsilon > 0$ the rate-separated natural gradient, $\epsilon\dot f = -\mathsf G_{\mathrm f}^{-1}\partial_f\mathcal{F}$ and $\dot s = -\mathsf G_{\mathrm s}^{-1}\partial_s\mathcal{F}$ in flow time measured in slow units, decreases the functional. On the graph $\mathcal{S}^\star = \{(f^\star(s), s)\}$ of a branch of conditional minimizers its fast component vanishes and its slow component is the natural gradient of the reduced functional $\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{red}}(s) = \min_f \mathcal{F}(f, s)$, by the envelope identity $d\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{red}} = \partial_s\mathcal{F}(f^\star(s), s)$~\cite[Ch.~8]{DennisGaugeVFE2026} (the envelope lemma and the rate-separated natural-gradient proposition). This holds for every $\epsilon$, but the orbit can depend on $\epsilon$, and it does not by itself say that trajectories starting off $\mathcal{S}^\star$ approach it.

That tracking statement is the adiabatic reduction proper, and it requires Tikhonov's hypotheses: a compact slow region $\mathcal{K}$ on which a $C^1$ branch of conditional minimizers satisfies the uniform fast-curvature bound $\partial^2_{ff}\mathcal{F}(f^\star(s), s) \succeq c_1 \mathsf G_{\mathrm f}$ for some $c_1 > 0$, a reduced slow orbit that stays in the interior of $\mathcal{K}$, and a fast initial state in the basin of the frozen fast flow. Under these hypotheses the fast variables track the conditional minimizer as $\epsilon \to 0$~\cite{Tikhonov1952}, and for smooth data a locally invariant slow manifold exists $O(\epsilon)$-close to $\mathcal{S}^\star$~\cite{fenichel1979geometric}. The effective small parameter is the fastest slow relaxation rate divided by the slowest fast relaxation rate, that is, the block rates multiplied by the spectra of $\mathsf G^{-1}\partial^2\mathcal{F}$ in each block, and not the bare ratio $\eta_s/\eta_q$.

For the recognition families of this paper these hypotheses are not established, and the Theory declares the reduction open at one base point~\cite[Ch.~8]{DennisGaugeVFE2026} (the open problem on slow-manifold reduction). The uniform fast-curvature bound is certified only in restricted regimes: zero within-scale coupling, where the cross-scale joint is a proper Gaussian with a unique optimum, and frozen isotropic couplings with $\mathrm{SO}(N)$ transport, where the mean update is a contraction; no interior fixed point is established for the fully coupled, state-dependent $\mathrm{GL}^+(K_q)$ system (Appendix~\ref{app:mf_fixedpoint_status}). The bound also fails generically where attention switches. After the rows are optimized, the curvature of the reduced row $-\tau\log Z_i$ is $\sum_j\beta^*_{ij}\nabla^2E_{ij} - \tau^{-1}\operatorname{Cov}_{\beta_i^*}(\nabla E_i)$, and the covariance correction can make it indefinite even when every frozen-weight curvature is positive~\cite[Ch.~6]{DennisGaugeVFE2026} (the attention-reduced potential proposition). A one-agent example exhibits the resulting fold. With a slow prior mean $m$, anchor strength $\alpha = 0.2$, two unit-precision neighbors at $x = \pm 1$, and $\tau = 0.05$, the reduced energy
\begin{equation}
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{red}}(\mu; m) = \frac{\alpha}{2}(\mu - m)^2 - \tau\log\sum_{j=\pm1}\exp\Big[-\frac{(\mu - x_j)^2}{2\tau}\Big]
\end{equation}
has two fast minima and a saddle for moderate $m$, and at $m \approx 4.22$ the left minimum and the saddle merge in a saddle-node, so the fast state must jump. At such folds the reduction must be replaced by jump or relaxation-oscillation analysis. Outside the restricted regimes above the adiabatic hierarchy is therefore a conjecture (status C in Table~\ref{tab:claim_status}); where the hypotheses hold, it supports a perturbative expansion in powers of $\epsilon$. The Born-Oppenheimer separation of electronic and nuclear motion~\cite{Born1927} is an analogy for the slaving of fast to slow variables, with a different mechanism (slaving to instantaneous eigenstates).

\subsection{Fisher Natural Gradient on Beliefs and Metric-Preconditioned Frame Retraction}

The system evolves by Fisher-Rao natural-gradient flow on the Gaussian belief sector and, when frames are evolved, by a metric-preconditioned group retraction on the frame sector. On the product manifold $\mathcal{M} = (\mathbb{R}^{K_q} \times \mathbb{S}^+_{K_q} \times G)^N$, the block-Fisher form for Gaussian beliefs (Appendix~\ref{app:fisher_gaussian}) gives $\tilde\nabla_\mu\mathcal{F} = \Sigma\nabla_\mu\mathcal{F}$ and $\tilde\nabla_\Sigma\mathcal{F} = 2\Sigma(\nabla_\Sigma\mathcal{F})\Sigma$, whose sandwich keeps covariance updates within $\mathbb{S}^+_{K_q}$ under an affine-invariant retraction. For the group sector write $U_i = \exp(\phi_i)$ and let $\xi_i \in \mathfrak{g}$ denote the left-trivialized Lie-algebra velocity consumed by the group equation. The local flow is
\begin{align}
\frac{d\mu_i}{dt} &= -\eta_\mu \tilde{\nabla}_\mu \mathcal{F}, \\
\frac{d\Sigma_i}{dt} &= -2\eta_\Sigma \Sigma_i (\nabla_\Sigma \mathcal{F}) \Sigma_i, \\
\frac{dU_i}{dt} &= -\eta_\phi U_i \xi_i,
\label{eq:gauge_natural_gradient}
\end{align}
with the pullback-preconditioned chart vector and its corresponding group velocity defined by
\begin{equation}
v_{\phi_i} := \mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{pull}}^{-1}(\phi_i) \nabla_{\phi_i} \mathcal{F},
\qquad
\xi_i := \mathrm{dexp}_{\phi_i}^{L}(v_{\phi_i}),
\label{eq:gauge_natural_gradient_def}
\end{equation}
where $\mathrm{dexp}_{\phi}^{L}(v) = \exp(-\phi)D\exp_{\phi}[v]$ is the left-trivialized differential of the exponential map, pairing with the right-multiplication retraction of Eq.~\eqref{eq:gauge_group_retraction}. With separate rates $\eta_\mu \neq \eta_\Sigma$ the belief flow is the natural gradient of the rate-weighted metric $G_\eta := (\Sigma_i^{-1}/\eta_\mu) \oplus (g_{\Sigma\Sigma}/\eta_\Sigma)$ and coincides with a single-rate Fisher natural gradient only when $\eta_\mu = \eta_\Sigma$. Identities that assume one scalar rate in the Fisher metric, such as the clock-dissipation identity of Section~\ref{sec:time_info}, hold exactly in the rate-weighted metric and block by block, and in the Fisher metric itself only at a single rate.

A chart gradient $\nabla_{\phi_i}\mathcal{F}$, or even the chart vector $v_{\phi_i}$, is not inserted directly into a group retraction unless it has been converted to the trivialized velocity $\xi_i$. On a compact semisimple Lie algebra one may use the negative Killing form, which is bi-invariant and positive-definite, to precondition the left-trivialized gradient directly. For a compact algebra with central or abelian factors, the Killing form is degenerate on those directions and must be supplemented by a separately chosen positive Ad-invariant inner product, such as the negative trace form in the defining matrix representation of $\mathfrak{so}(2)$. For non-compact $G$ the Killing form is indefinite, and $\mathrm{GL}^+(K_q)$ admits no bi-invariant Riemannian metric~\cite[Lemma~7.5]{Milnor1976}, so the positive-definite substitute used in exponential coordinates is the position-dependent pullback of the ambient flat Frobenius metric on $\mathrm{GL}^+(K_q)$ through the exponential map,
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{pull},ab}(\phi)
&= \langle D\exp_\phi[T_a], D\exp_\phi[T_b] \rangle_F \\
&= \mathrm{tr}\left(
\Psi_R(\mathrm{ad}_\phi)(T_a)^\top
\Psi_R(\mathrm{ad}_\phi)(T_b)
\exp(\phi)\exp(\phi)^\top
\right), \\
\Psi_R(z) &= \frac{e^{z} - 1}{z},
\end{aligned}
\label{eq:pullback_metric}
\end{equation}
where $\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_F$ is the ambient Frobenius inner product and $D\exp_\phi[H] = \Psi_R(\mathrm{ad}_\phi)(H)\exp(\phi)$ is the right-trivialized differential of the exponential map, so the weight $\exp(\phi)\exp(\phi)^\top = UU^\top$ is the right $\exp(\phi)$ factor of that differential. This form is positive definite exactly where $\mathrm{dexp}^{L}_\phi$ is regular. As the pullback of a flat metric by a local diffeomorphism it is flat, and it is invariant under neither left nor right translations by non-orthogonal elements of $G$. By contrast, for variations $\delta U=U\xi$, the quantity $U^\top\nabla_U\mathcal{F}$ is the left-trivialized, or body, covector. Identifying it with a velocity through a fixed Frobenius inner product on the algebra induces a left-invariant metric on $\mathrm{GL}^+(K_q)$. That metric is also right invariant only if the inner product is Ad-invariant, which no positive inner product on $\mathfrak{gl}(K_q)$ is; a right-invariant metric by itself exists on any Lie group and gives the mirror convention below. The position-dependent ambient pullback above is a third, distinct construction.
\begin{proposition}[dexp regularity]\label{prop:dexp_regularity}
The operator $\mathrm{dexp}^{L}_\phi$ is singular exactly when $\mathrm{spec}(\mathrm{ad}_\phi)$ contains a nonzero element of $2\pi i\mathbb{Z}$; equivalently, the pullback metric of Eq.~\eqref{eq:pullback_metric} is positive definite exactly away from that spectral locus.
\end{proposition}
Clipping $\|\mathrm{ad}_\phi\|$ below $2\pi$ is a sufficient guard. On the full $\mathfrak{gl}(K_q)$ chart a skew frame of rotation angle $\theta$ has $\mathrm{ad}_\phi$-eigenvalues $0$, $\pm i\theta$, and $\pm 2i\theta$, so $\mathrm{dexp}^{L}_\phi$ is singular already at $\theta = \pi$; the familiar $2\pi$ locus applies only to the chart restricted to $\mathfrak{so}(3)$. The mirror right-trivialized velocity convention would instead pair with a left-multiplication retraction $\exp(-\eta_\phi\xi_i)U_i$; we use the left-trivialized velocity because Eq.~\eqref{eq:gauge_natural_gradient} and Eq.~\eqref{eq:gauge_group_retraction} multiply the update on the right. The discrete analog of Eq.~\eqref{eq:gauge_natural_gradient} is the group-level retraction
\begin{equation}
U_i^{t+1} = U_i^t \exp\big(-\eta_\phi \xi_i^t\big),
\label{eq:gauge_group_retraction}
\end{equation}
which keeps $U_i \in G$ exactly at every step and is the canonical group update once $\xi_i$ has been computed.

For $G = \mathrm{GL}^+(K_q)$, which is open in $\mathfrak{gl}(K_q)$, the metric of Eq.~\eqref{eq:pullback_metric} is the exact pullback of the ambient Frobenius metric and $D\exp_\phi$ is surjective wherever $\mathrm{dexp}^{L}_\phi$ is regular. The pullback-preconditioned chart vector $v_{\phi_i} = \mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{pull}}^{-1}\nabla_{\phi_i}\mathcal{F}$ therefore pushes forward to the ambient gradient, $D\exp_{\phi_i}[v_{\phi_i}] = \nabla_{U_i}\mathcal{F}$, and its left-trivialized velocity telescopes exactly to $\xi_i = \mathrm{dexp}^{L}_{\phi_i}(v_{\phi_i}) = U_i^{-1}\nabla_{U_i}\mathcal{F}$. On the full $\mathfrak{gl}(K_q)$ this preconditioning reproduces ambient Euclidean descent on $U_i$, $U_i\exp(-\eta_\phi U_i^{-1}\nabla_{U_i}\mathcal{F}) = U_i - \eta_\phi\nabla_{U_i}\mathcal{F} + O(\eta_\phi^2)$, and it carries no Fisher content. In computation $\xi_i$ is formed directly from $\nabla_{U_i}\mathcal{F}$; the $K_q^2\times K_q^2$ metric $\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{pull}}$ need never be assembled, and inverting it explicitly would square the condition number of $D\exp_\phi$. On a proper subgroup such as $\mathrm{SO}(K_q)$, $D\exp_\phi$ maps onto $T_{U_i}G$ only, and the same construction gives the tangent projection $\xi_i = U_i^{-1}P_{T_{U_i}G}\nabla_{U_i}\mathcal{F}$. The additive chart step $\phi_i^{t+1} = \phi_i^t - \eta_\phi v_{\phi_i}$ and the group step~\eqref{eq:gauge_group_retraction} agree to $O(\eta_\phi^2)$ at every $\phi_i$, by definition of $\mathrm{dexp}^{L}$; the conversion is needed only when a chart vector is inserted into the group exponential. The retraction stays in $G$ at every discrete step, but global existence of the continuous frame flow in $\mathrm{GL}^+(K_q)$ is not established. The functional is constant along the noncompact orbits of the change-of-reference redundancy, so its sublevel sets are not compact, and nothing in the ambient flow keeps $U_i$ away from singular matrices. A gauge slice, a coercive frame prior, or an invariant bounded set is required (Appendix~\ref{app:mf_fixedpoint_status}).

\paragraph{Frame-flow covariance.}
The Gaussian sector thus has an intrinsic Fisher-Rao natural gradient, while the frame sector has a coordinate- and trivialization-dependent metric preconditioning. Because $\mathrm{GL}^+(K_q)$ has no bi-invariant Riemannian metric, no single frame metric respects both the per-agent left translations and the global right translation. The ambient-pullback flow, which equals the Euclidean flow $\dot U_i = -\eta_\phi\nabla_{U_i}\mathcal{F}$, is equivariant only under orthogonal left and right translations. The left-invariant (body) flow $\dot U_i = -\eta_\phi U_iU_i^\top\nabla_{U_i}\mathcal{F}$ is equivariant under per-agent left translations with beliefs co-transformed, and under right translations only by orthogonal $g$. The right-trivialized flow $\dot U_i = -\eta_\phi(\nabla_{U_i}\mathcal{F}U_i^\top)U_i$, paired with the left-multiplication retraction, is equivariant under the change-of-reference redundancy $U_i\mapsto U_ig$, so its trajectories descend to the quotient by that redundancy, at the cost of left equivariance. Under the first two choices, gauge-equivalent initial data evolve to physically different transports, and the linearized frame relaxation rates are not invariant under $U_i\mapsto U_ig$. In a two-agent example with $K_q = 2$ and $\mathcal{F} = D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_1\|\Omega_{12}q_2)$ at a minimizer, the slowest nonzero frame rate of the ambient flow is $1.07\eta_\phi$ in one gauge and $0.023\eta_\phi$ after $g = \mathrm{diag}(10, 1)$, with $\mathcal{F}$ unchanged, whereas the right-invariant metric gives the rates $(0.93, 4.01, 6.29, 7.04)\eta_\phi$ in every gauge. Any timescale assigned to the frame block is therefore gauge invariant only with a right-invariant frame metric. The choice of frame metric is a declared option, and a covariant flow exists for either quotient. Gauge-covariant trajectory claims for the other choices require compact (Ad-invariant), abelian, or near-identity hypotheses; under them the Gaussian update transforms covariantly as $\Sigma_i(\nabla_\Sigma\mathcal{F})\Sigma_i \to \rho(g)[\Sigma_i(\nabla_\Sigma\mathcal{F})\Sigma_i]\rho(g)^\top$.

\section{Statistical Precision as Stiffness and Friction}\label{sec:mass}

The natural-gradient flow of Eq.~\eqref{eq:gauge_natural_gradient} descends the consensus free-energy functional $\mathcal{F}$ of Section~\ref{sec:free_energy}, which is therefore the potential of the agent dynamics. This section computes the second variation of $\mathcal{F}$ in the Gaussian belief sector and derives the two roles of statistical precision in that first-order dynamics (status D, Table~\ref{tab:claim_status}). The Hessian is a stiffness tensor whose blocks are built from precisions: the prior term contributes the prior precision $\bar{\Lambda}_{p_i}$, and the interaction terms contribute transported neighbor precisions. The Fisher metric, which in the mean sector is the belief precision, is the friction tensor of the flow, and no term of the dynamics carries an inertia. The only velocity-quadratic form in the dynamics is the Rayleigh dissipation function $\mathcal{R}$, which is half the free-energy dissipation rate and fixes the rate of the information clock of Section~\ref{sec:time_info} through $(d\vartheta/dt)^2 = 2\eta\mathcal{R}$ (Section~\ref{sec:stiffness_friction}). An inertial reading of precision needs a separate kinetic-metric postulate with its own second-order equation of motion, stated in Section~\ref{sec:velocity_quadratic}.

\paragraph{Stiffness, friction, and inertia.} For a damped oscillator $m\ddot{x} + \gamma\dot{x} + kx = 0$ the three coefficients are independent data: the Hessian of the potential gives the stiffness $k$, the dissipation gives the friction $\gamma$, and the kinetic energy gives the mass $m$. The Hessian of $\mathcal{F}$ derived below supplies $k$, the Fisher metric of the natural-gradient flow supplies $\gamma$, and neither $\mathcal{F}$ nor the flow supplies $m$. We hold the prior parameters $(\bar{\mu}_i, \bar{\Sigma}_i)$ fixed, the quasi-static approximation in which they evolve slowly relative to the beliefs $(\mu_i, \Sigma_i)$.

\subsection{Setup and Notation}

Each agent $i$ maintains a belief distribution $q_i = \mathcal{N}(\mu_i, \Sigma_i)$ anchored to a prior $p_i = \mathcal{N}(\bar{\mu}_i, \bar{\Sigma}_i)$ and receives observations $o_i$ through the identity readout $p(o_i \mid k_i, m_i) = \mathcal{N}(o_i; k_i, \Sigma_{o_i})$, whose likelihood does not depend on the model parameter $m_i$ and is written $p(o_i \mid k_i)$ below. We define precision matrices
\begin{align}
\Lambda_{q_i} &= \Sigma_i^{-1} & &\text{(belief precision)} \\
\bar{\Lambda}_{p_i} &= \bar{\Sigma}_i^{-1} & &\text{(prior precision)} \\
\Lambda_{o_i} &= \Sigma_{o_i}^{-1} & &\text{(observation precision)},
\end{align}
with transported quantities $\tilde{\mu}_k = \Omega_{ik}\mu_k$ and $\tilde{\Sigma}_k = \Omega_{ik}\Sigma_k\Omega_{ik}^\top$, where $\Omega_{ik} \in \mathrm{GL}(K_q)$ is the transport of the interaction term: in Regime I the frame quotient $e^{\phi_i}e^{-\phi_k}$, and in Regime II an independent link on the interaction graph with orientation reversal $\Omega_{ki} = \Omega_{ik}^{-1}$. The block formulas below use only the transports and hold in both regimes. The transported precision is the inverse of the transported covariance,
\begin{equation}
\tilde{\Lambda}_{q_k} := (\Omega_{ik}\Sigma_k\Omega_{ik}^\top)^{-1} = \Omega_{ik}^{-\top}\Lambda_{q_k}\Omega_{ik}^{-1},
\label{eq:precision_transport}
\end{equation}
which differs from the covariance transport law because precision is the inverse of covariance. For orthogonal transport $\Omega \in \mathrm{O}(K_q)$ the two laws coincide ($\Omega^{-\top} = \Omega$), and Eq.~\eqref{eq:precision_transport} reduces to $\Omega\Lambda\Omega^\top$; for general $\mathrm{GL}(K_q)$ the dual transport is essential, and we use Eq.~\eqref{eq:precision_transport} throughout. The boxed identity $\Omega_{ji}^\top \tilde{\Lambda}_{q_i}^{(j)} \Omega_{ji} = \Lambda_{q_i}$ used below holds in $\mathrm{GL}(K_q)$ because $\Omega^\top \Omega^{-\top} = I$ algebraically; the secondary simplifications $\tilde{\Lambda}_k\Omega_{ik} = \Omega_{ik}\Lambda_k$ and $\Omega_{ki}^\top\tilde\Lambda_i = \Lambda_i\Omega_{ki}^\top$ hold only under $\mathrm{O}(K_q)$; the general-linear identity is given below.

\subsection{The Extended Free Energy Functional}

The extended working functional at one base point, the fixed-$\beta^*$ block-coordinate form of Eq.~\eqref{eq:free_energy_functional_final} with explicit sensory evidence (the attention-entropy block is omitted as constant in the beliefs; this is not the entropy-suppressed surrogate of Section~\ref{sec:complete_free_energy}), is
\begin{equation}
\mathcal{F}[\{q_i\}] = \sum_i D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| p_i) + \sum_{i,k} \beta_{ik} D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| \Omega_{ik}[q_k]) - \sum_i \mathbb{E}_{q_i}[\log p(o_i \mid k_i)].
\label{eq:extended_free_energy}
\end{equation}
The three terms represent prior anchoring (deviation from the prior $p_i$, which in the hierarchy of Section~\ref{sec:cross_scale_shadows} is the one-episode-lagged predictive prior supplied by the parent level), interaction (alignment with other agents through the transports), and sensory evidence. Under the product recognition law $q_i \otimes s_i$ and the identity readout, the jointly typed observation term $-\mathbb{E}_{q_i \otimes s_i}[\log p(o_i \mid k_i, m_i)]$ of Eq.~\eqref{eq:free_energy_functional_final} reduces to the $q_i$-expectation shown, and at fixed $s_i$ the model-fiber terms are constant in $(\mu_i, \Sigma_i)$ and drop out of every variation below. Under the frozen-$\beta^*$ convention adopted for this section (the differentiation-convention paragraph below), the omitted attention-entropy term $\tau\beta_{ik}\log(\beta_{ik}/\pi_{ik})$ of the canonical functional is an additive constant in $(\mu, \Sigma)$, so the first and second variations taken under that convention coincide with the frozen-$\beta$ variations of Eq.~\eqref{eq:free_energy_functional_final}.

\subsection{Component Free Energies for Gaussians}

For $q = \mathcal{N}(\mu_q, \Sigma_q)$ and $p = \mathcal{N}(\mu_p, \Sigma_p)$, the relative entropy is given by the closed-form expression of Eq.~\eqref{eq:gaussian_kl}, applied with the substitution $(\mu_1, \Sigma_1, \mu_2, \Sigma_2) \to (\mu_q, \Sigma_q, \mu_p, \Sigma_p)$.

For the Gaussian likelihood $p(o_i \mid k_i) = \mathcal{N}(o_i; k_i, \Sigma_{o_i})$, the expected log-likelihood evaluates to
\begin{equation}
-\mathbb{E}_{q_i}[\log p(o_i \mid k_i)] = \frac{1}{2}(o_i - \mu_i)^\top \Lambda_{o_i}(o_i - \mu_i) + \frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}(\Lambda_{o_i}\Sigma_i) + \mathrm{const}.
\end{equation}

\subsection{First Variations (Gradient)}

\paragraph{Prior Term}
\begin{align}
\frac{\partial D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| p_i)}{\partial \mu_i} &= \bar{\Lambda}_{p_i}(\mu_i - \bar{\mu}_i), \\
\frac{\partial D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| p_i)}{\partial \Sigma_i} &= \frac{1}{2}(\bar{\Lambda}_{p_i} - \Lambda_{q_i}).
\end{align}

\paragraph{Interaction Term}

With respect to receiver $i$:
\begin{align}
\frac{\partial D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| \tilde{q}_k)}{\partial \mu_i} &= \tilde{\Lambda}_{q_k}(\mu_i - \tilde{\mu}_k), \\
\frac{\partial D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| \tilde{q}_k)}{\partial \Sigma_i} &= \frac{1}{2}(\tilde{\Lambda}_{q_k} - \Lambda_{q_i}).
\end{align}

With respect to sender $k$ (using the general-linear precision transport of Eq.~\eqref{eq:precision_transport}):
\begin{align}
\frac{\partial D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| \tilde{q}_k)}{\partial \mu_k} &= \Lambda_{q_k}\Omega_{ik}^{-1}(\tilde{\mu}_k - \mu_i), \\
\frac{\partial D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| \tilde{q}_k)}{\partial \Sigma_k} &= \frac{1}{2}\Omega_{ik}^\top\left[\tilde{\Lambda}_{q_k} - \tilde{\Lambda}_{q_k}\big(\Sigma_i + \tilde{d}_{ik}\tilde{d}_{ik}^\top\big)\tilde{\Lambda}_{q_k}\right]\Omega_{ik},
\end{align}
where $\tilde{d}_{ik} := \tilde{\mu}_k - \mu_i = \Omega_{ik}\mu_k - \mu_i$ is the receiver-frame mean residual. The quadratic-form contribution $-\tilde{\Lambda}_{q_k}\tilde{d}_{ik}\tilde{d}_{ik}^\top\tilde{\Lambda}_{q_k}$ originates in differentiating the Mahalanobis term $\tilde{d}_{ik}^\top \tilde{\Lambda}_{q_k}\tilde{d}_{ik}$ with respect to $\tilde{\Sigma}_k$ via $\partial \tilde{\Lambda}_{q_k}/\partial \tilde{\Sigma}_k = -\tilde{\Lambda}_{q_k}\otimes\tilde{\Lambda}_{q_k}$, then chain-ruled through $\tilde{\Sigma}_k = \Omega_{ik}\Sigma_k\Omega_{ik}^\top$. The outer $\Omega_{ik}^\top(\cdot)\Omega_{ik}$ structure follows from the same chain rule applied to the leading $\tilde{\Lambda}_{q_k}$ and $\tilde{\Lambda}_{q_k}\Sigma_i\tilde{\Lambda}_{q_k}$ contributions; for the mean gradient, the same chain rule yields the explicit inverse $\Omega_{ik}^{-1}$, which for orthogonal $\Omega$ would equal $\Omega_{ik}^\top$. The $\tilde{d}_{ik}\tilde{d}_{ik}^\top$ term vanishes at consensus ($\tilde{d}_{ik} = 0$), so the boxed stiffness blocks of Eqs.~\eqref{eq:mass_mu_diagonal}--\eqref{eq:mass_sigma_offdiagonal}, evaluated at consensus, are unchanged. Off consensus the term contributes a rank-one stiffness aligned with $\tilde{d}_{ik}$, so any second-variation analysis away from the consensus point must retain it.

\paragraph{Sensory Term}
\begin{align}
\frac{\partial}{\partial \mu_i}\left[-\mathbb{E}_{q_i}[\log p(o_i \mid k_i)]\right] &= \Lambda_{o_i}(\mu_i - o_i), \\
\frac{\partial}{\partial \Sigma_i}\left[-\mathbb{E}_{q_i}[\log p(o_i \mid k_i)]\right] &= \frac{1}{2}\Lambda_{o_i}.
\end{align}

\paragraph{Total Gradient}
\begin{equation}
\frac{\partial \mathcal{F}}{\partial \mu_i} = \bar{\Lambda}_{p_i}(\mu_i - \bar{\mu}_i) + \sum_k \beta_{ik}\tilde{\Lambda}_{q_k}(\mu_i - \tilde{\mu}_k) + \sum_j \beta_{ji}\Lambda_{q_i}\Omega_{ji}^{-1}(\tilde{\mu}_i^{(j)} - \mu_j) + \Lambda_{o_i}(\mu_i - o_i),
\label{eq:total_gradient_mu}
\end{equation}
where $\tilde{\mu}_i^{(j)} = \Omega_{ji}\mu_i$ is agent $i$'s mean transported into agent $j$'s frame.

\subsection{Second Variations: The Stiffness Tensor}

The frozen-$\beta$ Hessian of the free energy, $M = \partial^2 \mathcal{F}/\partial\xi\partial\xi$ with the attention weights held at $\beta^*$ (see the differentiation-convention paragraph below), is a symmetric bilinear form on belief configuration space, the stiffness tensor of the potential $\mathcal{F}$. It is positive definite in the covariance sector at the reciprocal-consensus point established below, and it is not in general a metric: the reduced-functional correction and the state-dependent prior weight discussed below both add negative-semidefinite terms away from consensus. The Hessian is taken in the flat affine chart $(\mu_i, \Sigma_i) \in \mathbb{R}^{K_q} \times \mathrm{Sym}(K_q)$. A coordinate Hessian is a tensor, independent of the chart, only at a critical point of $\mathcal{F}$, and consensus is a critical point only when the prior and sensory terms are also stationary there. Away from critical points the chart matters: for $f(\Sigma) = D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\mathcal{N}(0,\Sigma)\|\mathcal{N}(0,\Sigma_p)) + \tfrac12\operatorname{tr}(\Lambda_o\Sigma)$ the second variations along the same tangent vectors in the $\Sigma$ chart and in the precision chart $\Lambda = \Sigma^{-1}$ agree at the critical point but differ by $49$ percent at a noncritical point. A chart-free alternative is the covariant Hessian of the Fisher Levi-Civita connection, which differs from the coordinate Hessian by a Christoffel-symbol term proportional to the gradient and therefore coincides with it at critical points. The full state vector is $\xi = (\mu_1, \ldots, \mu_N, \Sigma_1, \ldots, \Sigma_N)$, yielding the block structure
\begin{equation}
M = \begin{pmatrix} M_{\mu\mu} & C^{\mu\Sigma} \\ (C^{\mu\Sigma})^\top & M_{\Sigma\Sigma} \end{pmatrix},
\label{eq:mass_block_structure}
\end{equation}
where each block is itself an $N \times N$ matrix of sub-blocks.

\paragraph{Notation hierarchy.}
We use three related but distinct objects throughout: (i) $M_{\mu\mu}$ (a matrix), the full Hessian of the mean sector defined in \eqref{eq:mass_block_structure}; (ii) $\bar{\Lambda}_{p_i} = \bar{\Sigma}_i^{-1}$ (a matrix), the prior precision of agent $i$, which is the whole of $[M_{\mu\mu}]_{ii}$ when inter-agent coupling and observation likelihoods are absent ($\beta_{ij}=0$, $\Lambda_{o_i}=0$); and (iii) the scalar stiffness $\bar{M}_i := \operatorname{tr}([M_{\mu\mu}]_{ii})/K_q$, used when a one-number summary is needed. When unqualified, ``the stiffness tensor'' refers to (i) and ``stiffness'' as a scalar refers to (iii).

\paragraph{Differentiation convention.}
The second-variation expressions below treat the attention weights $\beta_{ij}$ as fixed at their softmax-equilibrium values $\beta_{ij}^*$. The envelope theorem licenses this frozen-$\beta$ convention at first order only: for the reduced free energy with $\beta$ eliminated, the true Hessian adds the correction $-\tau^{-1}\mathrm{Cov}_{\beta^*}(\nabla E_{ij}, \nabla E_{ij})$ to the frozen-$\beta$ blocks (the reduced-Hessian identity~\eqref{eq:rg_reduced_hessian} of Appendix~\ref{sec:meta_agent_rg}), a negative-semidefinite term that vanishes exactly at consensus, where the per-edge gradients $\nabla E_{ij}$ coincide, but persists at generic equilibria in which prior, interaction, and sensory pulls balance with nonzero per-edge gradients, so the frozen-$\beta$ blocks overstate the curvature of the reduced functional away from consensus. Differentiating through the softmax rows produces these cross terms through $\partial \beta_{ij}/\partial \mu$ and $\partial \beta_{ij}/\partial \Sigma$. The correction is bounded: writing $D_i = \max_{j,k}\|\nabla E_{ij} - \nabla E_{ik}\|$ for the per-edge gradient diameter, the identity $\mathrm{Cov}_\beta(X) = \tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{j,k}\beta_j\beta_k(X_j - X_k)(X_j - X_k)^\top$ gives the spectral bound $\|\tau^{-1}\mathrm{Cov}_{\beta^*}(\nabla E_{ij})\| \le D_i^2/(2\tau)$. At nondegenerate energy gaps the covariance decays exponentially in the gap over $\tau$, so the correction vanishes in the sharp-attention limit away from ties and is large precisely near attention switching points, where per-edge energies nearly coincide at small $\tau$; this is the rapid-reorganization regime in which the frozen-$\beta$ blocks are unreliable. The boxed forms below are written for general $\beta_{ij}^* \geq 0$ under this convention and reduce to the isolated-agent limit by setting $\beta_{ij}^* = 0$ in the sums. The first variations of the preceding subsection need no such convention, since by the envelope theorem the frozen-$\beta$ gradient equals the gradient of the reduced functional at $\beta = \beta^*$; the convention is stated here because it first matters at second order.

\paragraph{Mean Sector}

\paragraph{Diagonal blocks $(i = k)$:} From the prior we obtain $\partial^2 D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| p_i)/\partial \mu_i \partial \mu_i^\top = \bar{\Lambda}_{p_i}$. From the interaction term as receiver, $\partial^2 D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| \tilde{q}_k)/\partial \mu_i \partial \mu_i^\top = \tilde{\Lambda}_{q_k}$. From the interaction term as sender to agent $j$, $\partial^2 D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_j \| \tilde{q}_i)/\partial \mu_i \partial \mu_i^\top = \Omega_{ji}^\top \tilde{\Lambda}_{q_i}^{(j)} \Omega_{ji} = \Lambda_{q_i}$. From sensory evidence, $\partial^2[-\mathbb{E}_{q_i}[\log p(o_i \mid k_i)]]/\partial \mu_i \partial \mu_i^\top = \Lambda_{o_i}$. The total diagonal stiffness block is
\begin{equation}
\boxed{[M_{\mu\mu}]_{ii} = \bar{\Lambda}_{p_i} + \sum_k \beta_{ik} \tilde{\Lambda}_{q_k} + \sum_j \beta_{ji} \Lambda_{q_i} + \Lambda_{o_i}.}
\label{eq:mass_mu_diagonal}
\end{equation}

\paragraph{Off-diagonal blocks $(i \neq k)$:} From $D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| \tilde{q}_k)$, using Eq.~\eqref{eq:precision_transport},
\begin{equation}
\frac{\partial^2 D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| \tilde{q}_k)}{\partial \mu_i \partial \mu_k^\top} = -\tilde{\Lambda}_{q_k}\Omega_{ik} = -\Omega_{ik}^{-\top}\Lambda_{q_k},
\end{equation}
where the second equality follows from $\tilde{\Lambda}_{q_k}\Omega_{ik} = \Omega_{ik}^{-\top}\Lambda_{q_k}\Omega_{ik}^{-1}\Omega_{ik} = \Omega_{ik}^{-\top}\Lambda_{q_k}$. From $D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_k \| \tilde{q}_i)$ (if $k$ also attends to $i$),
\begin{equation}
\frac{\partial^2 D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_k \| \tilde{q}_i)}{\partial \mu_i \partial \mu_k^\top} = -\Lambda_{q_i}\Omega_{ki}^{-1}.
\end{equation}
The sensory term does not couple different agents. Therefore
\begin{equation}
\boxed{[M_{\mu\mu}]_{ik} = -\beta_{ik}\Omega_{ik}^{-\top}\Lambda_{q_k} - \beta_{ki}\Lambda_{q_i}\Omega_{ki}^{-1} \quad (i \neq k).}
\label{eq:mass_mu_offdiagonal}
\end{equation}
Under $\mathrm{O}(K_q)$ this reduces to $-\beta_{ik}\Omega_{ik}\Lambda_{q_k} - \beta_{ki}\Lambda_{q_i}\Omega_{ki}^\top$.

\paragraph{Off-diagonal block caveats.}\label{sec:mass_block_caveats}
As the Hessian of the scalar functional $\mathcal{F}$, the block matrix $M_{\mu\mu}$ is automatically symmetric in the bilinear-form sense $M_{\mu\mu}^\top = M_{\mu\mu}$, equivalently $[M_{\mu\mu}]_{ik} = [M_{\mu\mu}]_{ki}^\top$, by equality of mixed partial derivatives (Schwarz's theorem). The block-transpose identity is exactly the condition for $M_{\mu\mu}$ to define a quadratic form $\tfrac12 \delta\mu^\top M_{\mu\mu} \delta\mu$ on the stacked variable $(\mu_i)_i$, the second-order term of $\mathcal{F}$; no stronger blockwise-equality condition is required, and an individual block $[M_{\mu\mu}]_{ik}$ need not be symmetric. Where the block-transpose identity appears not to hold in displayed expressions, the cause is that the displayed $[M_{\mu\mu}]_{ik}$ has been restricted to a subset of the contributions to the Hessian (for example a single sender-only or receiver-only contribution), and the symmetry is restored once both contributions are summed.

Row-asymmetric attention does not by itself destroy the scalar potential: fixed asymmetric weights still define the scalar $\sum_{ij}\beta_{ij}E_{ij}$, and state-dependent Gibbs weights in the canonical entropy-retaining objective reduce to $-\tau\sum_i\log Z_i$. In both cases the full receiver-plus-sender Hessian is symmetric, and the full flow is a gradient flow for every fixed $\beta$, reciprocal or not. A nonpotential running rule appears only after the additional receiver-only truncation of Eq.~\eqref{eq:receiver_only_field}, which discards the sender-row derivatives. For that optional field the bilinear form $M_{\mu\mu}$ is not the linearization of the dynamics; the linearization is the Jacobian of the truncated field, which is not symmetric and can have complex eigenvalues. The boxed forms of this section describe the full scalar $\mathcal{F}$ and do not describe the receiver-only field.

\paragraph{Covariance Sector}

For matrix-valued variables, we use the directional derivative convention
\begin{equation}
\frac{\partial^2 f}{\partial \Sigma \partial \Sigma}[V, W] = \lim_{\epsilon \to 0} \frac{1}{\epsilon}\left[\left.\frac{\partial f}{\partial \Sigma}\right|_{\Sigma + \epsilon W} - \left.\frac{\partial f}{\partial \Sigma}\right|_{\Sigma}\right][V],
\end{equation}
with the identity $\partial(\Sigma^{-1})/\partial \Sigma = -\Sigma^{-1} \otimes \Sigma^{-1}$.

\paragraph{Diagonal blocks $(i = k)$:} From the prior,
\begin{equation}
\frac{\partial^2 D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| p_i)}{\partial \Sigma_i \partial \Sigma_i}[V, W] = \frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}[\Lambda_{q_i} V \Lambda_{q_i} W].
\end{equation}
In tensor notation, $\partial^2 D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| p_i)/\partial \Sigma_i \partial \Sigma_i = \frac{1}{2}(\Lambda_{q_i} \otimes \Lambda_{q_i})$. From the interaction term as receiver and as sender, identical contributions arise \emph{at consensus} (see the remark below). The sensory term $\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}(\Lambda_{o_i}\Sigma_i)$ is linear in $\Sigma_i$, so its second derivative vanishes. Therefore
\begin{equation}
\boxed{[M_{\Sigma\Sigma}]_{ii} = \frac{1}{2}(\Lambda_{q_i} \otimes \Lambda_{q_i}) \cdot \left(1 + \sum_k \beta_{ik} + \sum_j \beta_{ji}\right) \quad \text{at consensus.}}
\label{eq:mass_sigma_diagonal}
\end{equation}
The sensory precision $\Lambda_{o_i}$ contributes to the mean-sector stiffness but not to the covariance-sector stiffness.

\paragraph{Remark on the consensus simplification.} The factor $(1 + \sum_k \beta_{ik} + \sum_j \beta_{ji})$ in Eq.~\eqref{eq:mass_sigma_diagonal} arises because the sender contribution from $D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_j \| \tilde{q}_i)$ involves both a negative term $-\frac{1}{2}\Lambda_{q_i} \otimes \Lambda_{q_i}$ from $\partial^2 \ln |\tilde{\Sigma}_i|/\partial \Sigma_i^2$ and a positive trace-term contribution that, away from consensus, equals $\frac{1}{2}\partial^2 \operatorname{tr}((\Omega_{ji}\Sigma_i\Omega_{ji}^\top)^{-1}\Sigma_j)/\partial\Sigma_i^2$ and couples $\Sigma_i$ to $\Sigma_j$. At consensus ($\Sigma_j = \Omega_{ji}\Sigma_i\Omega_{ji}^\top$) this trace-term contribution simplifies to $+\Lambda_{q_i} \otimes \Lambda_{q_i}$, and the sender net contribution collapses to $+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda_{q_i}\otimes\Lambda_{q_i}$, recovering Eq.~\eqref{eq:mass_sigma_diagonal}. Off consensus, the sender contribution carries an explicit $\Sigma_j$-dependent cross-coupling and the diagonal block does not factor into a multiple of $\Lambda_{q_i}\otimes\Lambda_{q_i}$; the boxed form above is the consensus-point restriction.

\paragraph{Off-diagonal blocks $(i \neq k)$:} From $D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| \tilde{q}_k)$, varying both $\Sigma_i$ and $\Sigma_k$, with $\tilde{\Lambda}_{q_k}$ defined by Eq.~\eqref{eq:precision_transport},
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\begin{aligned}
\relax [M_{\Sigma\Sigma}]_{ik} = &-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{ik}\left(\Omega_{ik}^{-\top}\Lambda_{q_k} \otimes \Omega_{ik}^{-\top}\Lambda_{q_k}\right) \\
&-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{ki}\left(\Omega_{ki}^{-\top}\Lambda_{q_i} \otimes \Omega_{ki}^{-\top}\Lambda_{q_i}\right)^\top \quad (i \neq k).
\end{aligned}}
\label{eq:mass_sigma_offdiagonal}
\end{equation}
The block is a pure Kronecker square of the two mean-sector transport factors. Each contribution is independent of the native covariance of the agent whose divergence produces it, a structural property any candidate form of this block must satisfy: agent $i$'s native covariance $\Sigma_i$ enters $D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| \tilde{q}_k)$ only linearly through the trace $\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}(\tilde{\Lambda}_{q_k}\Sigma_i)$, so differentiating in $\Sigma_i$ removes $\Sigma_i$ entirely and leaves $\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\Lambda}_{q_k}$, and the subsequent differentiation in $\Sigma_k$ through the precision transport of Eq.~\eqref{eq:precision_transport} produces the Kronecker square $\Omega_{ik}^{-\top}\Lambda_{q_k} \otimes \Omega_{ik}^{-\top}\Lambda_{q_k}$, which depends on $\Sigma_k$ through $\Lambda_{q_k}$ but carries no residual $\Sigma_i$. By the same argument the sender contribution is independent of $\Sigma_k$ and depends on $\Sigma_i$ through $\Lambda_{q_i}$, so the assembled block depends on both precisions while each term is free of its own agent's covariance. The two-term structure mirrors the mean-sector off-diagonal block of Eq.~\eqref{eq:mass_mu_offdiagonal}: the first term is the receiver contribution from $D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| \tilde{q}_k)$ when $i$ attends to $k$, built from $A_{ik} = \Omega_{ik}^{-\top}\Lambda_{q_k}$; the second is the sender contribution from $D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_k \| \tilde{q}_i)$ when $k$ also attends to $i$, built from $B_{ki} = \Omega_{ki}^{-\top}\Lambda_{q_i}$. The transpose on the second Kronecker factor is essential. The sender mixed partial is naturally assembled with the index ordering $(\Sigma_k, \Sigma_i)$, whereas the block $[M_{\Sigma\Sigma}]_{ik}$ requires the ordering $(\Sigma_i, \Sigma_k)$; since $\Omega_{ki}^{-\top}\Lambda_{q_i} \otimes \Omega_{ki}^{-\top}\Lambda_{q_i}$ is not symmetric, the index swap is exactly the vec-space transpose, and it is what makes the assembled block satisfy the Schwarz block-transpose identity $[M_{\Sigma\Sigma}]_{ik} = [M_{\Sigma\Sigma}]_{ki}^\top$. As in the mean sector, this identity holds for every fixed $\beta$, reciprocal or not, while an individual block $[M_{\Sigma\Sigma}]_{ik}$ need not be symmetric. Under $\mathrm{O}(K_q)$ the factors reduce to $\Omega_{ik}\Lambda_{q_k} \otimes \Omega_{ik}\Lambda_{q_k}$.

At the reciprocal-consensus point, the covariance-sector Hessian restricted to the symmetric tangent space on which $\Sigma$ lives is positive definite for all $\beta \geq 0$ and arbitrary precision heterogeneity. Take Regime I transports $\Omega_{ik} = \exp(\phi_i)\exp(-\phi_k)$, so that the cocycle holds and the transported neighbor $\tilde{q}_k$ coincides with $q_i$, with reciprocal $\beta$ and each prior equal to the native belief. Every relative entropy then sits at its global zero, contributing a positive-semidefinite Hessian, while the strictly convex prior term $\frac{1}{2}(\Lambda_{q_i} \otimes \Lambda_{q_i})$ on each diagonal block makes the total strictly positive definite. Four scope conditions are required. The result is established only at the consensus point; off consensus the $\Sigma_j$ cross-coupling re-enters and positive definiteness is not established there. It uses the frozen-$\beta$ (envelope-theorem) convention of the differentiation paragraph above. It requires a global consensus through the cocycle, not merely pairwise reciprocity; the full $K_q^2 \times K_q^2$ vec operator can be indefinite on the non-symmetric complement, which is not the physical object, since $\Sigma$ is constrained to the symmetric subspace. And the point is a critical point of $\mathcal{F}$ only when the sensory term is absent: the sensory term adds no covariance curvature but contributes the constant covariance gradient $\tfrac12\Lambda_{o_i}$, so with sensory evidence the statement concerns the flat-chart second variation at a noncritical point, with the chart dependence described at the start of this subsection.

\paragraph{Cross Mean-Covariance Blocks}

The prior term gives $\partial^2 D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| p_i)/\partial \mu_i \partial \Sigma_i = 0$. The sensory free energy decomposes into a term quadratic in $\mu_i$ and a term linear in $\Sigma_i$; these are independent, so $[C^{\mu\Sigma}]_{ii}^{\mathrm{sensory}} = 0$.

For the interaction term, from $\partial D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| \tilde{q}_k)/\partial \mu_i = \tilde{\Lambda}_{q_k}(\mu_i - \tilde{\mu}_k)$ with $\tilde{\Lambda}_{q_k} = \Omega_{ik}^{-\top}\Lambda_{q_k}\Omega_{ik}^{-1}$ (Eq.~\eqref{eq:precision_transport}), varying $\Sigma_k$ gives $\delta\tilde{\Lambda}_{q_k} = -\Omega_{ik}^{-\top}\Lambda_{q_k} V \Lambda_{q_k}\Omega_{ik}^{-1}$ for $V = \delta\Sigma_k$, and therefore
\begin{equation}
\frac{\partial^2 D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| \tilde{q}_k)}{\partial \mu_i \partial \Sigma_k}[V] = -\Omega_{ik}^{-\top}\Lambda_{q_k} V \Lambda_{q_k} \Omega_{ik}^{-1} (\mu_i - \tilde{\mu}_k).
\end{equation}
In components,
\begin{equation}
\frac{\partial^2 D_{\mathrm{KL}}}{\partial(\mu_i)_a \partial(\Sigma_k)_{bc}} = -[\Omega_{ik}^{-\top}\Lambda_{q_k}]_{ab}[\Lambda_{q_k}\Omega_{ik}^{-1}(\mu_i - \tilde{\mu}_k)]_c.
\end{equation}
Under $\mathrm{O}(K_q)$ this reduces to $-\Omega_{ik}\Lambda_{q_k} V \Lambda_{q_k}\Omega_{ik}^\top(\mu_i - \tilde{\mu}_k)$. The cross block vanishes at consensus ($\mu_i = \tilde{\mu}_k$):
\begin{equation}
\boxed{[C^{\mu\Sigma}]_{ik} = 0 \quad \text{when } \mu_i = \tilde{\mu}_k.}
\label{eq:cross_block}
\end{equation}

\subsection{Precision as Stiffness and the Fisher Metric as Friction}
\label{sec:stiffness_friction}

The mean-sector stiffness of agent $i$, in the frozen-$\beta$ convention, is
\begin{equation}
M_i = \underbrace{\bar{\Lambda}_{p_i}}_{\text{prior}} + \underbrace{\sum_k \beta_{ik}\tilde{\Lambda}_{q_k}}_{\text{receiver}} + \underbrace{\sum_j \beta_{ji}\Lambda_{q_i}}_{\text{sender}} + \underbrace{\Lambda_{o_i}}_{\text{sensory}}.
\label{eq:effective_mass}
\end{equation}
The prior block resists deviation from the prior $p_i$, the receiver block resists deviation from the transported neighbors that agent $i$ attends to, the sender block resists moves that would disturb the agents attending to $i$, and the sensory block anchors the agent to its observations. The stiffness is dimensionless on the representational space; no identification with a physical mass is made.

\paragraph{Constant versus state-dependent self-coupling.} The boxed stiffness blocks of this section take the prior self-coupling weight at its canonical default $\alpha_i = 1$; the prior term of Eq.~\eqref{eq:extended_free_energy} carries unit weight. Under the state-dependent generalization of Section~\ref{sec:state_dependent_precision}, where $\alpha_i^*(c) = c_0/(b_0 + D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| p_i))$ is recomputed from the current belief at each inner step, the prior block scales as $\alpha_i^* \bar{\Lambda}_{p_i}$. Because $\alpha_i^*$ co-varies with the belief, the second variation acquires a rank-one residual that the first-order envelope cancellation of Eq.~\eqref{eq:alpha_envelope_cancel_itfb} does not remove,
\begin{equation}
[M_{\mu\mu}]_{ii}^{\text{prior}} = \alpha_i^* \bar{\Lambda}_{p_i} - \frac{(\alpha_i^*)^2}{c_0}\left(\bar{\Lambda}_{p_i}\Delta\mu_i\right)\left(\bar{\Lambda}_{p_i}\Delta\mu_i\right)^\top, \qquad \Delta\mu_i = \mu_i - \bar{\mu}_i,
\end{equation}
which vanishes only at $\mu_i = \bar{\mu}_i$. This is the prior-sector analog of the off-consensus rank-one stiffness retained for the interaction term in the first-variation analysis above. For a mean displacement at matched covariance, where $D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| p_i) = \tfrac{1}{2}\Delta\mu_i^\top \bar{\Lambda}_{p_i}\Delta\mu_i$, the regularized prior block stays positive definite until $D_{\mathrm{KL}} = b_0$, whereas the unregularized weight $\alpha_i^* D_{\mathrm{KL}}$ alone would lose definiteness already at $D_{\mathrm{KL}} = b_0/3$; the log-barrier delays it. Beyond that bound the prior contribution to the stiffness is indefinite. The boxed forms of this section are therefore the quasi-static $\alpha_i = 1$ specialization.

\paragraph{Friction and relaxation.} The flow of Eq.~\eqref{eq:gauge_natural_gradient} is first order. In the Gaussian sector it reads
\begin{equation}
\eta_b^{-1}G_b\dot{\xi}_b = -\nabla_{\xi_b}\mathcal{F},
\qquad
G_\mu = \Lambda_q,
\qquad
G_\Sigma[V, W] = \tfrac12\operatorname{tr}(\Sigma^{-1}V\Sigma^{-1}W),
\label{eq:friction_form}
\end{equation}
for each block $b \in \{\mu, \Sigma\}$ with its rate $\eta_b$, where $G_b$ is the Fisher metric of the block. This is the form $\gamma\dot{q} = -\nabla V$ of an overdamped system with friction tensor $\gamma = \eta^{-1}G$ and mobility $\eta G^{-1}$, and it is the Rayleigh form $\partial\mathcal{R}/\partial\dot{\xi} = -\nabla\mathcal{F}$ with dissipation function
\begin{equation}
\mathcal{R}(\dot{\xi}) = \sum_b \frac{1}{2\eta_b}G_b(\dot{\xi}_b, \dot{\xi}_b),
\qquad
\frac{d\mathcal{F}}{dt} = \nabla\mathcal{F}\cdot\dot{\xi} = -2\mathcal{R}.
\label{eq:rayleigh_dissipation}
\end{equation}
With a common rate $\eta_b = \eta$ the squared Fisher speed is $G(\dot{\xi}, \dot{\xi}) = (d\vartheta/dt)^2 = 2\eta\mathcal{R}$, which is the clock-dissipation identity of Eq.~\eqref{eq:clock_dissipation} for an autonomous single-rate flow. The block-descent law and the Fisher clock are established in general in the companion theory~\cite[Chs.~7 and 8]{DennisGaugeVFE2026}. Linearized about a critical point $\xi^*$, the flow is $\delta\dot{\xi} = -\eta G^{-1}M\delta\xi$. Since $G$ is positive definite and $M$ is symmetric, $G^{-1}M$ is similar to $G^{-1/2}MG^{-1/2}$, so its spectrum is real and the linearized flow has no oscillatory modes; complex eigenvalues arise only in the receiver-only truncation of Eq.~\eqref{eq:receiver_only_field}. For an isolated agent at $q_i = p_i$ the stiffness and the friction coincide, $[M_{\mu\mu}]_{ii} = \bar{\Lambda}_{p_i} = \Lambda_{q_i} = G_\mu$ and $[M_{\Sigma\Sigma}]_{ii} = \tfrac12\Lambda_{q_i}\otimes\Lambda_{q_i} = G_\Sigma$, so the linearized mean and covariance relaxation rates are exactly $\eta_\mu$ and $\eta_\Sigma$, independent of the precision. Under a Euclidean gradient flow $\dot{\xi} = -\eta\nabla\mathcal{F}$ the rates would instead be $\eta$ times the eigenvalues of $M$, so a precise agent would relax faster. In neither flow is a high-precision agent slow; large precision produces large stiffness and small static compliance $M^{-1}$.

\paragraph{High-precision agents.} Consider an agent with very sharp prior self-localization, $\bar{\Sigma}_i = \delta I$ with $\delta \ll 1$ a dimensionless precision scale to which no physical units are assigned (the macroscopic-object example of Appendix~\ref{sec:rock_derivations}). In the isolated-agent limit of Eq.~\eqref{eq:effective_mass}, where the interaction blocks switch off ($\beta_{ik} = \beta_{ji} = 0$) and the sensory block is negligible ($\Lambda_{o_i} \to 0$), $M_i$ reduces to the prior precision $\delta^{-1}I$; the interaction and sensory terms add further stiffness when the agent is embedded. Such an agent has large stiffness and compliance of order $\delta$, so a bounded force displaces it by an amount of order $\delta$; under the natural-gradient flow its linear relaxation rate is still $\eta$.

The stiffness above runs entirely on the state-fiber prior precision $\bar{\Lambda}_{p_i}$ and is therefore specific to the prior-pullback tier in the sense of Section~\ref{sec:three_tiers}. Under the structural-tier reading of Section~\ref{sec:gravity_pullback}, perceived geometry is carried instead by the model-fiber generative-model precision $\Sigma_{s,i}^{-1}$, so the stiffness of this section and the pulled-back geometry are distinct objects at that tier; relating them would require a dynamical link between $\bar{\Lambda}_{p_i}$ and $\Sigma_{s,i}^{-1}$ that the present construction does not supply.

\subsection{A Declared Kinetic-Metric Postulate}
\label{sec:velocity_quadratic}

\paragraph{Postulate (kinetic metric).} In addition to $\mathcal{F}$ and its first-order flow, one may postulate a kinetic energy $\mathcal{T} = \tfrac12 \dot{\xi}^\top M_K \dot{\xi}$ with inertia tensor $M_K$ and adopt the second-order equation of motion
\begin{equation}
M_K\ddot{\xi} + \eta^{-1}G\dot{\xi} = -\nabla\mathcal{F},
\label{eq:second_order_postulate}
\end{equation}
the Euler-Lagrange equation of $\mathcal{T} - \mathcal{F}$ with the Rayleigh dissipation of Eq.~\eqref{eq:rayleigh_dissipation}. Its overdamped limit, in which the inertial times are short compared with the relaxation times, is the natural-gradient flow of Eq.~\eqref{eq:friction_form}. Neither $\mathcal{F}$ nor the flow of Eq.~\eqref{eq:gauge_natural_gradient} supplies $M_K$. The inertial reading of precision that the postulate expresses has status S (Table~\ref{tab:claim_status}), and no other result of the paper uses Eq.~\eqref{eq:second_order_postulate}. The natural candidate takes $M_K = M$, the frozen-$\beta$ stiffness at consensus. Its consequences are fixed by linearizing about a critical point. Without dissipation the linearized equation is $M\delta\ddot{\xi} = -M\delta\xi$, so $\delta\ddot{\xi} = -\delta\xi$: every linear mode has the same frequency, $\omega = 1$ in flow-time units, whatever the precision. With the dissipation retained, an isolated agent at $q_i = p_i$, where $M = G$, obeys $\delta\ddot{\xi} + \eta^{-1}\delta\dot{\xi} + \delta\xi = 0$, with damping ratio $1/(2\eta)$; the motion is overdamped for $\eta < 1/2$, and for $\eta \ll 1/2$ the slow root is $-\eta$, the natural-gradient rate. Under the postulate $M_K = M$ the stiffness and the inertia coincide, so no precision-dependent frequency, and in particular no scaling $\omega^2 \propto 1/\bar{M}_i$, follows from it. A precision-dependent frequency requires an inertia tensor that is not the stiffness tensor, which the construction does not supply. For example, the different postulate $M_K = \mathrm{diag}_i(\bar{\Lambda}_{p_i})$ in the mean sector, with $K_c := M_{\mu\mu} - M_K$ the interaction-plus-sensory stiffness, gives $\omega^2 = \mathrm{eig}(M_K^{-1}M_{\mu\mu}) = 1 + \mathrm{eig}(M_K^{-1}K_c)$, a testable relation between frequencies and coupling-plus-sensory stiffness, not an identity; we do not adopt it. A dynamical test of any such postulate requires a trajectory generated by Eq.~\eqref{eq:second_order_postulate}, for example underdamped ringing, which the first-order dynamics used throughout this paper cannot produce, because its linearized spectrum is real; a static-response measurement of the compliance $M^{-1}$ tests the stiffness and says nothing about $M_K$.

Under the postulate $M_K = M$ the kinetic energy takes the form
\begin{equation}
\mathcal{T} = \frac{1}{2}\dot{\mu}^\top M_{\mu\mu} \dot{\mu} + \frac{1}{2}M_{\Sigma\Sigma}[\dot{\Sigma}, \dot{\Sigma}] + \frac{1}{2} G_\phi(\phi)[\dot{\phi}, \dot{\phi}],
\label{eq:full_kinetic}
\end{equation}
where the frame-sector coefficient $G_\phi$ is a further choice, fixed by cases. For $G=\mathrm{SO}(K_q)$ with $K_q\ge2$, the frame $\phi\in\mathfrak{so}(K_q)$ carries the bi-invariant inner product $G_\phi[\dot{\phi},\dot{\phi}]=\langle\dot{\phi},\dot{\phi}\rangle_{\mathfrak{g}}=-\operatorname{tr}(\dot{\phi}^2)$. When $K_q\ge3$, $\mathfrak{so}(K_q)$ is compact semisimple and this inner product is $1/(K_q-2)$ of the negative Killing form, since $B(X,X)=(K_q-2)\operatorname{tr}(X^2)$. At $K_q=2$, $\mathfrak{so}(2)$ is abelian and its Killing form vanishes, so the positive Ad-invariant negative trace form is a separate choice, not a Killing-form construction. Central or abelian factors in any other compact algebra require the same separate choice of positive Ad-invariant inner product. For the general $\mathrm{GL}(K_q)$ case the Killing form on $\mathfrak{gl}(K_q)$ is degenerate (its radical is the center) and indefinite on the semisimple part $\mathfrak{sl}(K_q)$, and $G_\phi$ would be supplied instead by the position-dependent pullback $\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{pull}}$ of the ambient Frobenius metric through the exponential map, Eq.~\eqref{eq:pullback_metric}, under the $\mathrm{dexp}^{L}_\phi$-regularity hypothesis recorded at Eq.~\eqref{eq:gauge_natural_gradient_def}. That form is flat, being the pullback of a flat metric by a local diffeomorphism, and its kinetic term $\tfrac12\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{pull}}(\dot{\phi}, \dot{\phi}) = \tfrac12\|\dot{U}\|_F^2$ is neither left nor right invariant. Under the common right translation $U_i \to U_i g$, which leaves every Regime I transport and hence $\mathcal{F}$ unchanged, the ratio $\|\dot{U}g\|_F^2/\|\dot{U}\|_F^2 = \operatorname{tr}(\dot{U}gg^\top\dot{U}^\top)/\operatorname{tr}(\dot{U}\dot{U}^\top)$ ranges over the interval between the smallest and largest eigenvalues of $gg^\top$, so the term is invariant for every velocity only when $g$ is orthogonal. Since $\mathrm{GL}^+(K_q)$ admits no bi-invariant Riemannian metric~\cite{Milnor1976}, any frame-sector kinetic term for noncompact frames breaks part of the redundancy that $\mathcal{F}$ respects, and in Regime I, where the frames are removable passive re-expressions (Section~\ref{sec:one_rung}), the frame sector of Eq.~\eqref{eq:full_kinetic} is an additional, frame-dependent postulate.

The second term of Eq.~\eqref{eq:full_kinetic} couples covariance evolution to the covariance-sector stiffness, which at an isolated equilibrium is the Fisher (affine-invariant) metric on $\mathbb{S}^+_{K_q}$. The inter-agent off-diagonal blocks contribute
\begin{equation}
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{couple}} = \sum_{i < k}\left[-\beta_{ik}\dot{\mu}_i^\top \Omega_{ik}^{-\top}\Lambda_{q_k}\dot{\mu}_k - \beta_{ki}\dot{\mu}_i^\top \Lambda_{q_i}\Omega_{ki}^{-1} \dot{\mu}_k\right],
\end{equation}
which under the postulate couples the accelerations of the agents. By the block-transpose symmetry of the off-diagonal block caveats paragraph above, this quadratic form is well defined for any $\beta$, reciprocal or not. Equality of the two displayed coefficient blocks is a separate requirement: it holds if and only if $\beta_{ik} = \beta_{ki}$ and the transport identity $\Omega_{ik}^{-\top}\Lambda_{q_k} = \Lambda_{q_i}\Omega_{ki}^{-1}$ hold, and under orientation reversal $\Omega_{ki} = \Omega_{ik}^{-1}$ the latter reduces to the consensus condition $\Omega_{ik}^{-\top}\Lambda_{q_k}\Omega_{ik}^{-1} = \Lambda_{q_i}$ (the transported precisions agree), which reciprocity alone does not enforce.
\section{Coarse-Graining I: The Meta-Agent as Gauge-Covariant Barycenter}\label{sec:meta_agent_formation}

A meta-agent is the gauge-covariant coarse-graining of a coherent cluster of agents into a single agent one level up. Its anatomy, used throughout the manuscript, has four parts. The meta-agent is \emph{defined} by its state, a variational barycenter whose frame-free part is the defining datum. \emph{When} a cluster should be replaced by such a parent is the question of a free-energy licensing criterion (status C). \emph{How} candidate clusters are found is a threshold detector, a coherence filter (status S) not derived from the criterion. Finally, gauge covariance makes the barycenter a frame-consistent coarse-graining map, not an exact one. The companion theory supersedes both the barycenter and the detector: its parents are exact pushforwards of the fine laws under declared coarse channels~\cite[Chs.~9 and~11]{DennisGaugeVFE2026}, and its formation results are witnesses for declared families~\cite[Ch.~13]{DennisGaugeVFE2026}. The barycenter is retained here as a working construction; its relation to the exact parent is stated at the end of Section~\ref{sec:meta_agent_definition}, and the exact coarse-graining step of this manuscript is developed in Section~\ref{sec:rg_status} and Appendix~\ref{app:rigorous_rg}.

\subsection{The Meta-Agent as Gauge-Covariant Variational Barycenter}
\label{sec:meta_agent_definition}

The meta-agent's belief is the M-projection barycenter of its transported constituents. Conditional on a parent existing, treating the parent as a variational distribution and minimizing the alignment energy along the child-parent edges over $q_I$ gives
\begin{equation}
q_I^* = \arg\min_{q_I} \sum_{i \in I} w_i^I D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\Omega_{Ii} q_i \| q_I) + \lambda D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_I \| p_I),
\label{eq:meta_agent_barycenter}
\end{equation}
a moment-matching barycenter, with the meta-belief $q_I$ in the second slot, regularized toward a prior $p_I$ on the parent. The belief coupling $D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| \Omega_{ij} q_j)$ of Eq.~\eqref{eq:beta_optimal} places the receiver's law in the first slot (the I-projection direction), so the two constructions use opposite slot orders. Here $w_i^I \ge 0$ is the cluster-aggregation weight of constituent $i$ in cluster $I$; it is a distinct object from the per-agent precision $\alpha_i$ of Section~\ref{sec:state_dependent_precision}, which weights the agent's own belief-prior coupling $D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| p_i)$. For Gaussian beliefs $q_i = \mathcal{N}(\mu_i, \Sigma_i)$ with transported moments $\tilde{\mu}_i = \Omega_{Ii}\mu_i$ and $\tilde{\Sigma}_i = \Omega_{Ii}\Sigma_i\Omega_{Ii}^\top$, the closed form (with $W_I := \sum_{i \in I} w_i^I$ and $\lambda \to 0$ for legibility) is
\begin{align}
\mu_I^* &= \tfrac{1}{W_I}\textstyle\sum_i w_i^I \tilde{\mu}_i,
\label{eq:meta_agent_mu_barycenter}
\\
\Sigma_I^* &= \tfrac{1}{W_I}\textstyle\sum_i w_i^I\big[\tilde{\Sigma}_i + (\mu_I^* - \tilde{\mu}_i)(\mu_I^* - \tilde{\mu}_i)^\top\big].
\label{eq:meta_agent_sigma_barycenter}
\end{align}
The mean is the $w^I$-weighted average of the transported constituent means; the covariance is the $w^I$-weighted average of the transported covariances plus a dispersion term that records how much the constituents disagree about the mean. The dispersion term vanishes at strict consensus, when all $\tilde{\mu}_i$ coincide. In the high-coherence regime it is $\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon)$ in the post-transport pairwise dispersion, and dropping it is an approximation, not an identity (the error bound and the working forms are given in Section~\ref{sec:meta_agent_state_construction}).

The M-projection is not the only equivariant variational barycenter. The I-projection $\arg\min_q \sum_i w_i^I D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q \| \Omega_{Ii} q_i)$, whose Gaussian solution is the precision average $\Lambda_I = \sum_i w_i^I \tilde{\Lambda}_i / W_I$, is also a strict minimizer and is equally covariant under common pushforward. We adopt the M-projection for two reasons: it is the exact M-step coordinate of a random-effects model for the constituents (Section~\ref{sec:meta_agent_variational}), and it pools the constituents' uncertainty, whereas the precision average sharpens it.

\begin{proposition}[Existence, uniqueness, and equivariance of the belief barycenter]
\label{prop:barycenter_existence}
Let $w_i^I \ge 0$ with $W_I = \sum_{i \in I} w_i^I > 0$, and let the transported covariances $\tilde{\Sigma}_i = \Omega_{Ii}\Sigma_i\Omega_{Ii}^\top$ be positive definite. Then the minimizer of Eq.~\eqref{eq:meta_agent_barycenter} with $\lambda \to 0$ over the Gaussian family exists and is unique, given by Eqs.~\eqref{eq:meta_agent_mu_barycenter}--\eqref{eq:meta_agent_sigma_barycenter}, and the map $(\{q_i\}, \{U_i\}, U_I) \mapsto q_I^*$ commutes with the action of one global $g \in G$ on beliefs and frames (Theorem~\ref{thm:rg_covariance}).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
In the natural parameters $\theta$ of $q_I$, each term $D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\Omega_{Ii}q_i \| q_\theta)$ is, up to a $\theta$-independent constant, the Bregman divergence $B_\psi(\theta, \cdot)$ of the Gaussian log-partition function $\psi$, which is strictly convex on the open natural-parameter domain, so the objective equals $\sum_{i \in I} w_i^I[\psi(\theta) - \langle \tilde{\eta}_i, \theta\rangle] + \mathrm{const}$ with $\tilde{\eta}_i$ the expectation parameter of the transported constituent $\Omega_{Ii}q_i$. Strict convexity gives a unique stationary point $\nabla\psi(\theta^*) = W_I^{-1}\sum_{i \in I} w_i^I \tilde{\eta}_i$, which is exactly the moment matching of Eqs.~\eqref{eq:meta_agent_mu_barycenter}--\eqref{eq:meta_agent_sigma_barycenter}, and the minimizer exists because a convex combination of interior expectation parameters lies in the open expectation domain. Equivariance is the statement of Theorem~\ref{thm:rg_covariance}.
\end{proof}

The same closed form, together with its minimized score $\tfrac12[\log\det\Sigma_I^* - \sum_i w_i^I \log\det\tilde{\Sigma}_i]$ for normalized weights, is the full-Gaussian barycenter proposition of~\cite[Ch.~G8]{DennisGaugeVFESuppG2026}. For $\lambda > 0$ the regularized objective adds the term $\lambda D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_I \| p_I)$, with the parent in the first slot, for which coercivity yields existence; uniqueness is claimed only for the $\lambda \to 0$ statement above.

The parent's gauge frame requires separate treatment, because it is not identifiable from anything the parent does. The next proposition states this and a companion obstruction.

\begin{proposition}[The parent frame is pure gauge; no symmetric equivariant frame mean]
\label{prop:parent_frame_gauge}
(i) Let a parent with frame $U_I$ and law $\mathcal{N}(\mu_I, \Sigma_I)$ in that frame supply constituent $i$ the transported law $(\Omega_{iI})_\#\mathcal{N}(\mu_I, \Sigma_I)$ with $\Omega_{iI} = U_i U_I^{-1}$, as in the cross-scale prior of Eq.~\eqref{eq:cross_scale_shadow}. This law is $\mathcal{N}(U_i m, U_i S U_i^\top)$ with
\begin{equation*}
m = U_I^{-1}\mu_I, \qquad S = U_I^{-1}\Sigma_I U_I^{-\top},
\end{equation*}
so every constituent law depends on $(U_I, \mu_I, \Sigma_I)$ only through the frame-free pair $(m, S)$. The barycenter of Eqs.~\eqref{eq:meta_agent_mu_barycenter}--\eqref{eq:meta_agent_sigma_barycenter}, computed with $\Omega_{Ii} = U_I U_i^{-1}$ and weights built from transported divergences, gives a pair $(m, S)$ that does not depend on $U_I$.
(ii) For $K_q \ge 2$ and $n \ge 2$ there is no map $M: \mathrm{GL}^+(K_q)^n \to \mathrm{GL}^+(K_q)$ that is symmetric under permutations of its arguments and left equivariant, $M(gU_1, \ldots, gU_n) = g M(U_1, \ldots, U_n)$ for all $g$; no continuity is assumed, and the same holds with $\mathrm{SO}(K_q)$ in place of $\mathrm{GL}^+(K_q)$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(i) The pushforward of $\mathcal{N}(\mu_I, \Sigma_I)$ by $\Omega_{iI}$ is $\mathcal{N}(U_i U_I^{-1}\mu_I, U_i U_I^{-1}\Sigma_I U_I^{-\top} U_i^\top)$, which is the stated law. For the barycenter, $\tilde{\mu}_i = U_I (U_i^{-1}\mu_i)$ and $\tilde{\Sigma}_i = U_I (U_i^{-1}\Sigma_i U_i^{-\top}) U_I^\top$, so Eqs.~\eqref{eq:meta_agent_mu_barycenter}--\eqref{eq:meta_agent_sigma_barycenter} give $m = W_I^{-1}\sum_i w_i^I U_i^{-1}\mu_i$ and $S = W_I^{-1}\sum_i w_i^I [U_i^{-1}\Sigma_i U_i^{-\top} + (m - U_i^{-1}\mu_i)(m - U_i^{-1}\mu_i)^\top]$, in which $U_I$ does not appear; transported divergences are invariant under the common pushforward by $U_I^{-1}$, so the weights do not depend on $U_I$ either. (ii) Let $v \in \mathrm{SO}(K_q)$ be a rotation by $2\pi/n$ in one coordinate plane, so that $v$ has order $n$, and take $U_j = v^{j-1}$ for $j = 1, \ldots, n$. Left multiplication by $v$ permutes this tuple cyclically, so symmetry gives $M(vU_1, \ldots, vU_n) = M(U_1, \ldots, U_n)$, while equivariance gives $M(vU_1, \ldots, vU_n) = vM(U_1, \ldots, U_n)$. Hence $(v - I)M = 0$, and since $M$ is invertible, $v = I$, a contradiction.
\end{proof}

Part (i) says that $U_I$ ranges over a $K_q^2$-dimensional orbit along which nothing observable varies, and only $(m, S)$ is determined. A search for the correct parent frame is therefore a search for a canonical value of a pure-gauge quantity: any rule fixing $U_I$ is a gauge convention to be declared, and the open problem recorded in Section~\ref{sec:open_problems} concerns the choice of such a convention, not a theorem. Part (ii) is a global statement. Frame means that are symmetric and equivariant exist on restricted domains of sufficiently concentrated frames, which exclude the cyclic tuples of the proof.

The default convention is the Riemannian center of mass of the constituent frames,
\begin{equation}
U_I^* = \arg\min_{U \in G} \sum_{i \in I} w_i d_G(U, U_i)^2.
\label{eq:meta_agent_frame_barycenter}
\end{equation}
For a compact gauge group with bi-invariant metric $d_G(U, V)^2 = \|\log(U^{-1}V)\|_F^2$, the construction is that of Grove and Karcher~\cite{GroveKarcher1973}, with estimates by Karcher~\cite{Karcher1977} (see also~\cite{Pennec2009}). The minimizer exists, is unique as the global minimizer over $G$, and lies in the ball, whenever the weighted frames lie in an open geodesic ball of radius $\rho < r_{\mathrm{cx}} = \tfrac12\min\{\operatorname{inj} G, \pi/\sqrt{\Delta}\}$, with $\Delta$ an upper bound on the sectional curvature~\cite[Thm.~2.1]{Afsari2011}. For $\mathrm{SO}(K_q)$ with this metric, $r_{\mathrm{cx}} = \pi/\sqrt{2}$ in $d_G$, which for $\mathrm{SO}(3)$ is a rotation angle of $\pi/2$ (for $\mathrm{SO}(3)$, $\Delta = 1/8$ and $\operatorname{inj} = \sqrt{2}\pi$; for $K_q \ge 4$, $\Delta = 1/4$ with the same injectivity radius). Kendall~\cite{Kendall1990} obtains the larger radius $\min\{\operatorname{inj} G, \pi/(2\sqrt{\Delta})\}$ only for the mean defined with the distance restricted to the ball, and that radius does not govern the global argmin~\eqref{eq:meta_agent_frame_barycenter}: three rotations by $0$, $2\pi/3$, and $4\pi/3$ about one axis lie inside Kendall's radius, and the uniform-weight objective attains its minimum $16\pi^2/27$ at each of the three. On the convex ball the Karcher convention is left and right equivariant, since $d_G$ is bi-invariant. The frame condition $\rho < r_{\mathrm{cx}}$ is not detectable by the consensus score of Section~\ref{sec:meta_agent_threshold}, which is built from gauge-invariant divergences: if the three constituents above carry the beliefs $(U_j)_\# q_0$ of one law $q_0$, every transported divergence vanishes and the belief-coherence factor equals one.

The natural variational-inference gauge group $\mathrm{GL}^+(K_q)$ is noncompact and admits no bi-invariant Riemannian metric~\cite[Lemma 7.5]{Milnor1976}, a connected Lie group carrying one exactly when it is isomorphic to a product of a compact group and a vector space. The Ad-invariant trace form $\langle X, Y \rangle = \mathrm{tr}(XY)$ on $\mathfrak{gl}(K_q)$ is bi-invariant and nondegenerate, so a bi-invariant \emph{pseudo}-Riemannian metric does exist, of signature $(K_q(K_q+1)/2, K_q(K_q-1)/2)$. Being indefinite, it induces no distance function and supplies no Fr\'echet objective. The expression $\|\log(U^{-1}V)\|_F$ is not a distance on $\mathrm{GL}^+(K_q)$: the principal logarithm is undefined on an open set of the group, and the triangle inequality fails. The obstruction is to a bi-invariant variance, not to a bi-invariant mean.
