The exponential barycenter of the canonical Cartan-Schouten connection, the solution $U_I$ of $\sum_i w_i \log(U_I^{-1}U_i) = 0$, is left-, right- and inversion-equivariant and exists uniquely for sufficiently concentrated frames~\cite[Thms.~11--12]{PennecArsigny2013}; it is not the minimizer of any Riemannian variance. Under a left-invariant Riemannian metric instead~\cite{Milnor1976, Andruchow2014}, the group is complete and homogeneous, so Afsari's theorem applies and the Riemannian center of mass exists and is unique below $r_{\mathrm{cx}}$, but it is only left-equivariant. The affine-invariant route through the polar decomposition~\cite{PennecFillardAyache2006} is a further option. By Proposition~\ref{prop:parent_frame_gauge}, the choice among these is a choice of gauge convention with no effect on the parent laws.

Gauge covariance of the barycenter map (Theorem~\ref{thm:rg_covariance}) makes the belief barycenter and a declared frame convention frame-consistent coarse-graining maps: under the theorem's hypotheses they commute with the global action on beliefs and frames, so the parent is not an artifact of the frames in which the constituents happen to be written. Covariance does not single these maps out, since the I-projection above is equally covariant, and it does not make the barycenter an exact coarse-graining. The exact parent of a cluster is the pushforward of the fine joint law, posterior, and recognition law under one declared coarse kernel~\cite[Ch.~9]{DennisGaugeVFE2026} (the pointwise parent-datum corollary). The Gaussian barycenter with its dispersion term is the moment-matched projection of the random-selection mixture of the transported constituents~\cite[Ch.~G8]{DennisGaugeVFESuppG2026}, and the pooled form without the dispersion term, used in Section~\ref{sec:meta_agent_state_construction}, is already for two scalar constituents with equal weights the image of no parameter-independent Markov kernel~\cite[Ch.~G8]{DennisGaugeVFESuppG2026} (the normal-barycenter kernel proposition). The evidence-preservation and data-processing statements of the exact construction therefore do not transfer to the working form.

\begin{definition}[Meta-Agent]
Let $I = \{i, j, \ldots\} \subseteq \mathcal{I}^{(\ell)}$ be a cluster of level-$\ell$ agents with (1) non-empty multi-child support
\begin{equation}
\mathcal{U}_I = \mathrm{ConnComp}\Big(\big\{c \in \mathcal{C}: \textstyle\sum_{i \in I} \chi_i(c) \ge 2\big\}\Big) \neq \varnothing,
\label{eq:meta_agent_support}
\end{equation}
a connected region in which at least two constituents maintain beliefs simultaneously, with $\chi_i: \mathcal{C} \to [0, 1]$ agent $i$'s support function (introduced with the continuous-field free energy in Section~\ref{sec:complete_free_energy}; Appendix~\ref{app:rigorous_rg} takes $\chi_i \in \{0, 1\}$), and (2) minimum cluster size $|I| \geq N_{\min}$ (typically $N_{\min} = 2$). The meta-agent $\mathcal{A}^{(\ell+1)}$ of $I$ is the measurable, piecewise-smooth section $(q^{(\ell+1)}, p^{(\ell+1)}, s^{(\ell+1)}, r^{(\ell+1)}, \phi^{(\ell+1)})$ over $\mathcal{U}_I$ whose belief is the barycenter~\eqref{eq:meta_agent_barycenter} and whose model is the analogous barycenter on the model fiber. Its defining datum is the frame-free pair $(m, S)$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:parent_frame_gauge}; the frame $\phi^{(\ell+1)}$, equivalently $U_I$, is a declared gauge convention, by default the Karcher mean~\eqref{eq:meta_agent_frame_barycenter}, which is single-valued when the constituent frames at $c$ lie in a geodesic ball of radius below $r_{\mathrm{cx}}$. The averaged fields are smooth wherever the active constituent set and the weights vary smoothly, but can fail to be smooth across support boundaries where $\sum_{i \in I}\chi_i$ crosses the threshold in~\eqref{eq:meta_agent_support}, so we claim only the measurability that the coarse-graining of Appendix~\ref{app:rigorous_rg} requires. Under the smooth-bump convention of Section~\ref{sec:complete_free_energy} (smooth $\chi_i$), the averaged fields are smooth on the interior of $\mathcal{U}_I$, and it is in this interior sense that the meta-agent re-enters the free energy as an agent at level $\ell+1$. The stricter $m$-fold variant $\sum_{i \in I}\chi_i(c) \ge m$ for $m > 2$, up to the global-intersection limit $m = |I|$, is available where applications require deeper simultaneous coverage. A meta-agent is \emph{licensed} when, in addition, the criterion~\eqref{eq:meta_agent_FE_criterion} of Section~\ref{sec:meta_agent_variational} holds; the threshold detector of Section~\ref{sec:meta_agent_threshold} is a filter for candidate clusters and is not part of the definition.
\end{definition}

The slow model channel $s$ carries the persistent identity across this definition: belief consensus shifts on the perceptual timescale, while the generative model changes only on the learning timescale. On an interpretive reading (status S), a culture, a school of thought, or a scientific community is a cluster whose members share enough of a common slow generative model, after transport into one another's frames, to render their mutual disagreements intelligible. Members belong to the same meta-agent not when $s_i = s_j$, a strict identity no nontrivial culture exhibits, but when $s_i \approx \tilde\Omega_{ij} s_j$, with the transport $\tilde\Omega_{ij}$ distinguishing intra-cultural disagreement (translatable across frames) from radical incommensurability (not translatable). The fast-channel beliefs $q_i$ may diverge so long as the models remain mutually translatable. The active-inference counterpart of the construction is the group-level generative model of Waade et al.~\cite{waade2025asone}, who propose that a collective can constitute a group-level agent if it maintains a group-level Markov blanket; the detector used here is a similarity threshold and does not test that conditional-independence condition. That coherent collective structure can emerge from each agent minimizing variational free energy with no hand-coded alignment rules is the central finding of Heins et al.~\cite{heins2024collective}, the closest external precedent for the emergent reading, while the complementary fragmented limit, in which confirmation-biased free-energy-minimizing sampling produces echo chambers tunable by prior precision, is the active-inference account of Albarracin et al.~\cite{albarracin2022epistemic}. The stronger reading, on which meta-agents are dynamical agents in their own right and not statistical summaries of their constituents, is an interpretive commitment of status S (Table~\ref{tab:claim_status}); Section~\ref{sec:meta_agent_variational} shows that the exact variational reading of the barycenter treats the parent as a fitted hyperparameter.

\subsection{Licensing: The Free-Energy Improvement Criterion}
\label{sec:meta_agent_variational}

The definition above specifies what a meta-agent is, conditional on its existing. The proposed rule for when a cluster should be replaced by one is a variational free-energy improvement criterion. A candidate meta-agent $I$ is licensed when the optimized free energy with the parent inserted, plus an organizational overhead $C(I)$, falls below the optimized free energy of the disaggregated constituents,
\begin{equation}
\mathcal{F}^*[q_I, p_I, s_I, r_I, \{q_i, s_i\}_{i \in I}] + C(I) < \mathcal{F}^*[\{q_i, p_i, s_i, r_i\}_{i \in I}].
\label{eq:meta_agent_FE_criterion}
\end{equation}
Here $C(I)$ is the cost of carrying the additional organizational structure (the parent's variational parameters and its place in the cross-scale hierarchy). It is not specified here; entropy or minimum-description-length terms in the parent's primitive variables are candidates, so the criterion contains a free function. The criterion is the $\Delta_I > 0$ special case of the description-length retention rule of Appendix~\ref{sec:meta_agent_rg} (Eq.~\eqref{eq:rg_retention_gain}), where $\Delta_I$ is the net retention gain at an unspecified complexity penalty $C(I)$; the general rule, its penalty choices, and the closure ansatz under which it is admissible are deferred to Appendix~\ref{sec:meta_agent_rg}, with statements and proofs in Appendix~\ref{app:rigorous_rg}. The criterion has status C for two reasons.

First, the source of any saving is not identified. Which intra-cluster coupling terms are removed when the parent is inserted is part of a post-merge functional that the present formalism leaves unspecified. At frozen attention weights, the row constraint $\sum_j \beta_{ij} = 1$ caps the energetic intra-cluster alignment $\sum_i \sum_{j \in I} \beta_{ij} D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| \Omega_{ij} q_j)$ at $|I|\varepsilon$ when the pairwise post-transport divergences are bounded by $\varepsilon$ (not the quadratic $|I|(|I|-1)\varepsilon$ an unweighted pair count would give). This bound does not measure a saving of $\mathcal{F}^*$. At Gibbs-optimal rows each row sits at the value $A_i = -\tau\log(Z_{\mathrm{in}} + Z_{\mathrm{out}})$, with $Z_{\mathrm{in}}$ and $Z_{\mathrm{out}}$ the prior-weighted partition sums over sources inside and outside the cluster, and deleting the intra-cluster sources raises it by $\tau\log(1 + Z_{\mathrm{in}}/Z_{\mathrm{out}}) > 0$ when the prior is left unnormalized; with the prior rescaled to unit mass over the survivors the change is still positive whenever the intra-cluster energies are the smaller ones, which is the high-coherence case. Any net gain must therefore come from the rows of agents outside the cluster, which couple to one parent in place of $|I|$ constituents, or from the parent's prior term, and this is not derived here.

Second, the criterion is degenerate when the parent is exact. If the parent laws are the pushforward of the fine laws under a declared coarse channel, the fine free energy equals the coarse one plus the discarded conditional information, $\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{fine}} = \mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{coarse}} + \Delta_A$ with $\Delta_A \ge 0$ for every channel, at unchanged evidence~\cite[Ch.~9]{DennisGaugeVFE2026} (the exact coarse free-energy theorem and its evidence-preserving corollary). Read with exact parents, Eq.~\eqref{eq:meta_agent_FE_criterion} reduces to $\Delta_A > C(I)$: it licenses whichever cluster discards the most conditional information, and the drop in free energy is neither model improvement nor evidence ascent. A usable licensing rule needs a post-merge functional that is not monotone under refinement and has a nondegenerate optimizer; neither this paper nor~\cite[Ch.~9]{DennisGaugeVFE2026}, where the selection of partitions is an open problem, supplies one.

The barycenter itself has an exact variational reading, once the generative model it belongs to is stated. Let the constituents' latent states be independent draws $k_i \sim \mathcal{N}(U_i m, U_i S U_i^\top)$ from a random-effects model whose hyperparameter $\theta = (m, S)$ is a point parameter, the frame-free parent pair of Proposition~\ref{prop:parent_frame_gauge}. In $\sum_i w_i^I \mathbb{E}_{q_i}[\log p_\theta(k_i)]$ the transport cancels from both the trace and the quadratic form, leaving a weighted Gaussian maximum-likelihood problem for the transported moments whose maximizer is exactly Eqs.~\eqref{eq:meta_agent_mu_barycenter}--\eqref{eq:meta_agent_sigma_barycenter}, dispersion term included. The barycenter is therefore the M-step, or type-II maximum-likelihood (empirical-Bayes), coordinate for $\theta$ at fixed recognition laws, and alternating it with updates of the $q_i$ descends one two-argument objective~\cite{Neal1998}; it is the Gaussian restriction of the aggregate-prior proposition of~\cite[Ch.~12]{DennisGaugeVFE2026}. In this model the parent is a fitted hyperparameter: it carries no belief of its own, $q_I$ is a point estimate, not a posterior, and $\Sigma_I$ is the population covariance of the constituents. A different generative model, with one parent latent and the constituents as noisy copies of it (Appendix~\ref{app:augmented_joint}), gives $\Sigma_I$ the meaning of posterior uncertainty; there the mean-field coordinate update of the parent is precision addition, which sharpens the parent as $\mathcal{O}(1/|I|)$, does not depend on the constituent covariances, and runs opposite to the covariance pooling of Eq.~\eqref{eq:meta_agent_sigma_impl}. The two readings are distinct models, and only the random-effects reading makes the barycenter an exact coordinate. The derivation fixes the form and not the weights: any weight vector yields a valid M-step coordinate, so the coherence weight $\exp[-D_{\mathrm{KL}}]$ of Section~\ref{sec:meta_agent_state_construction} is a declared modeling choice.

A further proposal treats the parent state as a predictive sufficient statistic of its constituents in the sense of the information bottleneck~\cite{tishby1999information}: reading $C(I)$ as the bottleneck rate term and the free-energy change as a proxy for the predictive term, the information-bottleneck Lagrangian~\cite{bialek2001predictability} would select the coarse-graining that retains maximal predictive information at fixed rate. This is an analogy, not a derivation. The closed-form Gaussian-IB solution of~\cite{chechik2005information} does not apply directly, because the parent state is a pair of Gaussian-parameterized laws on associated bundles, not a jointly Gaussian vector, and the deterministic~\cite{StrouseSchwab2017} and agglomerative~\cite{SlonimTishby2000} variants are not worked out here.

\subsection{The Threshold Detector: A Coherence Filter for Candidate Clusters}
\label{sec:meta_agent_threshold}

Evaluating~\eqref{eq:meta_agent_FE_criterion} requires the optimized free energy under both configurations, which is expensive, and the criterion is itself conjectural. Candidate clusters are therefore found with a threshold detector built from a coherence proxy that combines post-transport belief alignment, post-transport model alignment, and spatial overlap. Each factor is a bounded exponential of a dispersion, so the detector lives in $[0, 1]$. Belief coherence is the bounded exponential of the average post-transport belief dispersion,
\begin{equation}
C_q(I, x) = \exp\left[-\frac{1}{\tau_q |I|^2}\sum_{i,j \in I} D_{\mathrm{KL}}\big(q_i^{(\ell)}(x) \| \Omega_{ij}[q_j^{(\ell)}](x)\big)\right] \in [0, 1].
\end{equation}
Model coherence is defined analogously as $C_s(I, x) = \exp[-V^{(s)}_I(x)/\tau_s]$ with $V^{(s)}_I(x) = |I|^{-2}\sum_{i,j \in I} D_{\mathrm{KL}}(s_i^{(\ell)}(x) \| \tilde{\Omega}_{ij}[s_j^{(\ell)}](x))$, the superscript labeling the model channel and the subscript the cluster. Presence measures spatial overlap via $P(I, x) = |I|^{-1}\sum_{i \in I} \chi_i(x) \in [0, 1]$. The dispersion temperatures $\tau_q, \tau_s > 0$ set the belief and model resolution at which coherence is read off. The consensus score is the product $\Gamma(I, x) = P(I, x) C_q(I, x) C_s(I, x) \in [0, 1]$, and a cluster is a candidate when $\Gamma(I, x) > \Gamma_{\min}$ and $|I| \geq N_{\min}$. Boundedness matters because $D_{\mathrm{KL}}$ is unbounded above, so a $1 - D_{\mathrm{KL}}$ surrogate would be signed and could yield misleadingly positive products from two negative factors. The same exponential detector reappears in Appendix~\ref{app:rigorous_rg}, written with the barycenter-relative dispersions $V_I^{(q)}$, $V_I^{(s)}$ and strengthened by a spectral-gap condition. Numerical values quoted below use $\Gamma_{\min} = 1/2$, $N_{\min} = 2$, and $\tau_q = 0.05$ for illustration; they are not theoretical requirements.

The detector's relation to the licensing criterion is partial. Because $P, C_s \le 1$, the pass band $\Gamma > \Gamma_{\min}$ forces the mean belief dispersion below $\tau_q\log(1/\Gamma_{\min})$, which selects the high-coherence end, including the limit of vanishing dispersion. The criterion has no derived optimum against which this band could be compared (Section~\ref{sec:meta_agent_variational}), and we do not establish that the product $P \cdot C_q \cdot C_s$ tracks any free-energy change, even in the high-coherence limit. The strengthened detector of Appendix~\ref{app:rigorous_rg} additionally requires a positive constrained spectral gap $m_I > m_{\min}$; the canonical specification above does not check this condition, so by the appendix's own classification it operates as a similarity threshold and not as a closure surrogate. The appendix's retention theorem (Theorem~\ref{thm:rg_detector_retention}) concerns the strengthened detector with the gap condition, not the one specified here.

\subsection{Gauge-Covariant Belief Construction}
\label{sec:meta_agent_state_construction}

When a meta-agent $I$ forms, its belief is the barycenter of~\eqref{eq:meta_agent_barycenter}. The working form drops the dispersion term in~\eqref{eq:meta_agent_sigma_barycenter}, an approximation for high-coherence clusters. The dropped term is the constituent mean-spread $\sum_i w_i (\tilde{\mu}_i - \bar{\mu}_I)(\tilde{\mu}_i - \bar{\mu}_I)^\top$. With normalized weights and $\bar{\varepsilon}_w = \sum_{i,j} w_i w_j D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| \Omega_{ij}[q_j])$, it obeys the operator-norm bound
\begin{equation}
\Big\|\sum_i w_i (\tilde{\mu}_i - \bar{\mu}_I)(\tilde{\mu}_i - \bar{\mu}_I)^\top\Big\|_{\mathrm{op}} \le \max_j \lambda_{\max}(\tilde{\Sigma}_j) \bar{\varepsilon}_w,
\label{eq:dispersion_drop_bound}
\end{equation}
which follows from the pairwise-difference identity for weighted spreads, $\sum_i w_i (\tilde{\mu}_i - \bar{\mu}_I)(\tilde{\mu}_i - \bar{\mu}_I)^\top = \tfrac12\sum_{i,j} w_i w_j (\tilde{\mu}_i - \tilde{\mu}_j)(\tilde{\mu}_i - \tilde{\mu}_j)^\top$, the Gaussian mean-term lower bound $D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\mathcal{N}_1 \| \mathcal{N}_2) \ge \tfrac12(\Delta\mu)^\top\Sigma_2^{-1}(\Delta\mu)$, and cocycle invariance of the transported divergences, which holds in Regime I (trivial holonomy). The bound is tight for collinear equal-covariance configurations. The detector controls the unweighted mean dispersion $\bar{\varepsilon} = |I|^{-2}\sum_{i,j} D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| \Omega_{ij}[q_j])$, not $\bar{\varepsilon}_w$, and the two are related by $\bar{\varepsilon}_w \le |I|^2 (\max_i w_i)^2 \bar{\varepsilon}$. Inside the pass band the right side of~\eqref{eq:dispersion_drop_bound} is therefore at most $|I|^2(\max_i w_i)^2 \tau_q\log(1/\Gamma_{\min}) \max_j \lambda_{\max}(\tilde{\Sigma}_j)$, which reduces to $\tau_q \log 2 \max_j \lambda_{\max}(\tilde{\Sigma}_j) \approx 0.035 \max_j \lambda_{\max}(\tilde{\Sigma}_j)$ for uniform weights at the illustrative values. For uniform weights the truncation is thus a moment-matching approximation with a controlled error; for nonuniform weights the factor $|I|^2(\max_i w_i)^2$ must be carried, and at order-one dispersion temperatures the mean-dispersion bound degrades to $\log 2 \approx 0.69$ nats, a regime in which the dispersion term should be reinstated. The working forms keep the barycenter mean and the transported-covariance pooling and drop only the constituent mean-spread:
\begin{align}
\bar{\mu}_I(x) &= \frac{\sum_{i \in I} w_i(x) \Omega_{Ii}[\mu_i^{(\ell)}](x)}{\sum_{i \in I} w_i(x)},
\label{eq:meta_agent_mu_impl}
\\
\bar{\Sigma}_I(x) &= \frac{\sum_{i \in I} w_i(x) \Omega_{Ii}[\Sigma_i^{(\ell)}](x) \Omega_{Ii}^\top}{\sum_{i \in I} w_i(x)}.
\label{eq:meta_agent_sigma_impl}
\end{align}
The weights depend on presence and coherence through $w_i^I(x) = \chi_i(x) \exp[-D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\Omega_{Ii}[q_i^{(\ell)}] \| \bar{q}_I^{(\ell)})]$, with the comparison taken after transport into the meta-agent's frame. The scalar weight is gauge invariant, being built from a transported divergence and a support mask that are each invariant under common pushforward, as the covariance theorem of Appendix~\ref{app:rigorous_rg} requires. It is a declared modeling choice (Section~\ref{sec:meta_agent_variational}), not the approximation of an optimal weight. Because $\bar{q}_I^{(\ell)}$ appears on the right-hand side through the weights, the formula is implicit and is solved by fixed-point iteration. Its convergence is guaranteed when the maximum pairwise transported divergence satisfies $\varepsilon < \varepsilon_0$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:weight_contraction}). The detector's pass band bounds only the mean dispersion, which implies $\max_{i \ne j} D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| \Omega_{ij}[q_j]) \le |I|^2\tau_q\log(1/\Gamma_{\min})$ and not $\varepsilon < \varepsilon_0$, so for large clusters a separate check of the maximum divergence is required. The check is not a formality: with identity covariances, 609 coincident members and one member at distance $4.6$ have mean dispersion $0.0346 < \tau_q\log 2$ at $\tau_q = 0.05$, maximum pairwise divergence $10.6$, and a weight map with two stable fixed points, one of which gives the outlier $0.974$ of the total weight. A non-convergence test cannot detect this bistability, because the iteration converges.

\begin{lemma}[Contraction of the weight map at high coherence]
\label{lem:weight_contraction}
Fix $0 < \lambda_{\min} \le \lambda_{\max}$, assume Regime I transport, and suppose the transported constituents satisfy $\lambda_{\min} I \preceq \tilde{\Sigma}_i \preceq \lambda_{\max} I$, the supports satisfy $\sum_{i \in I}\chi_i > 0$, and the pairwise transported divergences satisfy $\max_{i \ne j} D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| \Omega_{ij}[q_j]) \le \varepsilon$. Let $T$ send a Gaussian $\bar{q} = \mathcal{N}(\bar{\mu}, \bar{\Sigma})$ with $\lambda_{\min} I \preceq \bar{\Sigma} \preceq \lambda_{\max} I$ to the pair of Eqs.~\eqref{eq:meta_agent_mu_impl}--\eqref{eq:meta_agent_sigma_impl} with weights $w_i(\bar{q}) = \chi_i \exp[-D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\Omega_{Ii}[q_i] \| \bar{q})]$. In the coordinates $(\mu, \Sigma)$ with the Euclidean and Frobenius norms, $T$ maps this bounded-spectrum set into the convex hull $H$ of the moments $\{(\tilde{\mu}_i, \tilde{\Sigma}_i)\}$, and on $H$ it is Lipschitz with constant $C(\lambda_{\min}, \lambda_{\max})\varepsilon$. For $\varepsilon < \varepsilon_0 = 1/C$ the fixed point is unique and the iteration converges geometrically from any start in the bounded-spectrum set. Every admissible constant satisfies $\varepsilon_0 \le 2$ when $\lambda_{\min} = \lambda_{\max} = 1$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
With normalized weights $\bar{w}_i$, $T$ is the convex combination $\sum_i \bar{w}_i \zeta_i$ of the moments $\zeta_i = (\tilde{\mu}_i, \tilde{\Sigma}_i)$ (not of the expectation parameters, which would reinstate the dispersion term), so it maps into $H$, and $H$ lies in the bounded-spectrum set. Writing $g_i$ for the gradient of $D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\Omega_{Ii}[q_i] \| \bar{q})$ in $(\bar{\mu}, \bar{\Sigma})$ and $\bar{g} = \sum_i \bar{w}_i g_i$, the derivative is $dT[h] = -\sum_i \bar{w}_i \langle g_i - \bar{g}, h\rangle (\zeta_i - \bar{\zeta})$, a weighted covariance between moment differences and gradient differences. By cocycle invariance, $D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\Omega_{Ii}[q_i] \| \Omega_{Ij}[q_j]) = D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| \Omega_{ij}[q_j]) \le \varepsilon$, so the Gaussian divergence bounds on the bounded-spectrum set give $\|\zeta_i - \zeta_j\| = \mathcal{O}(\sqrt{\varepsilon})$. The mean part of $g_i - g_j$ is $\bar{\Sigma}^{-1}(\tilde{\mu}_j - \tilde{\mu}_i)$, independent of $\bar{q}$, and the covariance part is $\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{\varepsilon})$ on the bounded set $H$; hence $\|dT\| = \mathcal{O}(\varepsilon)$ on the convex set $H$, and the Banach fixed-point theorem applies there. For the bound on $\varepsilon_0$, take two members $\mathcal{N}(\pm a, 1)$ in one dimension, with pairwise divergence $\varepsilon = 2a^2$. After one step $\bar{\Sigma} = 1$, and the mean map is $m \mapsto a\tanh(am)$, with slope $\varepsilon/2$ at $m = 0$; for $\varepsilon > 2$ it has three fixed points, so uniqueness fails.
\end{proof}

The defining property of the construction is gauge consistency: all constituent beliefs are first transported to the meta-agent's frame via $\Omega_{Ii}$ before averaging, so the scalar weights are gauge invariant while the averaged belief $\bar{q}_I$ transforms by pushforward with the meta-agent's frame. Without transport into a common frame the average would depend on the agents' passive choices of frame. By Proposition~\ref{prop:parent_frame_gauge} the parent frame cancels from every constituent law, so the licensing argument inherits no frame error when one convention for $U_I$ is used consistently.

The meta-agent's gauge frame and its independent model-frame analog are, in the working form, the Lie-algebra-additive averages
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
\phi_I^{(\ell+1)}(x)
&= \frac{\sum_{i \in I} w_i(x)\phi_i^{(\ell)}(x)}{\sum_{i \in I} w_i(x)}, \\
\tilde\phi_I^{(\ell+1)}(x)
&= \frac{\sum_{i \in I} w_i^{\mathrm{mod}}(x)\tilde\phi_i^{(\ell)}(x)}{\sum_{i \in I} w_i^{\mathrm{mod}}(x)}.
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
The first is the first fixed-point iterate, from the identity, of the exponential barycenter $\sum_i w_i\log(U_I^{-1}U_i) = 0$~\cite{PennecArsigny2013}: the iteration $U \mapsto U\exp[\sum_i w_i \log(U^{-1}U_i)]$ started at $U = I$ returns $\exp(\sum_i w_i \phi_i)$ when $U_i = \exp\phi_i$. It coincides with the exponential barycenter for commuting $\phi_i$, and in particular for abelian $G$~\cite{Hall2015}. It is not gauge-equivariant: its defect under $U_i \mapsto gU_i$ is controlled by the magnitude of the gauge transformation and by the spread of the $\phi_i$, not by $\|\phi_i\|$. The model-fiber weight $w^{\mathrm{mod}}_i$ is built from model-fiber transported divergences and solved by an independent fixed point on the model fiber, so the cross-scale model transport $\tilde\Omega_{iI}$ is well-defined at meta levels; in the tied-frame configuration $\tilde\phi_I$ is the $\rho_{\mathrm{model}}$-image of $\phi_I$ and no separate average is required. Reinstating the dispersion term in $\bar{\Sigma}_I$ recovers the strict moment-matched mixture covariance at modest computational cost.

\subsection{The Consensus Foil: Pointwise Consensus}
\label{sec:epistemic_collapse}

One limiting case brackets the construction and clarifies what it is not. In pointwise consensus, transported beliefs and models coincide, $\Omega_{ij}[q_j](c) = q_i(c)$ and $\tilde{\Omega}_{ij}[s_j](c) = s_i(c)$ for all $c$ in the shared support. Every inter-agent coupling term then attains its minimum value zero on both channels, and the couplings exert no force on beliefs or models, so learning is driven only by observations. Pointwise consensus is not the criterion for meta-agent formation, and applied across all agents it would be a static state in which the agents' distinct perspectives no longer register in the coupling terms. Formation requires bounded, not vanishing, internal disagreement modulo transport, with closure relative to the outside. Pointwise consensus nonetheless has a constructive role at the boundary: constituents that have collapsed to common values up to transport can be replaced by a single representative without information loss. The construction requires only that the system as a whole not reach pointwise consensus, from which it is kept away by continual observation and by hierarchical feedback that perturbs constituents heterogeneously.

\section{Coarse-Graining II: Cross-Scale Shadows as Lagged Predictive Priors}\label{sec:cross_scale_shadows}

Coarse-graining I built a single meta-agent from a coherent cluster, the upward leg of the construction. This section supplies the downward leg: how the meta-agent, once formed, sets the priors of its constituents. In the construction the prior $p_i^{(\ell)}$ and the hyper-prior $r_i^{(\ell)}$ at level $\ell$ are not independent dynamical fields. They are the transported belief and the transported model of the meta-agent $I$ at level $\ell+1$ that contains agent $i$ as a constituent,
\begin{equation}
p_i^{(\ell)}(c) = \Omega_{iI}\big[q_I^{(\ell+1)}\big](c),
\qquad
r_i^{(\ell)}(c) = \tilde\Omega_{iI}\big[s_I^{(\ell+1)}\big](c),
\label{eq:cross_scale_shadow}
\end{equation}
where $\Omega_{iI}$ is the gauge-covariant transport into agent $i$'s frame on the latent-state fiber and $\tilde\Omega_{iI}$ is its model-fiber counterpart. The state transport is $\Omega_{iI} = U_i U_I^{-1}$ with $U = \exp(\phi)$ (Section~\ref{sec:transport}); the model transport is the $\rho_{\mathrm{model}}$-image of $U_i U_I^{-1}$ when frames are shared, or an independent transport $\tilde\Omega_{iI} = \tilde U_i \tilde U_I^{-1}$ with $\tilde U = \exp(\tilde\phi)$ when model frames are independent. Under this assignment beliefs and priors share the latent-state manifold $\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{state}}$, models and hyper-priors share $\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{model}}$, and the four-field hierarchy $r \to s \to p \to q$ posited at the agent definition reduces to two primitive fields $(q, s)$ at each level connected by cross-scale transport: $r$ regularizes $s$ because $r$ is the meta-agent's model transported down, and $p$ regularizes $q$ because $p$ is the meta-agent's belief transported down.

Read as an assignment at one instant, Eq.~\eqref{eq:cross_scale_shadow} sets a prior equal to a transported posterior of the same instant. That is not a factor of one normalized joint: a generative kernel that takes a recognition law as its argument is a map from recognition laws to kernels, and the free energy built from it is not an evidence bound~\cite[Chs.~4 and 12]{DennisGaugeVFE2026}. Appendix~\ref{app:augmented_joint} makes the failure concrete. There a joint with Gibbs cross-scale factors is a well-defined density on the product space of all agent-level latents (Lemma~\ref{lem:aug_joint_welldefined}), and Lemma~\ref{lem:shadow_mf_optimum} computes the rigid-link variational predictive density of a child under that joint, which retains the transported parent covariance and is not a message of the joint (Remark~\ref{rem:shadow_marginal_vs_mf}). Evaluated against the full parent posterior, the shadow reuses each constituent's own evidence: in the rigid-link limit the child precision obtained from shadow times likelihood exceeds the exact child posterior precision by exactly the precision of the child's own observation, which has entered the parent posterior and then enters the child a second time. For the same reason the same-time shadow functional is not bounded below by the surprisal of the augmented joint.

Read across time, Eq.~\eqref{eq:cross_scale_shadow} is exact. Index episodes by $n = 0, 1, \ldots$, and let the tower of episode $n+1$ be generated from the parent latent $z_I$ of episode $n$ by a declared transition kernel, with a declared prior on the tower of episode $0$. This episode-indexed joint is normalized and acyclic, every kernel reads only structural data and latents of earlier episodes, and the prior of the tower at episode $n+1$ given the records $o_{0:n}$ is the filtered parent law pushed through the transition~\cite[Ch.~12, the delayed-feedback proposition]{DennisGaugeVFE2026}. Choose the transported-copy transition, which places child $i$'s latent at episode $n+1$ at $\Omega_{iI} z_I$. The predictive prior of child $i$ is then
\begin{equation*}
p_i^{(\ell), n+1} = (\Omega_{iI})_{\#} \mathbb{P}\big(z_I^{(n)} \in \cdot \mid o_{0:n}\big),
\end{equation*}
which is $\Omega_{iI}[q_I^{(\ell+1)}]$ whenever $q_I^{(\ell+1)}$ is the filtered parent law of episode $n$, and is the corresponding variational predictive density when $q_I^{(\ell+1)}$ approximates it (status D; the display is the pushforward of the filtered law under a deterministic kernel). A noisy transition $\mathcal{N}(\Omega_{iI} z_I, \Omega_{iI} \Sigma_\epsilon \Omega_{iI}^\top)$ adds $\Omega_{iI}\Sigma_\epsilon\Omega_{iI}^\top$ to the transported parent covariance. No evidence is counted twice, because child $i$'s record of episode $n+1$ has not entered the filter that produced the parent law of episode $n$. Eq.~\eqref{eq:cross_scale_shadow} is therefore the one-episode-lagged predictive prior of the episode-indexed joint, and this lagged construction of the companion theory supersedes the same-time shadow. The same-time form used in the energy of the following sections is an energy-level simplification of the lagged scheme (status S), not a limit of it.

Two further readings are consistent with an evidence bound within one episode. The standard hierarchical scheme~\cite{friston2008hierarchical, parr2022active} derives the level-$\ell$ prior from a declared conditional $p(z_\ell \mid z_{\ell+1})$ of the generative model; its variational term is the conditional coupling $\mathbb{E}_{q_I} D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| \chi(\cdot \mid k_I))$ of a downward kernel $\chi$~\cite[Ch.~12]{DennisGaugeVFE2026}, and the corresponding downward message is the cavity predictive $\int \chi(\cdot \mid k_I) q_I^{\setminus i}(dk_I)$, which uses the parent posterior with constituent $i$'s contribution removed. The second is a random-effects model: the constituents are independent draws $k_i \sim \mathcal{N}(\Omega_{iI} m_I, \Omega_{iI} S_I \Omega_{iI}^\top)$ with a point hyperparameter $\theta_I = (m_I, S_I)$, so that $q_I$ is a point estimate, $S_I$ is the population covariance, and minimizing the free energy over $(q, \theta_I)$ is type-II maximum likelihood, whose exact M-step is the strict barycenter with its dispersion term (Section~\ref{sec:meta_agent_variational}). Appendix~\ref{app:augmented_joint}'s single parent latent with noisy children is a different model, in which $\Sigma_I$ is posterior uncertainty and not population spread; its rigid-link lemma illustrates the predictive density and does not supply the shadow as a factor.

The single rung extends to a tower. Meta-agents at level $\ell+1$ can themselves cohere into meta-meta-agents at level $\ell+2$, so that individuals form groups, groups form communities, and communities form societies up to a maximum level $\ell_{\max}$, with information flowing bottom-up through consensus formation and top-down through predictive priors. This bidirectional coupling has the feedback structure of Wheeler's participatory loop (W4 in Table~\ref{tab:wheeler_correspondence}; status S). That structure alone does not distinguish the construction from hierarchical modeling with learned priors~\cite[Ch.~14]{DennisGaugeVFE2026}; formation of collectives by the agents' own inference would, and Section~\ref{sec:participatory} states the test of that reading. The hierarchy has two boundaries. At the top level there is no enveloping meta-agent, and the hyper-prior $r_i^{(\ell_{\max})}$ is a fixed declared prior, the root of the hierarchy (Section~\ref{sec:ouroboros}); at the bottom level $\ell = 0$ there are no constituents, so cross-scale propagation acts only downward from above. A prospective single-level comparison may treat $p_i, r_i$ as primitive boundary conditions, while a multi-level run must test the cross-scale relation of Eq.~\eqref{eq:cross_scale_shadow}.

\section{Coarse-Graining III: Exact Coarse-Graining and Closure}\label{sec:rg_status}

The coarse-graining of the preceding sections is an exact block-variable pushforward of a posited configuration ensemble followed by a truncation to the five-term family, a block transformation in Kadanoff's sense~\cite{kadanoff1966scaling} without a rescaling step. It is motivated by, but is not, the renormalization group of statistical physics~\cite{Wilson1971, Wilson1974, Cardy1996}. No rescaling, coupling-space flow, or fixed point is constructed, and on a finite network none can be: a step that reduces the number of agents admits no comparison identification of the interaction spaces before and after it, and with blocking ratio at least $b > 1$ the tower has at most $\log_b N$ levels~\cite[Ch.~11, the finite-network obstruction proposition]{DennisGaugeVFE2026}. The partitions are chosen by a state-dependent detector, so the map is not a homogeneous flow, and the attention weights $\beta_{ij}$ are state variables (the softmax of the current edge energies), not coupling constants; the couplings proper, the temperature $\tau$, the weight $\lambda_h$, and the declared precisions, are held fixed across levels by assumption, and the exact pushforward would in general change them; the one exception computed here is the parent's entropy weight, which shifts at one loop (below).

The status of the construction rests on three claims. First, the coarse-graining map admits an exact pushforward, and it is gauge covariant (Theorem~\ref{thm:rg_covariance}) for any Lie group with a left-invariant Riemannian metric when the frames of each cluster lie inside the convexity radius; compactness enters only through the normalizability of the Haar reference of the Gibbs ensemble, which fails for noncompact $\mathrm{GL}^+(K_q)$. For an exact channel the fine free energy equals the coarse free energy plus the discarded conditional information, with the same evidence at both levels~\cite[Ch.~9, the exact coarse free-energy theorem]{DennisGaugeVFE2026}, and the coarse action is the conditional-partition formula of~\cite[Ch.~11]{DennisGaugeVFE2026}. Second, closure of the level-$(\ell+1)$ free energy in the same five-term family is conditional and posited, not derived. Third, the consensus detector is used as a candidate filter in place of the variational formation criterion; it is not derived from that criterion (Section~\ref{sec:meta_agent_threshold}). One coupling does change under the step. At one loop in the mean sector, at coherence, the fluctuation term $\tfrac{T_{\mathrm{cfg}}}{2}\log\det' H_I^{\perp}$ of the internal modes equals, up to a $Y$-independent constant, $-\tfrac{T_{\mathrm{cfg}}}{2}(|I|-1)\log\det\Sigma_I$, so integrating out the internal modes shifts the coefficient of $\log\det\Sigma_I$, the entropy weight of the parent, in proportion to the cluster size (Eq.~\eqref{eq:rg_entropy_shift}); this induced renormalization vanishes at $T_{\mathrm{cfg}} = 0$, where the step reduces to constrained minimization.

Several precedents organize coarse-graining in these terms. B\'eny and Osborne~\cite{beny2015information} formulate coarse-graining as a family of channels and pull the Fisher metric back onto the space of models, so that the metric contraction measures the information destroyed per step; Machta et al.~\cite{Machta2013} trace effective theories to the compression of Fisher-information eigenvalues under coarse-graining; Berman et al.~\cite{BermanKlingerStapleton2023} read Bayesian updating as a flow on the Fisher manifold; and Mehta and Schwab~\cite{mehta2014exact} construct an exact mapping between Kadanoff's variational renormalization group~\cite{Kadanoff1975, KadanoffHoughtonYalabik1976} and restricted Boltzmann machines. Exact form preservation of a coarse-graining step in the strict sense is the classic solvable-model fact (for example one-dimensional Ising decimation), not a claim we attribute to any of these references. The closure statement here is a parametric form preservation: the level-$(\ell+1)$ functional has the same five-term shape as the level-$\ell$ functional, with coarse belief couplings $\beta^{(\ell+1)} = f_\beta(\{\beta_{ij}^{(\ell)}\})$ pooled by the raw-conductance rule of Eq.~\eqref{eq:rg_inter_cluster}, in which the pooled intra-cluster conductance acts as a parent self-loop, and model couplings $\gamma^{(\ell+1)} = f_\gamma(\{\gamma_{ij}^{(\ell)}\})$ left unspecified; the working forms, the conductance pooling, and the Schur elimination behind them are in Appendix~\ref{sec:meta_agent_rg}. Lemma~\ref{thm:rg_exact_closure} (mean-sector Gaussian elimination) concerns only the linear coarse-graining of the means at fixed covariances, frames, and couplings, where the exact pushforward of the mean-sector Gibbs density is Gaussian with the Schur-complement precision. A map that retains the parent covariance has bounded fibers and lies outside that statement; a level-dependent log-determinant generates non-quadratic terms at the next level, so the class is not closed under iteration, and exact closure generically requires hyperedges~\cite[Ch.~11, the hyperedge-generation proposition]{DennisGaugeVFE2026}. A merely local quadratic expansion retains the anharmonic remainder and is only a Laplace approximation.

The belief-mean sector shows which operation preserves the family. There a functional of the five-term family is a block-diagonal positive part plus a matrix-weighted Laplacian with positive definite edge weights, so each off-diagonal block satisfies $\operatorname{sign}\det\Lambda_{ij} = (-1)^{K_q}$ and the triple product around any triangle carries the same sign, a necessary condition involving neither frames nor transport. Integrating the within-cluster deviations out at fixed barycenter violates this condition on sparse fine graphs: on a chain of pairs it generates a coupling between non-adjacent clusters with the wrong sign, the standard way a decimation leaves a nearest-neighbor ferromagnetic ansatz. The condition is only necessary, and it is weak at even $K_q$, so it certifies failure and not success. The general statement is in the companion supplement: unrestricted matrix-weighted Kron reduction leaves the Laplacian-plus-self-term family, and on a fixed flat graph closure for every weight assignment holds exactly when every hidden component has at most two retained terminals~\cite[Ch.~G7]{DennisGaugeVFESuppG2026}; the scalar-weight theory of Kron reduction and graph coarsening is in~\cite{DorflerBullo2013Kron, Loukas2019, villegas2023laplacian}. Identifying constituents preserves the family exactly. The congruence $S^\top\Lambda S$ is the Galerkin coarse form of aggregation-based algebraic multigrid; the within-cluster Laplacian contributions cancel identically, the coarse coupling between two clusters is the sum of the fine weights on the edges cut between them, which supplies $\beta^{(\ell+1)}$ directly without the conductance ansatz, and successive aggregations compose~\cite[Ch.~G9]{DennisGaugeVFESuppG2026}. Tying has two distinct probabilistic readings. As a coarse model it is conditioning on zero within-cluster disagreement, with natural parameters $(S^\top \mathsf{h}, S^\top\Lambda S)$~\cite[Ch.~G7]{DennisGaugeVFESuppG2026}; this is precomposition, which defines a new model to which data processing and evidence preservation do not transfer~\cite[Ch.~9]{DennisGaugeVFE2026}. As a restriction of the recognition family on the means it keeps the fine joint and costs bound. The coarse couplings $\beta^{(\ell+1)}$ above are those of the first reading. Exact marginalization, a Markov pushforward, moves neither the evidence nor the bound. The rigorous literature on position-space coarse-graining maps shows that block-spin and decimation images of Gibbs measures can fail to be Gibbsian~\cite{VanEnterFernandezSokal1993}; its comparison between schemes does not settle the direction the framework's intuition assumes, so no analogy in either direction is asserted, and the lattice treatment of spatial coarse-graining is deferred to~\cite[Chs.~B7--B9]{DennisGaugeVFESuppB2026}.

The construction quantifies cross-scale information propagation through the transported relative entropy between constituent and meta-agent beliefs,
\begin{equation}
\mathcal{I}_{\ell \to \ell+1} = \sum_{I \in \mathrm{level}(\ell+1)} \sum_{i \in I} D_{\mathrm{KL}}\big(q_i^{(\ell)} \big\| \Omega_{iI}\big[q_I^{(\ell+1)}\big]\big),
\label{eq:cross_scale_information_flow}
\end{equation}
a divergence between two distributions on the same fiber after transport of the meta-agent belief into the constituent's frame, which vanishes when they agree after transport. We do not write this quantity as a mutual information, since a mutual information requires a joint distribution on $(k_i, k_I)$ and not a pair of marginals. In the lagged reading of Section~\ref{sec:cross_scale_shadows} each summand is the divergence of a constituent's belief from its predictive prior. In a functioning participatory system we expect $\mathcal{I}_{\ell \to \ell+1}$ to be small but nonzero for all $\ell < \ell_{\max}$: zero indicates constituents and meta-agent in exact agreement after transport, pointwise consensus (Section~\ref{sec:epistemic_collapse}), while large values indicate a meta-agent that has decoupled from its constituents. The quantity is distinct from the change in the alignment portion of $\mathcal{F}$ when the meta-agent is inserted: that change is signed, whereas $\mathcal{I}_{\ell \to \ell+1}$ is the nonnegative sum of the added child-parent divergences alone.

The complementary top-down channel can be quantified in the same way. Writing the active parent-child relations as a dynamically evolving graph $\mathcal{G} = \{(i, I) : i \in I\}$ whose edges are the channels along which a meta-agent sets a constituent's prior, the per-agent diagnostic
\begin{equation}
\Delta p_i(n) = \int_{\mathcal{C}} D_{\mathrm{KL}}\big(p_i^{(\ell)}(c; n) \big\| p_i^{(\ell)}(c; n-1)\big) \mu_{\mathcal{C}}(dc),
\label{eq:topdown_influence}
\end{equation}
with $\mu_{\mathcal{C}}$ the declared background density on $\mathcal{C}$ and $\Delta p_i > \theta_{\min}$ flagging active top-down feedback, measures how far the evolved parent has moved the constituent's predictive prior between successive episodes.

The construction also makes structural predictions about meta-agents at level $\ell+1$ relative to level $\ell$. In the mean-sector uniform-weight case the meta-agent law is a fixed Markov image of its constituents' laws, and wherever the meta-agent's frame introduces no horizontal defect, the meta-agent's pulled-back information geometry is dominated by its constituents', with equality exactly along directions whose fine score the meta-agent retains~\cite[Ch.~B2, the coarse or meta-agent perceived-geometry corollary]{DennisGaugeVFESuppB2026}. A horizontal defect can reverse the inequality~\cite[Ch.~B2, the exact signed positivity criterion]{DennisGaugeVFESuppB2026}, so the prediction that meta-agent beliefs vary more smoothly over $\mathcal{C}$ than constituent beliefs, with short-wavelength structure averaged out at higher levels, is conditional on frame compatibility, whose exact form is an isotropy condition; this comparison involves base directions and belongs to the base-transport work deferred to Supplement~B. Meta-agent covariances follow the convex-combination pooling of Eq.~\eqref{eq:meta_agent_sigma_impl}, so the construction predicts a parent covariance comparable to the weighted average of the transported constituent covariances, with the strict barycenter adding a positive-semidefinite dispersion term that can only enlarge it. A more confident parent would follow only from the precision-weighted product-of-experts pooling that the meta-agent state construction declines to adopt (Section~\ref{sec:meta_agent_state_construction}). Each prediction requires a multi-seed measurement before any emergence claim can be made (Section~\ref{sec:participatory}).

\section{Coarse-Graining IV: The Ouroboros Tower and Its Declared Root}\label{sec:ouroboros}

Assembling the rungs of the preceding sections yields the Ouroboros tower, the multi-level structure in which each level is a full free-energy functional and adjacent levels are coupled by the cross-scale shadows of Eq.~\eqref{eq:cross_scale_shadow}, read as lagged predictive priors. Wheeler's participatory universe envisions observer-participants whose registrations give tangible reality to what is observed~\cite{Wheeler1983, Wheeler1990}. The tower gives a formal counterpart of one strand of that picture (W4): when coherent clusters are licensed (Section~\ref{sec:meta_agent_variational}; status C), they form meta-agents that set the priors of their constituents. Agents are organized by level $\ell \in \mathbb{N}$: level-0 base agents cohere into level-1 meta-agents, which cluster into level-2 meta-meta-agents, and so on. Each level-$\ell$ agent is a full section $\sigma_I^{(\ell)}$ carrying a primitive belief $q_I^{(\ell)}$, a primitive generative model $s_I^{(\ell)}$, and a frame $\phi_I^{(\ell)}$, with its prior and hyper-prior given by the cross-scale shadows of the level-$(\ell+1)$ meta-agent through Eq.~\eqref{eq:cross_scale_shadow}. The tower is capped at a finite $\ell_{\max}$, and on a finite network a cap is forced in any case (Section~\ref{sec:rg_status}). The reading on which the resulting meta-agents are agents on a par with their constituents, not summaries of them, is an interpretation (status S); the derivation of Section~\ref{sec:meta_agent_variational} supplies instead the reading of the parent as a fitted shared prior.

Constituents receive their shadow from the immediate parent alone. A normalized hierarchy is depth-Markov: each level's factor reads the complete next-level state and no deeper variable, and for an adaptive tower, generated bottom-up by the coarse-graining maps, this holds automatically by Bayes reversal~\cite[Ch.~12]{DennisGaugeVFE2026}. A direct grandparent term is then not a factor of the tower law, and multiplying it in counts information already transmitted through the parent a second time; in a separately declared top-down hierarchy it would require its own normalized kernels. We therefore keep only the depth-one model coupling,
\begin{equation}
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{ouro}}\big[s_i; s_I^{(\ell+1)}\big] = \lambda_h D_{\mathrm{KL}}\big(s_i \big\| \tilde\Omega_{iI}\big[s_I^{(\ell+1)}\big]\big),
\label{eq:ouroboros_F}
\end{equation}
which is the model hyper-prior term of Eq.~\eqref{eq:free_energy_functional_final} with $r_i = \tilde\Omega_{iI}[s_I^{(\ell+1)}]$, the depth-one model shadow; deeper ancestors act on $s_i$ only through the chain of depth-one couplings. The cross-scale belief coupling is carried by the self-term $D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i \| p_i)$ of Eq.~\eqref{eq:free_energy_functional_final}, whose prior $p_i$ is the depth-one belief shadow $\Omega_{iI}[q_I^{(\ell+1)}]$, and is not re-added here.

A genuine tower predicts that meta-agents at level $\ell+1$ evolve more slowly than their constituents at level $\ell$. This cross-scale slowdown, $t^{(\ell)} < t^{(\ell+1)}$ at matched field type, is a conjecture (status C), not an imposed split. It rests on two mechanisms: meta-agents integrate over constituent beliefs, so high-frequency components of constituent drift are averaged out at the parent level, and changing a meta-agent state requires coherent change across several constituents through the gauge-covariant barycenter, which introduces consensus energy barriers that resist rapid updates. Parent stiffness supplies no third mechanism: with row-normalized $\beta$ the incoming relational stiffness is a convex combination of constituent precisions and does not grow with $|I|$ under the adopted covariance pooling. The within-level separation $t_{\mathrm{belief}}^{(\ell)} < t_{\mathrm{model}}^{(\ell)}$, by contrast, is imposed by the adiabatic hypothesis of Section~\ref{sec:timescales}, in which beliefs and attention rows form the fast block and models and prior parameters the slow block~\cite[Ch.~8]{DennisGaugeVFE2026}. The fully interleaved ordering $t_{\mathrm{model}}^{(\ell)} < t_{\mathrm{belief}}^{(\ell+1)}$ that a single chain would assert is supported by no mechanism here and is in tension with the prior-inheritance structure, under which level-$\ell$ priors inherit their timescales from the parent. The cross-scale slowdown is analogous to collective variables equilibrating more slowly than microscopic ones and to institutional change being slower than individual opinion change. It is not built into the construction, which uses one step size at every level; testing it requires a declared per-level timescale measurement.

The tower terminates at its top level. There is no parent meta-agent, and the top-level hyper-prior $r_i^{(\ell_{\max})}$ is a declared prior, the boundary condition of the hierarchy. For the belief channel an energy-level closure is available: top-level agent $i$ may take as its prior the coherence-weighted pool of the transported beliefs of all active agents at all levels. Mixtures of transported Gaussians leave the Gaussian family on which the closed-form divergences rely, so the pool is taken as the Gaussian moment projection of the mixture $\bar Q_i = \sum_j \bar w_j (\Omega_{ij})_{\#} q_j$, the minimizer of $D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\bar Q_i \| p)$ over Gaussians $p$,
\begin{equation}
\mu_i^{(\mathrm{top})} = \sum_j \bar w_j \Omega_{ij}\mu_j,
\qquad
\Sigma_i^{(\mathrm{top})} = \sum_j \bar w_j \Big[\Omega_{ij} \Sigma_j \Omega_{ij}^\top + \big(\Omega_{ij}\mu_j - \mu_i^{(\mathrm{top})}\big)\big(\Omega_{ij}\mu_j - \mu_i^{(\mathrm{top})}\big)^\top\Big],
\end{equation}
with normalized weights $\bar w_j = w_j / \sum_k w_k$ and $w_j \propto \exp(-\bar D_j)$, where $\bar D_j$ is agent $j$'s mean transported belief divergence from the collective, so that the weights favor agents coherent with the collective. The between-mean dispersion term is retained: dropping it, as the working form Eq.~\eqref{eq:meta_agent_sigma_impl} of the cluster barycenter does, would depart from the moment projection, and because the apex pool is not gated by coherence, the dispersion term is what records a globally incoherent collective as a broad prior. The model channel is not pooled.

The apex closes the tower at the level of the consensus energy and its update schedule, not as a generative model. Every finite acyclic generative model in which each record has a latent parent leaves at least one latent whose prior is declared and not constituted by the agents it organizes~\cite[Ch.~12, the declared-root proposition]{DennisGaugeVFE2026} (status P), and a reciprocal same-time structure is a probability model only as a globally normalized joint with its partition function~\cite[Ch.~4]{DennisGaugeVFE2026}. The pooled prior reads the current beliefs of the agents it organizes, so it is not a factor of one joint. The declared root is the formal residue of Wheeler's ``no tower of turtles''~\cite{Wheeler1990}, a reading of status S: the regress of priors stops at a declared prior and does not close on itself, and the companion theory accordingly replaces a self-closing apex by the declared root. The participatory content survives in three typed forms, each a sequence of fixed normalized joints~\cite[Ch.~12]{DennisGaugeVFE2026}: the inference loop, in which parent and child updates read each other under block coordinate ascent, as in any connected hierarchical model; slow learning of a declared top prior, whose optimum over an unrestricted family is the aggregate of the recognition laws it summarizes and, within the Gaussian family, their moment match including the dispersion retained above; and delayed feedback, in which the parent inferred at one episode sets its children's prior at the next (Section~\ref{sec:cross_scale_shadows}). The apex pool is the same-time, energy-level counterpart of the second: the companion theory aggregates the recognition laws of one top latent across episodes by a slow parameter update, whereas the apex pools across agents of all levels at one time. It has the feedback structure of Wheeler's self-excited circuit without being derived from it or evidence for it (status S). It is not that circuit, which runs through time, is constituted by registrations, and sustains itself~\cite{Wheeler1983}: the dynamics here relax to stationarity unless observations are injected, and the top-level model channel is a fixed boundary condition.

A participatory system must keep evolving. Stationarity of the specified dynamics means stationarity of $\mathcal{F}$, at which beliefs, priors, and frames cease evolving. Generic stationary points balance alignment against prior attachment, observation fit, and the attention entropy, and therefore retain persistent disagreement, so perfect consensus is one special stationary configuration and not the generic one; what makes a stationary state non-participatory is the cessation of evolution itself. The notion is non-stationarity of a deterministic descent. It is not the statistical-physics sense of distance from equilibrium, which refers to broken detailed balance or entropy production in a driven stochastic dynamics; the dynamics here is relaxational. Three indicators track it, each rendered dimensionless against a declared early-run reference window before any combination: an energy flux $\Phi_E(t) \propto |d\mathcal{F}/dt|$, an information flux $\Phi_I(t)$ tracking the magnitude of entropy change across belief and prior, and a gradient variance $V_{\nabla}(t) \propto \mathrm{Var}_{i}(\|\nabla_{q_i}\mathcal{F}\|^2)$ taken across agents at fixed $t$. They combine into the non-stationarity score $S_{\mathrm{ns}}(t) = (\Phi_E + \Phi_I + V_{\nabla})/3$, whose value depends on the reference window; values such as $0.5$ and $1$ are candidate thresholds to preregister and calibrate, not validated ones. Without observation drive the flow relaxes. The reduced functional is bounded below and real-analytic in the Gaussian parameters, so by the {\L}ojasiewicz gradient inequality~\cite{Lojasiewicz1963} every natural-gradient trajectory with precompact image converges to a stationary point and has finite total Fisher length~\cite[Ch.~7, the finite-duration proposition]{DennisGaugeVFE2026}; the corresponding statement for discrete descent iterates is~\cite{absil2005convergence}. Of the mechanisms that might sustain evolution, only observation injection supplies persistent exogenous drive. Heterogeneous top-down feedback and spatial separation shape how the system relaxes without preventing relaxation, and frame differences cannot maintain disagreement by themselves, since Regime~I frames are removable at a point. Whether observation injection sustains non-stationarity is open (status C).

Every per-level free-energy term is integrated over $\mathcal{C}$ against the same declared background density, the volume form of the background chart (Section~\ref{sec:flat_base}). That density is an input, not the geometry the agents induce. The induced geometry, the pulled-back metrics of Section~\ref{sec:pullback}, enters no free-energy term, so the participation loop of the tower closes only within information, from belief to meta-belief to predictive prior.

\section{Prospective Validation of Multi-Scale Formation}\label{sec:participatory}

The construction of Sections~\ref{sec:meta_agent_formation}--\ref{sec:ouroboros} defines how coherent clusters may form meta-agents and how their beliefs and models may propagate across levels. No simulation result is claimed (Section~\ref{sec:scope_limitations}); this section states the experiment that would test the multi-level claims. The formation rule under test is the threshold detector and barycenter of Section~\ref{sec:meta_agent_formation}; the companion theory replaces both by normalized marked parents~\cite[Ch.~13]{DennisGaugeVFE2026}, which is the rule a later protocol should test.

Formation alone cannot discriminate participation from seeding. On the homogeneous four-agent cycle of the companion theory, a collective forms from every Gaussian initial law with probability bounded below uniformly in the regime parameter, whether or not the source prior learns, because the random initial source assignment seeds the pair~\cite[Ch.~14, the seeded-contrast-formation proposition]{DennisGaugeVFE2026}. The source prior there is the attention prior $\pi_{ij}$ of this paper. Stable multi-child clusters, reproduction across seeds, and an active model channel would therefore all be met by a system whose attention prior never learns. The decidable form of the participatory reading is therefore the companion theory's pairing-selection conjecture (status C): slow learning of the attention prior selects which pair forms a collective, at the level of the law, against a frozen-prior control whose pairing law stays symmetric~\cite[Ch.~14, the pairing-selection conjecture]{DennisGaugeVFE2026}. The protocol below is its finite-sample counterpart in the present construction; neither bears on the participatory-physics readings of Part~II.

The protocol has three arms at one declared configuration, differing only as named. Arm A has a learning attention prior and an active, untied model channel with a declared nonzero $\gamma_{ij}$, so that the term $\gamma_{ij} D_{\mathrm{KL}}(s_i\|\tilde\Omega_{ij}[s_j])$ is exercised and not inferred from a frozen or tied channel. Arm B, the frozen-attention-prior control, is identical except that $\pi_{ij}$ does not learn. Arm C is identical to arm A except that the model channel is frozen ($\lambda_h = 0$ and $\gamma_{ij} = 0$). The arms share seeds: each seed fixes the initial means and the initial assignment draws for all three arms, so that comparisons between arms are paired.

The following quantities are declared here and may be revised only before execution, with any revision reported. The formation event at a recorded step is the existence of a connected block $I$ with $2 \le |I| \le N-1$ whose internal belief and model transported relative entropies are all at most $\delta_{\mathrm{in}} = 10^{-2}$ nats and whose boundary belief transported relative entropies all exceed $\delta_{\mathrm{out}} = 1/2$ nat, the form of the event in the companion proposition with its internal bound relaxed. The persistence window has flow-time length $W = 10/\eta$, ten relaxation times of an isolated belief mode under the natural-gradient flow of rate $\eta$, and ends at the declared horizon. The primary statistic for each seed and arm is the indicator $H$ that one block satisfies the formation event throughout the window, together with the identity of that block. Each arm runs $n = 20$ seeds. The formation claim passes in arm A if $H = 1$ in at least $16$ of the $20$ seeds. The participatory claim requires a difference between arms A and B: it passes only if an exact two-sided McNemar test on the paired indicators rejects at level $0.05$, or if an exact permutation test at level $0.05$ on the paired block identities rejects equality of the laws of the formed blocks. With twenty paired seeds no single aberrant seed decides the outcome, and the McNemar test needs at least six discordant pairs in one direction ($2 \cdot 2^{-6} = 0.031$). The model-channel claim passes if arms A and C differ by the same McNemar criterion.

The formation claim is refuted for the tested configuration if arm A fails its pass rule. The participatory claim is unsupported if arms A and B do not differ by the pass rule, whatever arm A shows. A result that appears only with the model channel frozen, only under thresholds chosen after inspection, or only in one seed validates nothing. Two conditions are preconditions for a valid measurement and not refutation criteria: population accounting must close at every recorded step, $N_{\mathrm{total}}(t) = \sum_\ell N_\ell(t)$ with $N_\ell(t)$ the number of active agents at level $\ell$, with every meta-agent's constituents and parent recorded; and the raw per-step states (beliefs, models, priors, hyper-priors, frames, transported pairwise divergences, $\beta_{ij}$, $\gamma_{ij}$, detector scores, assignments, energy components, and cross-scale links) must be retained so that every reported statistic can be recomputed. A failure of either invalidates the run as a measurement; it does not refute the tower.

Until this protocol is completed, the Ouroboros tower supplies a mathematical construction and a set of falsifiable predictions (status C), not evidence that the dynamics generate a persistent hierarchy or sustained non-stationarity.

\section{Discussion}\label{sec:discussion}

This section places the construction of Part~I relative to the dynamics of active inference and to existing participatory and structural positions in the foundations of physics. The dynamical statements of the first subsection are derived in Section~\ref{sec:mass} (status D); the positioning in the second and third subsections is interpretive (status S). Physical and phenomenal readings that rest on the postulates P1 to P10 are collected in Part~II.

\subsection{First-Order Dynamics: Stiffness, Friction, and the Kinetic-Metric Postulate}

The dynamics the construction defines is first order: the natural-gradient flow of Eq.~\eqref{eq:gauge_natural_gradient}, $\dot\xi = -G_\eta^{-1}\nabla\mathcal{F}$, where $G_\eta$ is the Fisher metric with each block divided by its rate ($\eta_\mu$ for the means, $\eta_\Sigma$ for the covariances). It has the form $\gamma\dot q = -\nabla V$ of the overdamped reduction of a mechanical particle $m\ddot q + \gamma\dot q = -\nabla V$, with the friction coefficient replaced by the tensor $G_\eta$ and no term in the inertial slot (Eq.~\eqref{eq:friction_form}). The comparison illustrates the regime only: the flow is a gradient flow on the statistical manifold and is not obtained as the overdamped limit of a Newtonian stochastic differential equation. First-order natural-gradient descent is also the regime of standard active inference, whose neuronal dynamics approximate natural-gradient descent on the variational free energy~\cite{DaCosta2021neural}, an intrinsic Riemannian gradient flow in the sense of Amari~\cite{Amari1998}; for cortical systems, where synaptic time constants dominate any putative inertial term, the overdamped regime is the appropriate one. Section~\ref{sec:mass} derives what precision does in this flow. The Hessian of $\mathcal{F}$ at consensus is a stiffness, so a high-precision agent has small compliance and a given force displaces it little. At an isolated equilibrium $q_i = p_i$ the stiffness equals the Fisher metric, so the linear relaxation rates are $\eta_\mu$ and $\eta_\Sigma$ whatever the precision. The linearized spectrum of the gradient flow is real, so there is no oscillation frequency to measure; complex eigenvalues appear only in the receiver-only truncation, whose field is not the gradient of $\mathcal{F}$. The only velocity-quadratic form of the dynamics is the Rayleigh dissipation of Eq.~\eqref{eq:rayleigh_dissipation}, and for a single rate its square root is the rate of the information clock (Eq.~\eqref{eq:clock_dissipation};~\cite[Ch.~7]{DennisGaugeVFE2026}).

The kinetic-metric postulate of Section~\ref{sec:velocity_quadratic} adds the inertia that the flow lacks, with its own second-order equation of motion, Eq.~\eqref{eq:second_order_postulate}, whose overdamped limit is the natural-gradient flow. It is adopted only under the reading of the variational principle as a candidate fundamental scaffolding, and the free-energy-principle canon does not imply it: Friston, Trujillo-Barreto and Daunizeau~\cite{Friston2008DEM} obtain second-order behavior through generalized coordinates of motion and a first-order variational filter, and Friston et al.~\cite{Friston2023PhysReports} decompose the flow into solenoidal and dissipative parts of a first-order stochastic vector field; neither writes a velocity-quadratic kinetic energy whose inertia tensor is the potential Hessian. Under the postulate as stated, with inertia $M_K$ equal to the stiffness $M$ at consensus, every undamped linear mode has unit frequency in flow-time units, so the scaling $\omega^2 \propto 1/\bar{M}_i$ is not a consequence of the postulate. A precision-dependent frequency requires an inertia tensor different from the stiffness tensor. Taking the bare prior precision $\bar{\Lambda}_p$ as inertia and the full Hessian $M = \bar{\Lambda}_p + K_{\mathrm{c}}$ as stiffness, with $K_{\mathrm{c}}$ the coupling contribution, gives $\omega^2 \in \operatorname{spec}(\bar{\Lambda}_p^{-1}M) = 1 + \operatorname{spec}(\bar{\Lambda}_p^{-1}K_{\mathrm{c}})$, which is a second and different postulate, testable and not an identity. On either postulate the Fisher information enters the dynamics the construction actually runs as a stiffness and a friction tensor, and belief configurations of large Fisher information are hard to displace, not hard to accelerate.

\subsection{Relation to Existing Participatory Approaches}

Of the strands of Wheeler's program in Table~\ref{tab:wheeler_correspondence}, the construction models W3 most directly: Wheeler's third question, how come ``one world'' out of many observer-participants, read with F{\o}llesdal's dictum that meaning is ``the joint product of all the evidence that is available to those who communicate''~\cite{Wheeler1990}. Agents compare laws only through the explicit transport $\Omega_{ij}$, the residual $D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_i\|\Omega_{ij}[q_j])$ is the frame-independent part of their disagreement, and intersubjective agreement is a stationary point of the consensus free-energy functional of Section~\ref{sec:free_energy}; reading this as Wheeler's strand has status S. The companion theory describes the multiscale participation loop in the same register, as resembling Wheeler's proposal without being derived from it or evidence for it, and notes that none of its three typed forms of participation separates the program from a hierarchical model with learned priors, while formation could~\cite[Ch.~14]{DennisGaugeVFE2026}.

Rovelli's relational quantum mechanics~\cite{Rovelli1996}, an informational theory in Wheeler's spirit that relativizes states and values to arbitrary physical systems (Section~\ref{sec:related_work}), is the closest relative of the construction, which relativizes laws to agents and makes the comparison map between perspectives explicit. The construction is compatible with the relational reading without establishing that physical laws are observer-dependent, and its observer-dependence is one of representation and belief, not of fact. With Regime~I frames the cocycle closes (Lemma~\ref{thm:vanishing_holonomy}), a global frame exists, and every transported law lives in one classical probability space, so the construction satisfies the classical assumptions that Brukner's no-go theorem for observer-independent facts~\cite{Brukner2018} exploits and does not model the situation it addresses. The precise point of contact with the cross-perspective-links debate within relational quantum mechanics~\cite{AdlamRovelli2022} arises with independent links on the interaction graph (Section~\ref{sec:discrete_regime_ii}). There a nontrivial loop holonomy obstructs a global frame, and a normalized record whose law separates loop-holonomy classes exists at one base point, by the companion theory's theorem on an operational record sensitive to network-loop holonomy~\cite[Ch.~10]{DennisGaugeVFE2026}. This is a belief-level counterpart of the question whether the perspectives of different relational observers can be linked.

QBism~\cite{Fuchs2014, Fuchs2017} reads quantum probabilities as an agent's personalist degrees of belief and, as participatory realism, claims Wheeler's program by name (Section~\ref{sec:related_work}). The construction shares the personalist starting point, since each agent carries its own $q_i$, and in this respect, together with the relational comparison through transport, it is of the relational and QBist type. It departs from QBism in two places. It has no quantum content, and it adds a transport law between agents and a consensus functional whose stationary points model intersubjective agreement, whereas QBism keeps measurement outcomes personal to each agent~\cite{DeBrotaFuchsSchack2020} and leaves agreement to communication between agents. Read classically, the consensus mechanism is precision-weighted opinion pooling of the kind DeGroot analyzed~\cite{degroot1974}; its closest quantum-foundations relative is quantum Darwinism, in which objectivity arises from records redundantly available to many observers~\cite{Zurek2009}.

M{\"u}ller's observer-first program~\cite{Mueller2020} formalizes Wheeler's law without law~\cite{Wheeler1983} directly. Observer states are primitive, no laws are presupposed, and an external world with simple probabilistic laws, together with agreement between observers, emerges as an asymptotic statistical phenomenon through algorithmic probability. That program is more faithful to Wheeler than the present construction on law without law, on the refusal of a continuum, and on the primacy of registration; the present construction is more explicit about multi-agent transport, gauge structure, and consensus dynamics. Law without law is not modeled here. The functional, the belief family, and the divergence are fixed in advance, so their stationarity conditions are laws in the ordinary sense, and the $\mathrm{GL}(K_q)$ invariance of the divergence is already present for a single agent, a consistency requirement that consensus must respect (Section~\ref{sec:gauge_invariance_consensus} and Remark~\ref{rem:kretschmann}). A law-without-law version would need the functional itself to be selected by the population dynamics, which is open (status C).

The mechanism of Page and Wootters~\cite{PageWootters1983} derives dynamics from correlations between a clock subsystem and the rest of a stationary global state, and so addresses the timelessness behind Wheeler's ``no time''. The construction does not do this. Its histories are ordered by an algorithmic flow parameter, and the information clock of Section~\ref{sec:time_info} supplies duration along that order, not the order itself. A classical relational analog is available in the construction's own terms: parametrize the other agents' histories by one agent's Fisher length, which is strictly increasing wherever that agent's free-energy gradient is nonzero and constant at its stationary points~\cite[Ch.~7]{DennisGaugeVFE2026}, so that the relational clock fails exactly where that agent stops updating. This is a structural parallel (status S), not a derivation of time.

\subsection{Kantian and Structural-Realist Positioning}

This subsection places the author's interpretive stance, cognitive-first structural monism (Section~\ref{sec:cognitive_first}), relative to Kant and to structural realism; none of it is derived. The construction recasts Kant's distinction between phenomena and noumena~\cite{Kant1781} in a structural-realist register closest to Cassirer's neo-Kantian variant~\cite{Cassirer1923}. Worrall's epistemic structural realism~\cite{Worrall1989}, which suspends judgment about the nature of the relata, is a related precursor. The ontic structural realism of Ladyman~\cite{Ladyman1998} and of Ladyman and Ross~\cite{Ladyman2007} is a neighbor whose naturalism the position departs from: the cognitive-first order of derivation reverses the Primacy of Physics Constraint, a methodological principle of Ladyman and Ross's naturalized metaphysics, not the definition of ontic structural realism. On this reading information geometry on the fibers is the structure available to agents. The base manifold $\mathcal{C}$ is a presupposed smooth index manifold carrying no physical metric, called noumenal by analogy only. Along an agent's belief section a Riemannian metric on $\mathcal{C}$ is pulled back from the Fisher metric; computed with the covariant derivative of the agent's own connection it is unchanged under a passive change of frame~\cite[Ch.~B2]{DennisGaugeVFESuppB2026}, so differences between agents' pulled-back metrics reflect differences in their beliefs. What measurement protocols label distance and duration then correspond to induced metric intervals and accumulated Fisher length, and what they label inertia corresponds at most to the precision that acts as a stiffness in Section~\ref{sec:mass}. The frame-invariant data of the construction, namely the relative-entropy and evidence-bound values, the conjugacy classes of link holonomies, and the per-edge twist classes, are compatible with epistemic, moderate, and ontic structural realism alike, and nothing in the mathematics selects among these positions or the neo-Kantian reading~\cite[Ch.~14]{DennisGaugeVFE2026}. The treatment of beliefs and frames as agent-internal constructs, not structural representations of an external reality, is likewise compatible with the enactive reading of active inference advanced by Ramstead, Kirchhoff and Friston~\cite{Ramstead2019}, although that reading rests on an action-perception coupling that the present construction does not model. On the Kantian side the position disavows the apodictic a priori, forms fixed and valid for all time, and keeps a revisable constitutive role for the requirement that shareable descriptions be invariant under transport (Section~\ref{sec:gauge_invariance_consensus}), in the sense of the relativized a priori that Friedman~\cite{Friedman2001} develops from Reichenbach's 1920 analysis of relativity. Whether the within-agent pullback can carry a Lorentzian geometry is taken up under the postulates of Part~II (Section~\ref{sec:signature_resolution}), where an indefinite form is exhibited and its selection remains open.

\section{Summary of Part I}\label{sec:conclusion}

Part~I has constructed the classical, multi-agent, information-geometric objects on which the Wheeler correspondence of Table~\ref{tab:wheeler_correspondence} rests. Agents are local sections of associated bundles over a presupposed smooth index manifold $\mathcal{C}$ carrying no physical metric; each carries a belief $q_i$ on the state fiber and a generative model $s_i$ on the model fiber, expressed in its own frame, and agents compare laws only through the transport operators $\Omega_{ij}$ and $\tilde\Omega_{ij}$. At one base point the active symmetry is one global copy of the structure group and per-agent frames are passive re-expressions. Regime~I frames are removable by the relative-entropy invariance of Theorem~\ref{thm:glk_invariance}, whereas independent links on the interaction graph, with orientation reversal imposed, carry loop holonomy that no choice of frames removes. The consensus functional couples agents through transported relative entropies; its attention rows are Gibbs optima with reduced value $-\tau\log Z$, and intersubjective agreement is a stationary point of the consensus free-energy functional, which is the formal content of W3. The dynamics is a first-order natural-gradient flow in which precision is a stiffness and the Fisher metric a friction tensor (Section~\ref{sec:mass}).

Participation across scales (W4) rests on three constructions. The first is hierarchical coarse-graining. A meta-agent is the gauge-covariant M-projection barycenter of its constituents, the moment-matching minimizer of the weighted sum of $D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\Omega_{Ii}q_i\|q_I)$, and it obeys the covariance theorem (Theorem~\ref{thm:rg_covariance}). The coarse-graining map is an exact block-variable pushforward followed by a truncation to the Gaussian family, with no rescaling, coupling-space flow, or fixed point, and with blocking ratio $b > 1$ it admits at most $\log_b N$ steps on a network of $N$ agents (Section~\ref{sec:rg_status}). The second is the cross-scale prior of Eq.~\eqref{eq:cross_scale_shadow}, read across episodes: when the transition of an episode-indexed joint copies the transported parent latent into the child, the predictive prior of the companion theory's delayed-feedback proposition is exactly this formula, with the parent law filtered from the previous episode in the role of $q_I$~\cite[Ch.~12]{DennisGaugeVFE2026}. The same-time form used in the energy is an energy-level simplification (status S), not a prior of one joint.
